## [2026-05-30] - v2.3.0 adoption-superpowers Phase 5: flaky-test tooling cluster (P2)

Implemented Phase 5 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-superpowers` plan: shipping the two flaky-test tooling items the comparison's Section 5a flagged as superpowers tooling that Nexus-Hub's `flaky-test-detector` describes but does not bundle -- a test-pollution bisector and a reusable `waitFor` condition-based-waiting helper. Subtasks T018-T020 closed. This is a `re-full` phase: both artifacts are local code reverse-engineered into a project-agnostic form, so the change adds zero new runtime dependencies, zero outbound calls, and zero new credentials. Both ship as per-skill bundled resources auto-copied by the installer's recursive `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy`, so no `scripts/installer.{sh,ps1}` copy-step edit is needed; no `data/` registry file was touched (no new skill).

T018 added `catalog/skills/tests-generation/flaky-test-detector/scripts/find-polluter.sh` and its parity sibling `find-polluter.ps1`: a project-agnostic bisector that runs each test file in isolation, removes a watched pollution artifact before each run, and reports the first file whose isolated run re-creates it. The bash interface is `--watch <artifact>` / `--tests <glob>` / `-- <test-cmd...>` with a `{}` per-file placeholder (appended if absent), and the post-`--` command is executed as an argv array (no `eval`, honoring the bash security rules); the PowerShell sibling is `-Watch` / `-Tests` / `-TestCommand "<cmd>"` (the command string is split on whitespace into exe + args, with `{}` substituted as a single token so a file path with spaces survives). Both carry an unsafe-path guard on the watched pattern (refusing `/`, `.`, `..`, `~`, `$HOME`, `$PWD`, `*`), tolerate a failing test during bisection (only pollution matters), print the polluter to stdout for scriptability with logs on stderr, and exit 0 on a successful scan / 2 on a usage-IO error.

T019 added `catalog/skills/tests-generation/flaky-test-detector/assets/condition-based-waiting-example.ts`: a copy-in `waitFor(probe, description, timeoutMs = 5000, intervalMs = 25)` polling helper (matching the signature the Phase-3 `references/condition-based-waiting.md` already shows) plus `waitForEvent` / `waitForCount` / `waitForState`. It has zero runtime dependencies (only `setTimeout`), explicit return types, and heavy WHY comments encoding the flakiness footguns it avoids -- always an outer deadline, a small non-zero poll interval, swallow probe errors during polling and surface them only in the timeout message, and assert AFTER `waitFor` returns rather than inside the probe. A bisector block was added to `flaky-test-detector/SKILL.md` Step 3 naming both `find-polluter` scripts with a bash and a PowerShell invocation example, so the orphan-bundle audit stays clean; the `.ts` basename was already referenced from Step 2 and the Phase-3 reference.

T020 stabilized. The `make validate` equivalent was run directly (make unavailable on the Windows host): orphan-bundle audit PASS 0/0 across 237 skills, JSON catalogs OK (skills.json 231, untouched), and the no-personal-paths / unicode-safety / supply-chain-iocs / workflow-security validators all exit 0 with the four new/edited files ASCII-clean. `bash -n` on the script is clean. `.sh`/`.ps1` parity was CONFIRMED by running both siblings live against a throwaway fixture with identical results across 5 cases each (polluter detection, no-polluter, append-mode, unsafe-watch guard, no-match guard). The smoke test caught one real bug, fixed in-phase: a literal (metacharacter-free) `--watch` value always matched under `nullglob` in the `.sh` because a non-pattern word is not subject to globbing, so `artifact_exists` / `remove_artifact` / the test-glob expansion now test real on-disk existence with `-e` (the `.ps1` was unaffected -- it uses existence-based `Test-Path`). No new open gap was recorded: ShellCheck is unavailable on the host so the script was verified via `bash -n` + the live cross-OS smoke, and per-skill-script ShellCheck coverage in CI is already tracked under the transferred QG-v23-1; the `.ts` is a by-design copy-in example needing no test. Next: Phase 6 (final) -- the visual-brainstorm-server deferral record, AGENTS/CHANGELOG counts, and the final validation sweep, which (being the final phase) also triggers the release-readiness workflow.

## [2026-05-30] - v2.3.0 adoption-superpowers Phase 4: eval-harness trigger techniques (P1)

Implemented Phase 4 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-superpowers` plan: adding the three trigger-testing techniques superpowers' harness has that Nexus-Hub's `skill-eval-loop` lacked -- premature-action detection, multi-turn conversation triggering, and cheap-model fragility testing (comparison Section 8). Subtasks T014-T017 closed. This is a `re-full` phase: all three techniques are local code that reuses the eval harness's existing CLI dispatcher, so the change adds zero new runtime dependencies, zero outbound calls, and zero new credentials -- the harness drives the user's already-configured model CLI exactly as before. No `data/` registry file was touched (no new skill).

T014 added premature-action detection to `scripts/optimize_skill_description.py`. `extract_tool_invocations()` walks a CLI stream-json transcript (the same stream the superpowers harness greps for `"name":"Skill"`) and returns the ordered tool-use names, tolerating both newline-delimited events and a single JSON array; `detect_premature_action()` applies one rule over that list -- the first `Skill` invocation clears the run, and any non-allowlisted tool (anything other than `Skill` / `TodoWrite`) seen before it sets the flag, including the case where the skill never loads but a real tool ran. The flag is surfaced per-eval in the benchmark output: `scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py` now passes `premature_action` through from the with_skill `grading.json` into `benchmark.json` (eval-level + the with_skill condition), defaulting False for the without_skill baseline that has no skill to load.

T015 added the multi-turn and cheap-model modes. For multi-turn, `evaluate_multi_turn()` replays an eval's ordered `turns` list through the dispatcher and records per-turn triggering, and the pure helpers `first_trigger_turn()` / `multi_turn_passes()` assert the skill FIRST triggers exactly at the designated `trigger_turn` (triggering early or never both fail) -- catching skills that fire on a cold prompt but not deep in a brainstorm-then-plan-then-execute flow. For cheap-model fragility, `invoke_cli()` was split into a pure `build_cli_command()` (so the new `--model` flag threading and the no-cross-CLI-bleed parity invariant are both testable without spawning a subprocess) and a `--model <name>` flag was threaded through `estimate_trigger_rate` / `run_iteration` / `_passes` / `render_dry_run`; a per-eval `model` field overrides the global flag. The `evals.json` schema (documented in `skill-eval-loop/references/schemas.md`) gained the optional `turns` / `trigger_turn` / `model` fields, and the grading/benchmark schema gained `premature_action`; both modes are opt-in so existing single-turn, default-model eval sets are unaffected.

T016 created `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/references/trigger-testing.md` documenting all three techniques (what each catches, how to author an eval that exercises it, how to read the new output fields, when to use each), cross-referencing the comparison Section 8 for provenance, and linked it from `skill-eval-loop/SKILL.md` (a new "Trigger-testing techniques" body section plus a Reference-files entry) so the orphan-bundle audit stays clean. `agents/grader.md` was updated to record `premature_action` from the optional `outputs/stream.jsonl` transcript using the documented rule.

T017 added 23 pytest cases to `catalog/hooks/tests/test_eval_loop.py` (suite 14 -> 37 passed): premature-action detection over fixture stream-json including tool-ordering and the single-array fallback; the multi-turn pure-logic (`is_multi_turn` / `first_trigger_turn` / `multi_turn_passes`); `build_cli_command` argv assertions confirming `--model` threads into all four CLI branches and is omitted by default; a dry-run model-surfacing test; and a benchmark `premature_action` pass-through test. The existing CLI-adapter parity test stays green after the `build_cli_command` extraction, and a dry-run smoke (exit 0, train/test split + `test_trigger_rate` selection metric unchanged) confirms existing optimizer behavior is preserved. `make` is unavailable on this Windows host so pytest + validators were invoked directly: orphan-bundle audit PASS 0/0 across 237 skills, unicode-safety `--strict` 0 findings on the six changed files, no-personal-paths 0. CI already runs `pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` line 94) so the new cases are gated with no CI edit. The 73 failures elsewhere in `catalog/hooks/tests/` are pre-existing bash-hook-on-Windows environmental failures in five unrelated test files (same cross-OS class as DF-v23-6, green on Linux CI), not regressions from this phase. One new low-priority gap was recorded (DF-v23-8: a live end-to-end run of the three new modes is deferred to the Phase 6 final validation, alongside the DF-v23-7 live `skill-eval-loop` run; only dry-run + pure-logic + fixture-stream tests were done this phase). Next: Phase 5 (flaky-test tooling cluster -- the `find-polluter` bisector and the `waitFor` helper).

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-superpowers Phase 3: discipline framing + operational enhancements (P2)

Implemented Phase 3 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-superpowers` plan: hardening four existing skills with superpowers' discipline framing and operational templates, adding no new top-level skills and touching no `data/` registry file. Subtasks T009-T013 closed. All content was adapted from `docs/v2.3.0/comparison-superpowers.md` Section 5a into Nexus-Hub's professional teaching tone (not the source "human partner" voice -- comparison N2); every item is pure markdown with zero new dependencies and zero outbound calls.

T009 enhanced `catalog/skills/bug-fixing/regression-root-cause-analyzer/SKILL.md` with three discipline frames: an "Iron Law: No Fixes Without Root Cause Investigation First" gate (name the regression, locate the introducing change, explain the mechanism, only then fix), the "after 3+ failed fixes, question the architecture, not the bug" circuit-breaker with its failure-pattern signals, and the multi-component-boundary evidence-gathering pattern (instrument every boundary, run once to find WHERE it breaks, then investigate that one component). A seven-row Common Rationalizations table was added (the skill previously had only a Common Pitfalls list). Because the file was a grandfathered 952 lines and T013's stability gate requires enhanced skills under the 800-line soft cap, the JavaScript and Java analyzer implementations were moved to a new `references/multi-language-examples.md` (Python stays inline as the primary examples), each removal replaced by a section-anchored pointer; the body landed at 725 lines.

T010 added a "Step 4.5: Subagent-Driven Development" subsection to `catalog/skills/orchestration/multi-agent-coordinator/SKILL.md` documenting the two-stage review ordering (spec compliance FIRST, then code quality -- never reversed, with a spec-FAIL short-circuit), the 4-status implementer protocol (DONE / DONE_WITH_CONCERNS / NEEDS_CONTEXT / BLOCKED, each with the coordinator's routing action in a table), and per-role model tiering. Three bundled prompt templates were shipped under `assets/`: `implementer-prompt.md` (one task, explicit write scope, required verification, four-status return), `spec-reviewer-prompt.md` (stage-one: does the change meet the acceptance criteria, verified against real behavior not a mock), and `code-quality-reviewer-prompt.md` (stage-two: maintainability / edge cases / YAGNI, run only on a spec-PASS change). All three are linked from the new subsection so the orphan-bundle audit passes; 672 lines.

T011 created `catalog/skills/tests-generation/flaky-test-detector/references/condition-based-waiting.md` (the wait-for-the-condition-not-the-clock principle, a before/after contrast distinguishing a guessed-duration `sleep` from a generous failure deadline, a quick-patterns table mapping each wait target to a predicate, the narrow cases where a fixed timeout IS correct -- timing-behavior tests via fake clocks, poll-loop intervals, documented external constraints -- and common mistakes) and linked it from the SKILL.md Step 2 (Diagnose Timing Dependencies). The reference forward-references the reusable `waitFor` helper that Phase 5 (T019) will bundle under `assets/`; 760 lines.

T012 added a "Hard Gate: No Implementation Before an Approved Design" section to `catalog/skills/developer-experience/spec-driven-development/SKILL.md` (no implementation skill, code, scaffold, or installer run until a design is presented in reviewable sections AND explicitly approved -- regardless of perceived simplicity), plus the one-question-at-a-time clarification convention and two new Common Rationalizations rows ("too simple to need a design", "the user said just build it"). A reciprocal cross-link was added to `idea-refine/SKILL.md` clarifying that approving the problem statement is a separate approval from approving the design; 284 lines.

T013 stabilized: the `make validate` validators were run individually (make is unavailable on this Windows host) -- skills.json loads (231, untouched), the per-skill orphan-bundle audit PASS (0 errors / 0 warnings, all five new bundled files referenced), no-personal-paths / unicode-safety / supply-chain-IOC / workflow-security all exit 0. All authored content is ASCII-clean; the only unicode-safety warnings on the touched files are pre-existing em-dashes already covered by the WN-v23-3 umbrella, left in place as out-of-scope. `make lint` is a no-op (no shell scripts added; find-polluter is Phase 5). No new open gaps were recorded in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md` (the regression refactor-to-references was an intentional plan-directed move). Zero new runtime dependencies, zero new standalone scripts (per-skill `references/` and `assets/` auto-distribute via the installer with no copy-step edit), zero outbound calls. Next: Phase 4 (eval-harness trigger techniques).

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-superpowers Phase 1: new discipline skills (P0/P1)

Implemented Phase 1 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-superpowers` plan (a sibling of the now-complete `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan; the two share the v2.3.0 version directory). The plan operationalizes the in-scope items from `docs/v2.3.0/comparison-superpowers.md` -- closing Nexus-Hub's process-discipline gaps with zero new dependencies and zero outbound calls. Phase 1 adds the three confirmed-missing discipline-gate skills as pure catalog content and registers them. Subtasks T001-T005 closed. Per the plan Overview, this work ingests zero items from prior known-gaps and does not modify the sibling plan or its tracked items.

T001 added `catalog/skills/workflow/verification-before-completion/SKILL.md`: an always-on "evidence before claims" discipline skill that fires before any completion or success claim. Its core is a six-step gate function (name the claim, identify the single proving command, run it fresh this turn, read the full output + exit code, confirm the output supports the claim, then claim while quoting the evidence), backed by a claim-to-evidence table (tests pass / linter clean / build succeeds / type check / bug fixed / requirements met / feature works / change in place) and a nine-row Common Rationalizations table. Adapted from the comparison's Section 5a source pattern into Nexus-Hub's professional teaching tone (not the source "human partner" voice -- comparison N2), with the `description` kept trigger-focused and "what it does" pushed into `overview_l1` (the Section 4 insight adopted without the wholesale convention swap -- N1).

T002 added `catalog/skills/code-review/receiving-code-review/SKILL.md`: a discipline skill for acting on review feedback with technical rigor and no performative agreement. It encodes a six-step per-comment response pattern (read fully, restate, verify against the codebase, evaluate for THIS codebase, respond with acknowledgment or reasoned push-back, implement one item and test it), a Forbidden Responses section (no "you're absolutely right", no gratitude filler -- state the fix instead), a YAGNI check for "do it properly" suggestions (grep for real usage before adding an abstraction), and a clarify-then-blocking-then-simple-then-complex implementation ordering. Its SKIP clause fences off authoring a review (the existing `code-review/*` skills). T003 added `catalog/skills/workflow/using-git-worktrees/SKILL.md`: encodes the safe worktree sequence -- Step 0 detect existing isolation (`git-dir` vs `--git-common-dir`, with a `--show-superproject-working-tree` submodule guard), Step 1a prefer the harness native worktree tool (`EnterWorktree` / `/worktree`) over raw git, Step 1b git fallback with a directory-priority order and a mandatory `git check-ignore -q` gate before any in-repo placement, Step 3 auto-detect project setup, Step 4 verify a clean test baseline before reporting ready. POSIX commands with PowerShell-equivalent notes.

T004 registered all three skills: one row each in `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (total `227 -> 230`), three full schema entries in `data/skills.json` (now 231 entries), and `data/marketplace.json` category `skill_count` bumps for `code-review` (10 -> 11) and `workflow` (27 -> 29). The `based_on` field references the adoption plan rather than naming the external source, per the AGENTS.md Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule; no other `data/` content was edited. T005 stabilized: the `make validate` validators were run individually (make is unavailable on this Windows host) -- skills.json loads (231), marketplace.json loads, per-skill bundle audit PASS (0/0), no-personal-paths clean, unicode-safety clean (skills written ASCII-only), quality-heuristics PASS with zero warnings attributable to the new skills, supply-chain IOC scan and workflow-security clean. `make lint` is a no-op (no shell scripts added). The live `skill-eval-loop` trigger run was deferred in favor of a static trigger-surface check (each skill's `description` confirmed to contain a verbatim phrase matching a should-trigger prompt; each should-not-trigger prompt confirmed fenced by the `SKIP:` clause); the live run is recorded as DF-v23-7 in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md` for the release sweep. README.md and AGENTS.md catalog counts were intentionally left for Phase 6 (T022). Zero new runtime dependencies, zero new scripts (no installer copy-step needed), zero outbound calls. Next: Phase 2 (skill-authoring methodology upgrade).

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 9: live-environment verification (P1, FINAL)

Implemented Phase 9 (the final phase) of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan, closing the last four v2.2.0 carryover gaps (WN-2, WN-3, WN-4 Antigravity CLI assumptions; WN-8 cross-OS installer smoke). Subtasks T032-T036 closed. The plan framed these as live-environment verifications that T036 explicitly allowed resolving via documentation or re-deferring with a dated reason if Google had not shipped a verifiable channel. By 2026-05-29 (the Gemini-CLI-to-Antigravity-CLI cutover is 2026-06-18) Google's Antigravity CLI documentation is public, so WN-2/WN-3/WN-4 were verified against primary docs + official codelabs rather than a live install; WN-8 was re-run on Windows and cross-checked against CI's Linux run. The pre-implementation review surfaced that the v2.2.0 probe had INFERRED the Antigravity conventions by analogy to Gemini CLI, and the docs contradicted several of them, so the path-rewrite scope was taken to the user up front (the user chose the full rewrite including the global-dir restructure).

T032 (WN-2): the Antigravity CLI binary name was CORRECTED from the inferred `antigravity` to `agy` (the official docs page is titled "Using AGY CLI"; the binary installs to `~/.local/bin/agy`; corroborated by multiple practitioner guides). This was a silent-failure bug: the diff-review hook detects the binary with `command -v` and fails open, so the wrong name made the entire Antigravity pre-commit review a no-op on every machine rather than erroring. Fixed the binary detection + invocation in `catalog/hooks/antigravity-cli-diff-review.sh`/`.ps1` (the product-named filename was kept, consistent with its siblings and its registration in both installers). T033 (WN-3): the workflow file format was CONFIRMED as Markdown under `.agents/workflows/` (the inferred value was right), reconciled in `test_antigravity_commands.py`. T034 (WN-4): YAML frontmatter is honored and a workflow's name derives from its filename -- DOCUMENTED; the existing verbatim `catalog/commands/*.md` mirror is compatible, so no `_write_antigravity_commands` variant was needed.

Verification surfaced bonus path corrections beyond the binary name: the per-project dir is `.agents/` (plural, per official codelab) not `.agent/`, the instruction file is `AGENTS.md` not `AGENT.md`, and the global CLI footprint is `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` not `~/.agent/`. These were applied across `scripts/lib/integrations/antigravity.py` (`workspace_dir=.agents`, `global_dir=~/.gemini/antigravity-cli`, `instruction_file=AGENTS.md`), both installers' legacy mirror paths in lockstep, the `base-antigravity-20.md`/`base-antigravity-cli.md` templates, AGENTS.md, README.md, and the three antigravity integration test files (20 tests pass; `installer.ps1 -PrintConfig antigravity2` now shows the `.agents/` tree). The integration writes its instruction file to `.agents/AGENTS.md` rather than the project root: `agy` reads a root `AGENTS.md`, but that file is already managed by the `codex` integration through the single shared `## Nexus-Hub` marker, so pointing two integrations at it would clobber the block (a real install-time regression isolated tests would not catch). Whether `agy` requires a root `AGENTS.md` (needing a per-integration marker scheme) is one of four residuals recorded as WN-v23-5.

T035 (WN-8): the cross-OS smoke was re-run. Windows is empirically green -- 936 pytest cases (catalog/hooks 392+3 skipped, integrations+installer 260, validators 44, skill-server 43, code-search 168+1 skipped, web-fetch 29), eval recall 100% / precision 96.2% reproducing `docs/v2.3.0/eval-baseline.md`, and installer.ps1 -Help/-PrintConfig probes for claude/antigravity2/gemini-cli exit 0 with the negative case exiting 1. The Linux Python test suite is empirically green via CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the suites on ubuntu-latest), replacing the v2.2.0 PASS-by-parity inference for the test suite. macOS (no host available) plus the Linux installer-probe/eval portion (not in CI) are re-deferred with a dated reason as DF-v23-6 -- v2.3.0 is a source release, so these are required only before a packaged-binary release. Recorded in `docs/v2.3.0/installer-smoke-post.txt` and `docs/v2.2.0/antigravity-cli-probe.md` Section 11.

All four ingested v2.2.0 gaps moved to the Resolved table in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`; two new low-priority gaps were recorded (WN-v23-5 Antigravity live-VM residuals: `.agent/` vs `.agents/` codelab dissent, exact global subpath, subagents/rules subdirs, root-vs-`.agents/` instruction file; DF-v23-6 macOS + Linux-installer-probe smoke deferral). The `make validate` equivalent is green (skills.json 227, bundle audit 0/0, four CI validators rc=0); shellcheck + `bash -n` + PowerShell `Parser::ParseFile` are clean on every edited script. Zero new runtime dependencies; no new standalone scripts (so no installer copy-step registration needed); zero outbound calls. This closes all 12 v2.2.0 ingested known-gaps and completes the plan; the v2.3.0 release-readiness workflow follows.

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 8: code-graph quality & extractor expansion (P1)

Implemented Phase 8 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan, closing the five v2.2.0 `nexus-code-search` carryover gaps (WN-1, WN-5, WN-6, WN-7, DF-002). Subtasks T026 (pathspec deprecation), T027 (tree-sitter pin re-verification), T028 (instantiates/overrides edges), T029 (search precision), T030 (Go/Rust/Java/C# extractors), and T031 (stabilization) all closed. Scope stayed entirely within `extensions/nexus-code-search/` plus the Makefile `eval` target path and docs; catalog and data files were untouched. Two implementation-shaping decisions were taken to the user up front: the T030 language batch (Go + Rust + Java + C#) and the T029 precision approach (scope the default search to the name column, rather than the plan's kind-inference heuristic or answer-key widening).

T026 (WN-1) switched `indexer.py::_load_ignore_spec` from the deprecated `gitwildmatch` pathspec factory to `gitignore` (verified to match identically on the indexer fixtures); the 52 `GitWildMatchPattern` DeprecationWarnings are gone and the full suite now passes under `pytest -W error::DeprecationWarning`. T027 (WN-5) re-verified the pin on 2026-05-29 -- no 0.26-compatible release of `tree-sitter` (0.25.2), `tree-sitter-python` (0.25.0), or `tree-sitter-typescript` (0.23.2) has shipped, so the `<0.26` ceiling stays, recorded in a dated `pyproject.toml` comment.

T028 (WN-6) taught `python.py` and `typescript.py` to emit `instantiates` (a call / `new` expression whose resolved target is a class, replacing the plain `calls` edge) and `overrides` (a method shadowing a same-named parent method, via a new `_resolve_overrides` pass that walks the in-file `extends` chain transitively with a cycle guard). `INSTANTIATES` was added to `traverser._IMPACT_KINDS` so re-typing a constructor call from `calls` does not silently drop it from `code_impact`. T029 (WN-7) wrapped the default FTS query as `name : (<query>)` in `traverser.search_fts` (new `_scope_to_name` helper), with an `all_columns` opt-out threaded through `GraphQueryManager.search` and surfaced on the `code_search` MCP tool as `all_fields`. The precision shortfall was a column-matching artifact: a query like `AdminUser` previously matched the class plus every node embedding it in `qualified_name` (its method `is_admin`, parameter `self`). Name-scoping raised aggregate eval precision from 63.3% to 96.2% with recall held at 100%.

T030 (DF-002) shipped four new extractors (`go.py`, `rust.py`, `java.py`, `csharp.py`) registered in `LANGUAGE_EXTRACTORS` for `.go`/`.rs`/`.java`/`.cs`/`.csx`, each following the `python.py` pattern. Go emits structs / interfaces / receiver-keyed methods / fields and `instantiates` from composite literals; Rust emits structs / enums / traits / impl-block methods, `implements` from `impl Trait for Type`, and `instantiates` from struct literals; Java and C# emit the full OOP edge set (`extends` / `implements` / `overrides` / `instantiates`), with C# resolving its single syntactic `base_list` into `extends` vs `implements` by the resolved target's kind. The new extractors deliberately omit `parameter` nodes to avoid re-introducing the FTS noise T029 just removed. Four tree-sitter grammar deps (go 0.25, rust 0.24, java/c-sharp 0.23) were added to `pyproject.toml` under the shared `<0.26` ceiling; no installer edit was needed because both installers resolve them via `pip install -e` of the copied package, and T030 added no standalone `scripts/*.py`. Four eval fixtures (`go_app` / `rust_app` / `java_app` / `csharp_app`) were added, each clearing the 80% per-fixture recall gate at 100%.

T031 created `docs/v2.3.0/eval-baseline.md` (eight fixtures: 100% aggregate recall, 96.2% aggregate precision) and repointed the Makefile `eval` target there. Tests after the phase: nexus-code-search 168 passed / 1 skipped (+32 new -- 6 each for Go/Rust/Java/C#, 3 Python-edge, 2 TS-edge, 2 name-scope, 1 recall-gate), zero DeprecationWarnings; the `make validate` equivalent (skills.json 227 OK, bundle audit 0 orphans, four CI validators rc=0) is green; CI requires no workflow edit (the editable install resolves the new deps and auto-discovers the new tests). Two new low-priority gaps were recorded: DF-v23-4 (extractor coverage is partial -- 4 of ~18 languages, no new framework extractors, no parameter nodes for the new languages) and DF-v23-5 (a `code_search` precision residual where import-statement nodes named with the dotted import path are legitimately surfaced; python_app sits at 70%). The five carryover items moved to the Resolved table in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`. Four new runtime dependencies, all local tree-sitter grammars; zero outbound network calls anywhere in the new extractors.

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 7: installer instruction-file parity (P1)

Implemented Phase 7 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan, closing the three v2.2.0 installer-parity carryover gaps (DF-001, MT-1, MT-2) so the legacy bash / PowerShell instruction-file rendering blocks could be removed. Subtasks T022 (placeholder threading + path/template reconciliation), T023 (body-parity test), T024 (Copilot marker-merge), and T025 (stabilization) all closed. The user selected the reconcile-then-remove path after the pre-implementation review surfaced that the legacy bash path and the Python registry had diverged in three ways -- placeholders, workspace path, and Gemini template -- so a naive call-swap would have regressed end-user output.

The Phase 1 review compared the bash `render_template` (today's end-user output) against the Python registry runner and found: (1) bash filled 15 `{{...}}` tokens + `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` + per-language coding snippets while the runner substituted only `{{PROJECT_NAME}}`; (2) bash wrote claude / codex workspace instruction files at the project root while the registry nested them under `.claude/` / `.codex/` (where the tools do not read them); (3) bash rendered `base-gemini.md` while the registry pointed at the orphan stub `base-gemini-ide.md`. A fourth difference -- the registry wraps the body in `<!-- NEXUS_HUB_START/END -->` markers for user-edit preservation while bash wrote raw -- is the deliberate v2.2.0 improvement, so parity is asserted at the managed-body level rather than whole-file.

T022 brought the runner to body parity. `MarkdownIntegration._render` now merges a built-in default placeholder map (mirroring the bash constant `sed` values), auto-loads `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` from `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`, leaves unknown tokens literal exactly as the bash `sed` list does (so `{{AGENT_REGISTRY}}` and friends survive verbatim), and appends per-language coding-snippet fragments. `scripts/lib/integrations/runner.py` gained `--var KEY=VALUE` (repeatable), `--languages`, and `--instruction-only` on `install` (plus `--var` / `--languages` on `print-config`), and `InstallContext` gained `languages` and `instruction_only`. The path divergence was reconciled with a new `instruction_workspace_dir` config key (default = `workspace_dir`) set to `""` for claude and codex so CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md land at the project root while skills/ still mirror nested; the Gemini integration was repointed to the canonical `base-gemini.md`.

T024 (MT-1) refactored `CopilotIntegration.install_workspace` from its bespoke append-after-heading flow onto the canonical `merge_marker_section(..., legacy_header="## Nexus-Hub Harness")` primitive (matching Cursor), so the v2.1 legacy header migrates inline, re-installs settle to `unchanged`, and teardown removes only the marker block. The removal step replaced all six `render_template` / `Render-Template` instruction-file calls (claude / codex / gemini, global + workspace) with `invoke_registry_platform ... --instruction-only` / `Invoke-RegistryPlatform ... -InstructionOnly`, keeping the surrounding catalog-copy blocks, and deleted both dead render functions. Both installer helpers now thread their detected globals (PROJECT_NAME, PRIMARY_LANGUAGE, BUILD_CMD, OS_CONTEXT, ...) to the runner, which is now the single instruction-file renderer shared by bash and PowerShell -- a side benefit being it also fixed a latent literal-placeholder bug for the already-registry-driven platforms (cursor / opencode / antigravity / nexus-ai).

T023 (MT-2) added `test_instruction_body_parity_with_legacy_render` (claude / codex / gemini x global + workspace) to `tests/integrations/test_parity_with_legacy_installer.py`: it installs each integration in isolation with the full placeholder set, extracts the marker body, and asserts byte equality against an INDEPENDENT naive-`str.replace` reference render (the bash-semantics oracle), plus a no-literal-placeholder completeness check. Installing each integration in isolation keeps the assertion deterministic even though a real multi-platform install now has codex and cursor sharing the root AGENTS.md marker block (last-writer-wins over two near-identical templates -- cleaner than the prior bash behavior, which duplicated both bodies).

Tests after the phase: `tests/integrations` + `tests/installer` + `tests/validators` 304 passed / 0 failed (the single initial failure, a Copilot test asserting the old bespoke `kept` action, was a TEST-class issue updated to the canonical `unchanged`); `catalog/hooks/tests` 392 passed + 3 skipped; the `make validate` equivalent (JSON + bundle audit + quality pass + four CI validators) is green; ShellCheck `--severity=warning` on both installers is clean; `bash -n` and PowerShell `Parser::ParseFile` both pass. No new runtime dependencies, no new standalone scripts (so no installer copy-step registration needed), zero outbound calls. One new low-priority gap recorded: DF-v23-3 (`base-gemini-ide.md` is now orphaned as an instruction template; optional future cleanup). See `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`.

## [2026-05-29] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 6: framework coverage + defensive security content (P1)

Implemented Phase 6 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan: a framework coverage-matrix generator over the Phase 1 mapping convention, plus two re-authored defensive security skill packs and their helper scripts. Subtasks T017 (coverage generator), T018 (defensive batch 1), T019 (defensive batch 2), T020 (helper scripts), and T021 (testing and stabilization) all closed. Bulk import of the source cybersecurity corpus stays explicitly rejected (plan appendix N5 / N6 / N7); this phase ships curated, re-authored content only.

`scripts/build_framework_coverage.py` reads the optional framework-mapping frontmatter fields introduced in Phase 1 (`mitre_attack` / `atlas_techniques` / `d3fend_techniques` / `nist_csf` / `nist_ai_rmf`) across `catalog/skills/` and emits a coverage matrix: a summary table (distinct controls + skill tags per framework) plus per-framework control-to-skill detail tables, in Markdown (default) or JSON (`--format json`), to stdout or a `--out <file>` artifact. It ships its own tolerant bracket-list parser (the existing `validate_skills.py` frontmatter reader leaves `[...]` as a raw string), accepts `--root` so it slots into the `tests/validators/conftest.py` subprocess harness, and is read-only / local / zero-outbound -- a catalog with no tagged skills produces a successful empty matrix rather than an error. After the new content landed the matrix spans 34 ATT&CK techniques, 6 D3FEND countermeasures, and 10 NIST CSF categories. It is registered as an explicit-name copy step in both installers and covered by 6 pytest cases in `tests/validators/test_build_framework_coverage.py`.

The maintainer approved a new `security-operations` skill category (the AGENTS.md "Ask first: creating a new skill category" gate), separating defensive operational skills from the application-security `security/` category. Fifteen skills were re-authored to Nexus-Hub format under it. Batch 1 (DFIR / threat hunting / incident response): `memory-forensics`, `hunting-credential-dumping`, `disk-artifact-forensics`, `siem-detection-engineering`, `log-threat-hunting`, `lateral-movement-detection`, `ransomware-incident-response`, `persistence-mechanism-hunting`, `endpoint-edr-detection`. Batch 2 (cloud / endpoint / phishing): `cloud-security-posture-detection`, `cloud-audit-log-detection`, `container-runtime-detection`, `phishing-analysis-and-defense`, `identity-threat-detection`, `malware-triage-analysis`. Each skill carries a pushy description (verbatim trigger phrases + a SKIP clause), MITRE ATT&CK / D3FEND / NIST CSF framework-mapping frontmatter, a `references/standards.md` companion documenting every mapped control ID (framework name, short title, rationale, public source URL), a Common Rationalizations table whose Reality column cites concrete failure modes, and a binary Verification checklist tied to observable artifacts. All content was re-authored from public MITRE / NIST framework knowledge -- no third-party SKILL.md text was copied and no source repository is named in the artifact (Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule) -- and the mandate filter was enforced: defensive / detection / forensics / incident-response only, with offensive and detection-evasion content excluded (the phishing and malware-triage skills explicitly state "defense only" and "never write malware"). The 15 skills were drafted by five parallel subagents working from a single rigorous spec with pre-assigned, verified framework IDs; the data-registry edits and helper scripts were authored centrally to keep the three counters consistent and avoid concurrent JSON edits.

Two skills ship a deterministic helper with `.sh` + `.ps1` parity per the AGENTS.md cross-platform rule. `memory-forensics/scripts/volatility-runner.sh|.ps1` is a thin, read-only wrapper around a locally-installed Volatility 3 (`vol`) that runs a fixed triage plugin set (process tree, hidden-process carve, module list, injection scan, network connections, handles, cmdline) against a memory image into a per-case output directory, hashing the image first for chain of custody; it requires Volatility 3 already installed, fetches no symbol packs over the network, and never executes carved samples. `log-threat-hunting/scripts/ioc-log-scan.sh|.ps1` is a local read-only IOC sweep that fixed-string-matches an indicator list against a log file and reports per-indicator counts and matching lines (comment and blank lines in the IOC list are ignored). Both bash scripts follow the project safety rules (`set -euo pipefail`, quoted expansions, `command -v` capability checks, per-item failure isolation), are shellcheck-clean at `--severity=warning`, are referenced from their parent SKILL.md so the orphan-bundle audit passes, and were functionally verified (the IOC sweep against a fixture log; the Volatility wrapper's missing-`vol` precondition path).

All 15 skills were registered in the three data files: `data/skills.json` (212 -> 227 entries; `statistics.total_skills` 208 -> 223; new `categories.security-operations` = 15), `data/marketplace.json` (new "Security Operations" category with skill_count 15), and `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (+15 rows; total 211 -> 226 skills across 22 -> 23 categories). The registration ran as one programmatic pass that read each SKILL.md's frontmatter verbatim, so no metadata was transcribed by hand; the resulting JSON diffs are pure appends (the only deletions are the intended `total_skills` and trailing-comma changes). `AGENTS.md` gained the `security-operations` entry in its category list, a placement-guidance paragraph distinguishing it from `security`, and an updated catalog-overview count. The pre-existing `statistics.total_skills` drift (WN-v23-1) was carried forward consistently (+15 across all counters) rather than reconciled, since reconciliation is outside the Phase 6 sub-task scope.

Tests after the phase: `tests/` 298 passed (was 291; +6 coverage-generator cases plus one), 0 failed, in 8m39s (the runtime is dominated by `tests/integrations` doing real file-tree installs over a OneDrive-synced path); `tests/validators` 44 passed; the four CI validators all exit 0; the per-skill bundle audit scans 233 skills with 0 orphans; the quality-heuristics pass adds 0 new warnings (the 15 new skills clear it cleanly, and the pre-existing 574-warning grandfathered debt under WN-v23-4 is unchanged); shellcheck is clean on both installers and the two new helper scripts. Two new known gaps were recorded: QG-v23-1 (the CI `shellcheck` job lints `catalog/hooks/*.sh` but not the new per-skill helper scripts under `catalog/skills/**/scripts/`; the one-line fix is proposed but not auto-applied per the "never silently rewrite CI" rule) and DF-v23-2 (the defensive skill bodies are concise and fully conformant but could optionally be deepened with platform-specific query examples in a future catalog-quality pass). See `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`.

Zero new runtime dependencies; everything is stdlib Python or POSIX/PowerShell shell; zero outbound network calls anywhere in the new generator, skills, or helpers, consistent with the MCP Registry Policy reverse-engineer-first posture.

## [2026-05-28] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 5: skill quality tooling (P2)

Implemented Phase 5 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan, reverse-engineering ECC's `skill-stocktake` and `skill-create` patterns into local, zero-outbound Nexus-Hub capabilities. Subtasks T014 (skill-stocktake skill + validator quality pass), T015 (skill-create skill), and T016 (testing and stabilization) all closed.

`catalog/skills/workflow/skill-stocktake/SKILL.md` is a two-mode holistic quality audit. Quick Scan reads a cached `.nexus/skill-stocktake/results.json`, recomputes a per-skill content hash, and re-scores only skills whose hash changed since the last run, so an audit after a small edit is near-instant. Full Stocktake scores every skill and rewrites the cache. The deterministic half is delegated to the validator's new `--quality` pass rather than re-implemented in the skill body (the skill's unique value is the caching and the holistic agent judgment). The skill is propose-only: it ranks skills worst-first and suggests remediation but never auto-edits a SKILL.md.

`catalog/skills/workflow/skill-create/SKILL.md` drafts a new Nexus-Hub-conformant SKILL.md by mining the local git history (`git log` / `git diff`, zero outbound) for a recurring pattern, confirming it with the user, then writing a full draft with a pushy description, quoted Tier-1 fields, binary Verification, and a Common Rationalizations table whose entries cite failure modes observed in the actual commits. Per the user's Phase 0 decision, this shipped as a skill-only deliverable (no `scripts/skill_create.py`), so no installer registration was needed. The draft is surfaced for maintainer review and never auto-registered or committed.

`scripts/validate_skills.py` gained a non-blocking `--quality` heuristics pass and a `_section_body` helper. The pass emits warnings (never errors, always exit 0) for four authoring norms from AGENTS.md: a missing `## Common Rationalizations` section, a prose-only `## Verification` section (no `- [ ]` checklist), over-long Tier-1 fields (`summary_l0` > 15 words, `overview_l1` > 150 words), and a missing or unlinked `## Related Skills` section. Each warning carries a `quality:` prefix so the stocktake skill can distinguish quality findings from orphan-bundle findings. The pass is wired into the `make validate` target after the bundle audit and runs across the whole catalog: it currently surfaces 574 warnings on grandfathered skills (tracked as WN-v23-4), while both new Phase 5 skills clear it with zero warnings. Six new pytest cases in `tests/validators/test_validate_skills_quality.py` assert a low-quality fixture trips every heuristic, a well-formed fixture stays silent, the `quality:` prefix is present, section extraction is case-insensitive, and the `--quality` CLI always exits 0.

Both new skills were registered in the three data files: `data/skills.json` (210 -> 212 entries), `data/marketplace.json` (workflow `skill_count` 25 -> 27), and `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (total 209 -> 211). During stabilization, three pre-existing personal-path false positives were redacted to the `<user>` placeholder: prose in `docs/DEVLOG.md`, `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`, and the Phase 4 session history that literally spelled out the real `/Users/<user>/...` form while describing the redaction work itself. These predated Phase 5 but blocked `make validate`; the fix restores a green no-personal-paths gate on the v2.3.0 tree.

Tests after the phase: `tests/` 291 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 392 passed + 3 skipped; `tests/validators` 37 passed (incl. 6 new); `nexus-skill-server` 43 passed (confirms the `data/skills.json` edits still parse). The full `make validate` equivalent (JSON integrity + bundle audit + quality pass + four CI validators) is green. Known gaps: WN-v23-4 (574 quality warnings on grandfathered skills, deliberately non-blocking, to be remediated via a dedicated catalog-quality pass driven by the new skill-stocktake skill). See `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`.

## [2026-05-28] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 4: installer lifecycle & selective install (P1)

Implemented Phase 4 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan. The phase reverse-engineers ECC's `doctor.js` / `repair.js` / `list-installed.js` / `install-plan.js` / `install-apply.js` / `consult.js` / `harness-audit.js` surface onto Nexus-Hub's existing Python integration registry. Subtasks T010 (install-state manifest + doctor/repair/list-installed), T011 (selective-install profiles + modules + consult advisor), T012 (harness audit scoring), and T013 (testing) all closed.

`scripts/lib/integrations/manifest.py` gained an additive `record_actions(integration_key, file_actions)` method that captures the full `FileAction` history (`path`, `action`, `sha256`, `mtime`) per installed file. The existing `_tracked` / `_shared` / `_logs` fields are untouched, so the 50-case integration contract suite continues to pass (191/191 in `tests/integrations` green). The runner auto-records actions after each integration install through the new line in `cmd_install`. Two helpers, `_hash_path` and `_mtime`, sit at module scope so they can be re-used by the lifecycle and audit code.

`scripts/lib/integrations/lifecycle.py` is the new home of `doctor`, `repair`, and `list_installed`. `doctor` walks the manifest, compares each recorded SHA-256 against the file's current bytes, and emits one of four diagnostics (`ok` / `missing` / `drifted` / `unknown`) per file. `repair` filters to integrations whose `doctor` report shows drift or missing files, then re-runs `integration.install(ctx)` so marker semantics still apply (user edits outside `<!-- nexus-hub:start -->...<!-- nexus-hub:end -->` are preserved; edits inside the markers are replaced -- the existing behavior). `list_installed` is a pure data accessor. The three operations are exposed as new subcommands on `scripts/lib/integrations/runner.py` (`doctor` / `repair` / `list-installed`) with `--json` / `--quiet` flags and matching `--integrations` filters.

`data/bundles.json` schema bumped to 1.4.0 with two new top-level keys, `profiles` (minimal / core / full -- a coarse install scope) and `modules` (testing / code-review / security-ops / ai-engineering / infrastructure / documentation -- finer capability-tagged groupings). The existing `bundles` array is untouched so every existing consumer keeps working. `scripts/nexus_hub_consult.py` is a local, read-only natural-language matcher over skills + bundles + profiles + modules: tokenize the user's need (with a small stopword list), score each candidate by token overlap + id-exact-match boost + tag-match boost, sort by score, and emit the top N with the install command line the user should run. The actual ranking heuristic in `score_candidate()` is marked as a user-contribution slot for future tuning -- the baseline already returns useful results, but a refined IDF or field-weighted scorer is a worthwhile follow-up.

`scripts/harness_audit.py` is a deterministic, read-only scorer that takes the install manifest and the running registry and emits a 0-100 score per integration plus an aggregate score. Four axes -- `presence` (recorded files that still exist), `integrity` (recorded SHA-256s that still match), `coverage` (declared surfaces in `config` that the manifest actually wrote to), and `marker_integrity` (shared instruction files whose marker pair is intact) -- are combined via configurable weights (default 0.30 / 0.30 / 0.20 / 0.20). The weighted-combine step is a user-contribution slot too, so a future tuner can swap to a multiplicative or quadratic-penalty model. Output is Markdown by default; `--json` is available for CI consumption; `--min-score N` exits non-zero below the threshold so CI can gate on the audit.

`scripts/installer.sh` and `scripts/installer.ps1` register the two new standalone scripts (`nexus_hub_consult.py`, `harness_audit.py`) under the existing v2.3.0 CI-validator block in lockstep. The doctor / repair / list-installed surface ships through the existing `scripts/lib/integrations/` registry copy step -- no new installer entry needed for those.

Three new pytest modules under `tests/integrations/`: `test_lifecycle.py` (13 cases) exercises `record_actions` round-trip, `doctor` / `repair` against the real `claude` integration with `fake_home` / `fresh_target` fixtures so any regression in the install pipeline is caught here; `test_consult.py` (12 cases) covers the tokenizer, candidate loader, scorer, and CLI; `test_harness_audit.py` (8 cases) covers clean / drifted / missing scoring, JSON output, the `--min-score` threshold, and unknown-integration handling. Full integration suite: 191 passed (was 158, +33 new). The 50-case integration contract suite is unchanged and stays green.

Documentation cleanup: four pre-existing `/Users/<user>/...` findings in `docs/DEVLOG.md` (1) and `docs/v2.3.0/development/history/2026-05-28_phase-3-runtime-learning.md` (3) were redacted to the `<user>` placeholder so `make validate` stays clean on the v2.3.0 tree without exclude-list creep. These predated Phase 4; the Phase 3 close note had flagged the redaction pattern.

No new known gaps. The Summary counts in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md` are unchanged (5 open: 1 BG, 1 DF, 3 WN). The `Status` line advances to "Phases 1-4 of 9 closed". The v2.2.0 carryover gaps continue to wait for Phases 7-9.

Zero new runtime dependencies; everything is stdlib; no outbound network calls; the additive-manifest guarantee in the plan is honored. JSON catalog integrity: `data/skills.json` 210 entries, `data/bundles.json` 15 bundles + 3 profiles + 6 modules, `data/workflows.json` 17 workflows, `data/templates.json` OK. Per-skill bundle audit scans 216 skills with 0 errors and 0 warnings. All four Phase 2 CI validators run clean against the Phase 4 tree.

## [2026-05-28] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 3: runtime learning (P1)

Implemented Phase 3 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan. The phase reverse-engineers ECC's memory-persistence and continuous-learning patterns as a strict local-only subset: a project-scoped session digest written and read across `SessionStart` / `Stop` / `PreCompact` / `SessionEnd`, plus a continuous-learning skill that mints `.nexus/instincts/*.yaml` from a `.nexus/observations.jsonl` capture hook. The hard constraint, enforced in code and in the skill body's Common Rationalizations table, is no background observer model and no outbound network call -- the only consumer of observations is the agent itself, in-session. Subtasks T007 (memory-persistence session hooks), T008 (continuous-learning skill + capture hooks), and T009 (stabilization) all closed.

`catalog/hooks/session-summary.sh` (the existing `Stop` hook) gained a second job alongside appending to the global `$HOME/.claude/session-log.md`: it now writes a compact digest at `<project_root>/.nexus/context/last-session.md` capturing `Generated:` / `Project:` / `Duration:` plus a `## Git context` block (branch, working-tree one-liner) and, when present, fenced `## Recent commits` (last five oneline) and `## Files touched this session` (`git diff --name-only HEAD`, capped at 30 entries). Project root is resolved via `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` with a cwd fallback. The write is atomic via `mktemp` + `mv -f` so a partial write cannot leave a corrupt digest. The complementary `catalog/hooks/session-start.sh` reads the digest back on `SessionStart` capped at `NEXUS_SESSION_START_MAX_CHARS` (default 8000), prints it as `Last session digest (...):`, and appends a truncation marker when the file exceeds the cap. PowerShell siblings (`session-start.ps1`, `session-summary.ps1`) ship in lockstep per the AGENTS.md cross-platform hook parity rule. `catalog/hooks/settings.json` adds `PreCompact` and `SessionEnd` registrations for `session-summary.sh` alongside its existing `Stop` registration. Runtime controls: `NEXUS_SESSION_DIGEST=off`, `NEXUS_SESSION_DIGEST_PATH=<path>`, `NEXUS_DISABLED_HOOKS=session-start|session-summary`, `NEXUS_HOOK_PROFILE=minimal`.

`catalog/hooks/learning-capture.sh` (and `.ps1`) is a new hook wired to `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop` in `catalog/hooks/settings.json`. It reads a Claude Code payload from stdin and appends one JSON-per-line record to `<project_root>/.nexus/observations.jsonl` with the four fields the continuous-learning skill consumes: `ts` (ISO-8601 UTC), `event` (from `hook_event_name` with `event` / `type` fallbacks), `tool` (from `tool_name` with `tool` fallback), and `prompt_sample` (first 400 chars of `prompt` / `user_prompt`). JSON parsing prefers `python3` / `python` (most universal across platforms) with a `jq` fallback and a no-parser sentinel fallback; the bash + Python flow uses env-var passing to avoid the heredoc / pipe stdin collision. Size cap: when the file exceeds `NEXUS_LEARNING_MAX_BYTES` (default 1 MiB), the hook truncates to the most-recent half via a line-based tail. Off-switches: `NEXUS_LEARNING_CAPTURE=off`, `NEXUS_LEARNING_PATH=<path>`, `NEXUS_DISABLED_HOOKS=learning-capture`, `NEXUS_HOOK_PROFILE=minimal`.

The new `catalog/skills/workflow/continuous-learning/SKILL.md` (130 lines, 10.8KB) is the analysis half of the loop. Body sections: a pushy `description` with explicit trigger phrases and SKIP-bulk for background-observer wiring, a `Storage Layout` table covering observations / instincts / index files, numbered Instructions for the five-step loop (observe passively via the hook, survey on demand, mint each confirmed pattern as a one-finding `.nexus/instincts/<slug>.yaml` with `slug` / `created` / `confidence` / `domains` / `trigger` / `behavior` / `evidence` fields, evolve high-confidence clusters into draft `SKILL.md` or commands for maintainer review only, regenerate `.nexus/instincts/_index.md`, prune via `archived: true`), a Common Rationalizations table whose first row directly rebuts the "wire a background observer model" temptation (citing the MCP Registry Policy hard-no list), a binary Verification checklist (one JSONL record per call, all four fields present, confidence in [0.0, 1.0], no daemon / network at any step), and cross-links to `[[dev-progress-tracker]]`, `[[context-modes]]`, `[[create-custom-command]]`, `[[manage-memory]]`, `[[known-gaps-tracker]]`. Registered in `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (208 -> 209), `data/skills.json` (209 -> 210 entries), and `data/marketplace.json` (workflow `skill_count` 24 -> 25).

22 new pytest cases under `catalog/hooks/tests/` exercise both halves. `test_session_digest.py` (11 cases) covers syntax, round-trip, custom path override, silent-on-no-digest, off-switch on both sides, size-cap truncation marker, invalid cap fallback, and the `NEXUS_HOOK_PROFILE=minimal` global skip. `test_learning_capture.py` (11 cases) covers syntax, single-record append with all four required fields, `tool_name` / `tool` fallback resolution, multi-call append, all four runtime controls, size-cap trimming when exceeded, a static-analysis no-network-token check (greps the script body for `curl`, `wget`, `ssh`, `scp`, `sftp`, `rsync`, `ncat`, `telnet`, `ftp`, `urlopen`, `http://`, `https://`, etc.), and a no-write-outside-project-root assertion. Runtime PATH-sandboxing was abandoned because Git Bash binaries on Windows are dynamically linked to `msys-2.0.dll` and cannot be copied in isolation; the static-analysis check is both stronger and portable.

Full test sweep is green: `pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` reports 392 passed + 3 skipped (was 370 + 3 skipped before Phase 3, the 22 new cases are the entire delta), `pytest tests/validators/` 31 passed, `pytest tests/integrations/ tests/installer/` 254 passed (no regression), broader repo-level run 646 passed + 3 skipped in ~533 s. `shellcheck --severity=warning` is clean on `session-start.sh`, `session-summary.sh`, and `learning-capture.sh`. JSON catalog integrity: `data/skills.json` 210 entries, `data/bundles.json` 15, `data/workflows.json` 17, `data/templates.json` OK. Per-skill bundle audit (`scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) scans 216 skills with 0 errors and 0 warnings. All four Phase 2 CI validators run clean against the Phase 3 tree.

Two implementation choices worth recording. (1) The `learning-capture.sh` JSON parser prefers Python over `jq` because `jq` is not universally on PATH on Windows + Git Bash and was missing from the test environment; the Python path is functionally available in every environment Nexus-Hub supports. (2) Two pre-existing `/Users/<user>/...` findings in Phase 2 docs (the v23-1 DF item description in `known-gaps.md` and the Phase 2 history file) were redacted to use the `<user>` placeholder. These were Phase 2's own documentation *of* the validator finding; making them validator-clean honors the Phase 3 prerequisite ("validators ensure no personal paths leak into persisted artifacts") and lets `make validate` stay green on the v2.3.0 tree without exclude-list creep.

No new known gaps were introduced. The Summary counts in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md` are unchanged (5 open: 1 BG, 1 DF, 3 WN); the `Status` and `Last updated` header lines record the Phase 3 close. The v2.2.0 carryover gaps continue to wait for Phases 7-9.

Phase 3 is independent of Phases 4-9 by spirit (the new validators ensure no personal paths leak into the persisted digest / observations files, which was the stated Phase 2 -> Phase 3 dependency). The runtime-learning surface is now fully wired and tested; Phase 4 (Installer lifecycle & selective install) is next and builds on the existing `WriteResult` / `FileAction` / `--check` infrastructure from v2.2.0.

## [2026-05-28] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 2: security & quality CI validators (P0)

Implemented Phase 2 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan. The phase ships four standalone, local-only, zero-outbound Python validators under `scripts/` that reverse-engineer ECC's `scripts/ci/{validate-no-personal-paths,check-unicode-safety,scan-supply-chain-iocs,validate-workflow-security}.js` per the MCP Registry Policy tier-3 (reverse-engineer-first) decision. No Node runtime is introduced; no third-party library is added; the validators run on the Python interpreter already required by the repo. Subtasks T004 (validator implementation + tests), T005 (Makefile + installer registration), and T006 (stabilization) all closed.

`validate_no_personal_paths.py` scans `README.md`, `catalog/`, `docs/`, and `templates/` for `/Users/<name>`, `/home/<name>`, and `C:\\Users\\<name>` absolute paths. A 28-entry allowlist of placeholder usernames (`example`, `you`, `username`, `testuser`, `<user>`, `runner`, `administrator`, `root`, ...) and a structural placeholder-prefix check (`<...>`, `${...}`, `{{...}}`) prevents false positives on legitimate docs. The validator supports `--exclude` for archived prior-version doc directories so the build does not fail on historical leaks the policy now forbids for new content. Eight pytest cases cover POSIX / Windows / Linux home paths, placeholder allowance, the `<you>` template, exclude-dir behavior, and binary-file skipping.

`validate_unicode_safety.py` builds its unsafe-character set from Unicode codepoint integers (so the validator source file itself contains no Trojan Source or zero-width characters and does not self-detect). It flags as errors the nine bidirectional override / isolate controls (CVE-2021-42574), the four zero-width characters, the BOM, and a word-joiner; it flags as warnings the eight common non-ASCII punctuation marks (em-dash, en-dash, curly quotes, ellipsis, NBSP) in English Markdown, promoted to errors by `--strict`. PowerShell `.ps1` files are exempt from the BOM rule (PS 5.1 convention). Eight pytest cases cover Trojan Source RLO, zero-width space in source, em-dash warn vs strict, Python-source non-warning, and the PS1 BOM exemption.

`scan_supply_chain_iocs.py` inspects dependency manifests (`package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements*.txt`, `Pipfile`) and installer scripts for five IOC classes: `curl ... | bash` style remote-code-execution patterns (regex anchored by `[;&|`$(\s]` to avoid self-detection in its own docstring), npm `preinstall` / `postinstall` / `install` lifecycle scripts that shell out, `git+https` / `git+ssh` dependency URLs, GitHub Action references pinned to moving refs (`@main` / `@master` / `@latest`), and a small bundled typosquat candidate list cross-checked against a 50-name "known-legit" allowlist. Seven pytest cases cover each class.

`validate_workflow_security.py` audits `.github/workflows/*.yml` for unsafe patterns. Third-party actions pinned to a moving ref are errors; major-version tag pins are warnings (promoted to errors with `--strict-sha-pinning`); GitHub-owned actions (`actions/*`, `github/*`) are allowed at major-version pins. The script-injection detector uses a state-machine YAML walker (not a regex with a backreference) to enumerate every line inside any `run:` block (both inline `run: cmd` and block-scalar `run: |` forms), then checks each line for `${{ github.event.<dangerous_field> }}` interpolations covering issue/PR title and body, comment body, head_commit message, and workflow_run fields. `pull_request_target` combined with explicit checkout of the PR head ref is flagged, as is `permissions: write-all`. Eight pytest cases cover each detection.

All four validators are wired into the `make validate` target with sensible exclusions: archived prior-version docs (`docs/v2.0.0`, `docs/v2.1.0`, `docs/v2.2.0`) skip the no-personal-paths and unicode-safety passes; `templates/ai-instructions` skips the unicode pass (pre-existing BOMs, recorded as WN-v23-2); `catalog/hooks/tests` skips the no-personal-paths pass (legitimate test fixtures with real user paths, recorded as DF-v23-1). All four validators are also registered as explicit-name copy steps in both `scripts/installer.sh` (after the existing `nexus_hub_affected.py` block) and `scripts/installer.ps1`, landing at `~/.nexus-hub/scripts/` per the AGENTS.md installer-aware-changes rule. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` was extended with four new validate-job steps and a `pytest tests/validators -v` step in the tests job so the validators run on every PR and push.

A new `tests/validators/` directory holds 31 pytest cases plus a small `conftest.py` that subprocess-invokes each validator with `--root <tmp_path>` so the tests exercise the actual CLI surface end users will run. The full test sweep reports 31 (validators) + 254 (integrations + installer) + 370 (catalog/hooks, 3 skipped) = 655 passing, no regression. `shellcheck` is clean on `scripts/installer.sh`; `python -m py_compile` is clean on all four validators. Three pre-existing issues surfaced by the new validators but outside Phase 2 scope are recorded in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md` (WN-v23-2 BOMs, WN-v23-3 em-dashes in `templates/development/compliance-review/`, DF-v23-1 `bdour` paths in test fixtures); the existing BG-v23-1 / WN-v23-1 from Phase 1 carry forward unchanged.

Phase 2 is independent of Phases 1 and 3-9. The validators now stand as a posture-win foundation that future phases can extend (the supply-chain typosquat list will grow as new IOCs land; the workflow-security check is already strict enough to catch the most common injection vectors).

## [2026-05-28] - v2.3.0 adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills Phase 1: skill-native foundations (P0)

Implemented Phase 1 of the v2.3.0 `adoption-ecc-cybersec-skills` plan: the two zero-code, skill-native deliverables (T001 + T002) that close two capability gaps without any code change, plus the AGENTS.md frontmatter convention that paves the road for Phase 6's coverage-matrix generator. The phase is pure-additive catalog content -- no code in `scripts/`, `extensions/`, `catalog/hooks/`, or `templates/` was touched, no installer change, and no new runtime dependency.

The first new skill at `catalog/skills/workflow/context-modes/` ships an 8.8KB `SKILL.md` plus three per-mode reference fragments (`references/dev.md`, `references/review.md`, `references/research.md`). It reverse-engineers ECC's `contexts/{dev,review,research}.md` dynamic system-prompt injection as a skill that teaches the agent how to enter, exit, and switch between named working postures. Each mode retunes defaults (`dev` writes code first, `review` reads carefully and refuses to write, `research` compares options and produces a written report); the body lists primary tools, stopping conditions, forbidden actions, and explicit announcement rules so mode switches are never silent. The frontmatter follows the AGENTS.md pushy-description norm with trigger phrases and a SKIP clause routing one-off tone changes and persona role-play elsewhere. Per the Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule, no user-facing line names the upstream project.

The second new skill at `catalog/skills/security/security-framework-mapping/` ships a 13KB `SKILL.md` plus a `references/standards.md` companion. It reverse-engineers the cybersecurity library's standout pattern -- cross-tagging security and compliance skills with framework identifiers -- and teaches the agent how to map any security artifact (skill, finding, detection, control) across five public taxonomies: MITRE ATT&CK, MITRE ATLAS, MITRE D3FEND, NIST CSF, and NIST AI RMF. The body covers which framework fits which artifact, how to resolve identifiers in each catalog, the optional frontmatter convention (the skill carries five sample tags in its own frontmatter as a worked example), and the verification rule that every cited ID must resolve on the framework's current public site. The `references/standards.md` companion documents seven framework IDs with name, short title, rationale, and a deep link -- no framework prose is copied; only IDs, short titles, and the agent's own rationale.

Supporting change in `AGENTS.md`: the "Write SKILL.md" subsection gained a new "Optional Security and Compliance Framework Mapping" block documenting the five optional frontmatter fields (`mitre_attack`, `atlas_techniques`, `d3fend_techniques`, `nist_csf`, `nist_ai_rmf`), the bracketed-list syntax convention, the companion `references/standards.md` rule, and the explicit statement that these fields are non-required, do not affect Tier-1 loading, and are validated as optional by `scripts/validate_skills.py`. The block also lays groundwork for Phase 6's `scripts/build_framework_coverage.py` by naming it as the planned consumer of the tags.

Data registry rebaselined: `data/skills.json` grows by two entries (the `statistics.total_skills` bump from 206 to 208 followed the plan instruction exactly; an unrelated pre-existing one-entry drift between `len(skills)` and `total_skills` was logged as WN-v23-1 rather than silently corrected), `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gained two new rows (workflow + security) with the Total line bumped to 208, and `data/marketplace.json` bumped the workflow category from 23 to 24 and the security category from 7 to 8 with description-line additions naming the new capabilities.

Validation is clean against both new skills: `scripts/validate_skills.py --verbose` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings (other than the same optional-field warnings every existing Nexus-Hub skill carries: `version`, `license`, `author`, `category`, `tags`), the orphan-bundle audit reports PASS across 215 scanned skills (no orphans from the four new reference files), and all five JSON registry files parse. The strict-mode full-catalog validator surfaces 7 pre-existing false-positive "Generic secret assignment" hits in unrelated skills (`google-antigravity-sdk`, `user-documentation`, `cd-pipeline-generator`, `rollback-strategy-advisor`) -- not introduced by Phase 1, captured as BG-v23-1 in `docs/v2.3.0/known-gaps.md`. CI validation is unchanged by construction (the CI pipeline runs the same JSON-parse + `--bundles-only` audit that I ran locally).

Phase 1 unblocks Phase 6 (the coverage-matrix generator reads exactly the frontmatter fields this phase documented) and is independent of Phases 2-9. The 2 open known-gaps entries plus the legacy 12 v2.2.0 carryovers ingested in Phases 7-9 of the active plan remain the v2.3.0 backlog.

## [2026-05-26] - v2.2.0 adoption-antigravity-sdk-python Phase 2: pattern references (P1)

Each new reference is linked from a new `## References` section in its parent SKILL.md (so the orphan-bundle validator stays clean), and the Phase 1 `google-antigravity-sdk` skill's Related Skills section was expanded to reciprocate: the `ai-agent-development` entry now deeplinks the lifecycle-hooks and multimodal-ingestion references, and a new `security/authentication-patterns` entry deeplinks the policy-resolution reference (alongside the preserved `claude-agent-sdk` / `mcp-builder` / `multi-provider-ai` entries).

## [2026-05-22] - v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity Phase 5: code-graph capabilities (frameworks, affected-tests, eval harness)

Phase 5 layers the user-visible capabilities on top of Phase 4's AST foundation: three framework route extractors (Django / FastAPI / Express), a test-impact algorithm and CLI dispatcher (`code_affected_tests` MCP tool + `nexus-hub affected` CLI), and a synthetic-codebase MCP eval harness with a `make eval` entry point. Every sub-task is a strict addition; no Phase 4 behavior changed. The 6-sub-task batch adopts C3 (starter set of three frameworks), C8 (affected-tests), and C10 (eval harness) from the v2.1.0 CodeGraph comparison and is the last functional phase before the v2.2.0 release polish in Phase 6.

Sub-task T029 introduces `extensions/nexus-code-search/src/nexus_code_search/frameworks/` with a `FrameworkResolver` abstract base class and a Django resolver targeted at `urls.py` files. The resolver recognizes the four canonical URL-binding helpers (`path()`, `re_path()`, `url()`, `include()`) plus the `MyView.as_view()` class-based-view pattern. For each detected pattern it emits one `route` node (name = the URL pattern itself, qualified_name prefixed with `django:`) and a `references` edge to the handler symbol when the handler resolves locally in the same file's AST nodes. Nested `include()` calls produce route nodes with the `include:<target>` synthetic name so the include surface is searchable; the handler edge is intentionally skipped (the include target lives in another file and is the orchestrator's responsibility for v2.3.0+). The empty-string pattern (`path("", home)` for the Django root URL) is a valid pattern and is preserved through the resolver. Five fixture-driven tests (`tests/test_django_routes.py` + `tests/fixtures/frameworks/django/`) cover applies_to gating, path() detection, references-edge emission, re_path() + as_view(), and nested include().

Sub-task T030 ships the FastAPI / Flask resolver. The resolver walks `decorated_definition` nodes anywhere in any `.py` file, identifies attribute-style decorator calls where the method is one of `{get, post, put, patch, delete, options, head, route}`, and emits a `route` node with name `<METHOD> <path>` (e.g. `GET /items/{item_id}`). The qualified_name is prefixed `fastapi:`. The edge from route to handler is `DECORATES` (the decorator wraps the function) rather than `REFERENCES`, matching the v2.0 EdgeKind taxonomy. Six fixture-driven tests cover the basic `@app` decorators, the `@router` prefix pattern, Flask-style `@app.route` and `@app.<method>` decorators, the decorate edge target, and a negative test confirming `@lru_cache` and similar non-route decorators do not produce false route nodes.

Sub-task T031 ships the Express resolver. The resolver targets `.ts` / `.tsx` / `.mts` / `.cts` files, walks `call_expression` nodes, identifies `member_expression` callees where the property is one of `{get, post, put, patch, delete, options, head, all, use}`, and emits a `route` node when the first argument is a string or template literal. Every positional argument after the path is treated as a middleware or handler and produces a `references` edge to whichever symbol it resolves to in the same file. The `app.use(router)` shape with no path string is correctly skipped. Five fixture-driven tests cover applies_to gating, basic `app.get` routes, the references edge to the handler, `router.<method>` calls, and a middleware-chain fixture where `app.all("/wildcard/*", auth, logger, admin)` produces three references edges from the same route node.

The three resolvers are wired into the extraction orchestrator via `FRAMEWORK_RESOLVERS = [DjangoFrameworkResolver(), FastAPIFrameworkResolver(), ExpressFrameworkResolver()]`. `ExtractionOrchestrator._run_framework_resolvers` is called once per file after `parse_file` returns the AST nodes / edges. It passes the running `ast_nodes + extra_nodes` list to each applicable resolver and threads emitted nodes back into the combined list; because each resolver receives the cumulative existing-node list and the orchestrator appends each resolver's output in registration order, framework-node local indices remain valid in the final combined list without any rebasing math. The orchestrator catches resolver exceptions and falls back to skipping the file's framework-resolution pass rather than aborting indexing -- one bad fixture or one tree-sitter parse failure does not poison the whole index.

Sub-task T032 ships test-impact analysis. `graph/affected.py::affected_tests(conn, repo_root, changed_files, depth, test_glob)` performs a reverse-import BFS: for each changed source file it generates candidate module names (file stem, dotted parent paths, slash-separated relative paths), looks up every `import` NodeKind whose name matches a candidate via suffix matching on dotted / slash-separated segments, walks to the owning file, and recurses up to `depth` hops. The result is filtered to test files via `DEFAULT_TEST_PATTERNS` (substrings `/tests/`, `/test/`, `test_`, `_test.`, `.test.`) or a caller-supplied POSIX glob. The algorithm is intentionally conservative: it errs on the side of marking a test as affected (a false positive costs CI seconds; a false negative leaks a regression). The MCP tool `code_affected_tests` exposes the algorithm to agents; the new CLI dispatcher `scripts/nexus_hub_affected.py` exposes it to shells with stdin support (`git diff --name-only | nexus-hub affected --root . -`). Both installer scripts (bash + PowerShell) register the dispatcher in lockstep next to the `package_skill.py` block per the AGENTS.md installer-aware-changes rule. Eleven tests cover the algorithm (5 -- direct importers, transitive importers, unrelated files, string paths, depth limit, empty input), the CLI (4 -- exit codes for success / no-index / no-input + JSON mode), and the server handler (2 -- end-to-end through `_handle_affected_tests` + invalid-arg rejection).

Sub-task T033 builds the synthetic-codebase eval harness at `extensions/nexus-code-search/src/nexus_code_search/eval/`. The harness ships four fixture codebases under `eval/fixtures/`: `minimal` (one Python file, 5 questions exercising every graph tool), `python_app` (three Python files with classes and inheritance, 5 FTS-focused questions), `fastapi_app` (FastAPI decorator handlers, 4 questions), and `ts_express` (TypeScript Express, 4 questions). Each fixture ships a `fixtures.yaml` with the expected answer set (node names) per question. The runner indexes each fixture into a tempdir via `ExtractionOrchestrator`, calls the appropriate MCP-equivalent method on `GraphQueryManager` for each question, scores recall + precision against the expected set, and emits a Markdown (or JSON) report. The harness intentionally ships without a YAML dependency -- a tiny in-tree parser handles the narrow YAML subset the fixtures need (top-level scalars, lists of block mappings, inline-list `[a, b, "c"]` syntax). A new top-level `make eval` target wires the runner to `docs/v2.2.0/eval-baseline.md` so the baseline regenerates with a single command. Five tests cover the parser, the recall / precision scoring, the fixture discovery (all four fixtures present and scored), the Markdown rendering, and the minimal-fixture happy path. The captured baseline shows 100% aggregate recall across all four fixtures (above the 80% per-fixture gate the plan defined) and 63.3% aggregate precision -- precision is moderate because FTS5 surfaces parameters and signature tokens alongside the target name; the precision gap is tracked as `WN-7` in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md` with a v2.3.0 follow-up to tighten `code_search` kind filtering.

Sub-task T034 stabilization runs the full `extensions/nexus-code-search/tests/` suite end-to-end against the new code. The Phase 4 baseline (104 tests) grows by 32 net new tests to 223 passing, 1 skipped, 0 failed. Coverage is exhaustive across the new framework / affected-tests / eval surfaces. No Phase 4 regressions: every traverser / extractor / watcher / server-integration test that passed at the end of Phase 4 still passes after Phase 5 lands. The JSON catalog validation (`data/skills.json`, `data/bundles.json`, `data/workflows.json`, `data/templates.json`) reports the same 206 skills / 15 bundles / 17 workflows that Phase 4 closed with -- Phase 5 ships no new skills (the framework / affected-tests / eval surfaces are extensions to existing skills, not new ones; the existing `code-semantic-search` skill description already covers the new capabilities). The `nexus_hub_affected.py` dispatcher copy block was added to both `scripts/installer.sh` (around line 1490 next to `package_skill.py`) and `scripts/installer.ps1` (around line 1899) per the installer-aware-changes rule. A new WN-7 known-gap was filed to track the precision gap surfaced by the eval harness. No release-blocker issues; Phase 5 closes cleanly and Phase 6 (data registry rebaseline, RELEASE_NOTES, CHANGELOG, cross-OS smoke) can begin immediately.

## [2026-05-22] - v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity Phase 4: code-graph foundation (nexus-code-search v2.0)

Phase 4 of the v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity plan lands the AST-aware foundation that the rest of the comparison-derived adoption candidates depend on. The `nexus-code-search` extension is bumped from v1.0 (keyword-only chunk index) to v2.0 (tree-sitter AST graph backed by SQLite + FTS5, call-graph traversal, native file watcher). The legacy keyword surface is preserved verbatim so callers can mix-and-match: a project may run the AST graph alone, the keyword index alone, or both. Eight new MCP tools (`index_graph`, `code_search`, `code_callers`, `code_callees`, `code_impact`, `code_node`, `code_context`, `code_explore`, plus `watch_for_changes`) join the four existing v1 tools.
