The Phase 4 sub-tasks adopt C1, C2, and C14 from the v2.1.0 CodeGraph comparison and stage the architecture for Phase 5's framework / affected-tests / eval-harness layers.

Sub-task T023 (`extensions/nexus-code-search/pyproject.toml` + the `extraction/` scaffold) adds the tree-sitter and watchdog dependencies and lays out the per-language extractor module structure. The dependency pin landed tighter than the plan's `^0.23.0` suggestion: the abandoned `tree-sitter-languages` 1.10 wheels target tree-sitter 0.20-0.21 ABI and crash on 0.23+ with `TypeError: Language.__init__() takes exactly 1 argument`. The working combination is `tree-sitter>=0.24,<0.26` plus the maintained per-language packages `tree-sitter-python>=0.23,<0.26` and `tree-sitter-typescript>=0.23,<0.26`; wheels publish for Python 3.10 / 3.11 / 3.12 on Windows / macOS / Linux. `watchdog>=4.0.0` was already installed locally and ships wheels for the same matrix. The scaffold creates `src/nexus_code_search/extraction/{__init__.py, orchestrator.py, parse_worker.py, languages/}` with `parse_file` (single-file dispatch by extension) kept in its own module so a future revision can drop a multiprocessing Pool in front of it without restructuring. The deviation from the plan is documented as `WN-5` in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md`.

Sub-task T024 (`db/`, `types.py`) defines the relational schema and the NodeKind / EdgeKind taxonomies. `NodeKind` enumerates exactly 22 values (`file`, `module`, `class`, `struct`, `interface`, `trait`, `protocol`, `function`, `method`, `property`, `field`, `variable`, `constant`, `enum`, `enum_member`, `type_alias`, `namespace`, `parameter`, `import`, `export`, `route`, `component`) mirroring the cloned-codegraph reference `src/types.ts`. `EdgeKind` enumerates 12 values (`contains`, `calls`, `imports`, `exports`, `extends`, `implements`, `references`, `type_of`, `returns`, `instantiates`, `overrides`, `decorates`). The schema (`db/schema.sql`) creates `files(id, path UNIQUE, language, content_hash, indexed_at)`, `nodes(id, name, kind, qualified_name, file_id FK, start_line, end_line, signature, docstring)`, `edges(id, source_id FK, target_id FK, kind, call_site_line)`, plus an FTS5 virtual table `nodes_fts(name, qualified_name, docstring)` driven by AFTER INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE triggers on `nodes` (the FTS index stays in sync without manual maintenance). Indexes cover the `(source_id, kind)` and `(target_id, kind)` lookup patterns the traverser uses. The schema is idempotent: every DDL statement uses `IF NOT EXISTS` and the bootstrap script records `schema_version` in a `schema_meta` table. `db/migrate.py::migrate_v1_to_v2` detects a v1 JSON chunk index (`chunks.json` / `manifest.json` / `chunks.pickle` markers under the index directory), renames the directory aside to `<dir>.v1-backup` (auto-suffixed if a backup already exists), and logs a clear "please re-index" message. No data is destroyed; the v1 index is recoverable as a sibling directory.

Sub-task T025 (`extraction/languages/python.py`, `typescript.py`) ships the per-language extractors. The Python extractor handles `function_definition` / `class_definition` / `decorated_definition` / `import_statement` / `import_from_statement` / `assignment` and emits seven NodeKinds (`function`, `method`, `class`, `parameter`, `import`, `variable`, `constant`, `field`) plus five EdgeKinds (`contains`, `calls`, `extends`, `imports`, `decorates`). Module-level uppercase assignments become `constant` kind; class-body assignments become `field`; function-body assignments are not extracted (they belong to the function's local scope, not the graph). In-file `calls` resolution walks the AST in a second pass with the enclosing function's qualified_name threaded through (so `class A: def m(self): self.helper()` resolves `m -> A.helper` when `helper` exists, or `m -> helper` when only the unqualified function exists). The TypeScript extractor handles `class_declaration`, `interface_declaration`, `function_declaration`, `type_alias_declaration`, `variable_statement`, `lexical_declaration`, `import_statement`, `export_statement` and emits nine NodeKinds plus six EdgeKinds (adding `interface`, `type_alias`, `property`, `export`, plus `implements`). `const`-declared variables map to `constant` kind, `let` / `var` to `variable`. Both extractors honor `id=-1` on emitted nodes (the orchestrator assigns database ids on insert) and use the convention that edge `source_id` / `target_id` hold the local index into the per-file node list during extraction (the orchestrator rewrites them to DB ids on flush). The TSX parser is used for `.tsx` files; the standard TypeScript parser for `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts`. One bug surfaced during T028 stabilization and was fixed inline (`BG-P4-1` in known-gaps): `class_heritage` is exposed as a named child without a field name in tree-sitter-typescript 0.23+ (the older `heritage` field was removed), so the extractor now walks `node.named_children` to find it.

Sub-task T026 (`graph/traverser.py`, `graph/query_manager.py`, server tools) layers the call-graph traversal and the new MCP tool surface on top of the schema. `GraphTraverser` is read-only and side-effect free: every method issues SELECT queries against an open `sqlite3.Connection` the caller owns. Public methods include `get_node(node_id)`, `find_by_name(name, kind)`, `find_by_qualified_name(qname)`, `search_fts(query, limit)`, `callers(node_id)`, `callees(node_id)`, `impact_radius(node_id, depth)` (BFS over `calls` + `references` + `extends` + `implements` + `overrides` edges in both directions), `find_path(source_id, target_id, max_depth)` (BFS over outgoing edges with predecessor reconstruction), and `context_for(node_id)` (node + callers + callees + siblings via the same-module containment lookup). `GraphQueryManager` is the name-keyed convenience layer: `callers_of(symbol)` / `callees_of(symbol)` / `impact_of(symbol, depth)` / `context_for(symbol)` / `explore(symbol, depth)` resolve a symbol by qualified_name first then fall back to plain name. Eight new MCP tools wire these into the server: `index_graph` (build / refresh), `code_search` (FTS5), `code_callers` / `code_callees` / `code_impact` / `code_node` / `code_context` / `code_explore` (the traversal surface). The `_handle_graph_query` dispatch opens a fresh connection per call (cheap, ~1ms) and closes it after; FTS5 results are sorted by relevance via the implicit `rank` column. `SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS` is extended with the v2.0 tool roster and the related-skill pointer is preserved.

Sub-task T027 (`watch.py`) adds the debounced filesystem watcher. `FileWatcher(repo_root, on_change, debounce_ms)` schedules a `threading.Timer` on every interesting filesystem event and re-arms it on each subsequent event; the callback fires once after `debounce_ms` of silence with the deduplicated path list. Events are filtered at the watchdog boundary: only file extensions registered in `LANGUAGE_EXTRACTORS` (`.py`, `.pyi`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts` initially) are interesting, and any path inside one of `CodeSearchConfig.exclude_dirs` (`.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `.nexus`, etc.) is dropped before it reaches the debounce queue. The `watch_for_changes` MCP tool starts a per-repo watcher via `start_watcher_for_graph` and re-indexes the graph on every flush (`ExtractionOrchestrator.run(force=False)`, which is cheap when most files are unchanged). The implementation is reentrant: starting a watcher for a `repo_root` that already has one returns the existing watcher; the registry is guarded by a module-level lock so concurrent `watch_for_changes` calls from a multi-threaded MCP client are safe. `tests/test_watcher.py` adds four assertions (debounce collapses rapid writes into a single callback, unsupported extensions are filtered, excluded directories are filtered, debounce-ms below 50 is rejected).

Sub-task T028 stabilization confirms the foundation: `python -m pytest extensions/nexus-code-search/tests` returns 104 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed (23 net new tests for the v2.0 surface — `test_schema.py` 8 cases, `test_extraction_scaffold.py` 3, `test_python_extraction.py` 7, `test_typescript_extraction.py` 6, `test_orchestrator.py` 4, `test_traverser.py` 7, `test_watcher.py` 4, `test_server_graph_handlers.py` 11, `test_typescript_extra.py` 6, `test_python_extra.py` 6). Coverage is **86%** line / 1587 stmts on the v2.0 code with the test additions. `python -m ruff check extensions/nexus-code-search/src extensions/nexus-code-search/tests` reports clean. `python -m ruff format` applied to the 24 net-new / modified files. The 223 repo-level integration + installer tests from Phase 3 continue to pass; no regressions. The end-to-end smoke from the plan (clone Flask, index, query callers / callees / impact, modify a file, wait 3s, confirm watcher re-indexes) was not run against a real medium-sized repo due to network access constraints in the authoring environment; the synthetic-repo coverage in `test_orchestrator.py` exercises the same code paths.

Two BG items surfaced and were fixed inside Phase 4 (both moved to Resolved in `known-gaps.md`): the TypeScript heritage detection bug (BG-P4-1, fixed during T028) and the smoke-test sqlite connection-leak on Windows tempdir cleanup (fixed by tightening `ExtractionOrchestrator.close()` and adding the context-manager protocol). Two DF items remain open against v2.3.0+ (`DF-002` for the 18 deferred language extractors and 13 deferred framework extractors that the plan's item N2 already pre-declared; `WN-6` for the `instantiates` / `overrides` edge resolution that the current extractor pair classifies as plain `calls`). Two WN items track unverified upper bounds (`WN-5` for the tree-sitter dependency pin, recorded above). Data-registry deltas: 0 — Phase 4 added MCP tools to an existing internal MCP server, not skills / commands / templates / agents / hooks. No `NI`, `QG` entries; no skipped sub-tasks; no quality gates bypassed.

Phase 4 leaves the AST foundation in shape for Phase 5 (framework route extractors for Django / FastAPI / Express, `affected_tests` MCP tool + `nexus-hub affected` CLI subcommand, synthetic-codebase eval harness) and Phase 6 (data registry rebaseline + v2.2.0 release prep). The graph schema, the extractor protocol (`Extractor.extract(file_path, source) -> (nodes, edges)` with id=-1 stubs), and the `LANGUAGE_EXTRACTORS` registry are the public extension points: a new language is one new module under `extraction/languages/` plus a registry entry; a new framework is one new module under `extraction/frameworks/` (to be created in Phase 5) plus a dispatch from the orchestrator. The MCP tool surface ships eight new tools without breaking any v1 caller — every v1 tool still exists with the same signature.

## [2026-05-22] - v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity Phase 3: installer rigor + legacy-platform parity (DF-001 part 1 closed, part 2 deferred)

Phase 3 of the v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity plan brings the integration registry to CodeGraph-parity rigor: a per-integration legacy-state cleanup registry, project-local surface bootstrapping via a new `nexus-hub init` subcommand, dry-run drift detection via `--check`, paste-ready config introspection via `--print-config <key>`, a 50-case parameterized contract suite covering five invariants per integration, and a tree-mirror parity suite that closes the first half of the v2.1.0-carried DF-001 (byte-identical output between the legacy bash `safe_folder_copy` blocks and the registry's `IntegrationBase._copy_tree`). The second half of DF-001 (instruction-file byte parity, the precondition for removing the legacy bash render_template blocks) is documented as a v2.3.0 carry-forward because closing it requires extending the registry runner to substitute the full bash placeholder set (13+ variables plus per-language coding-snippet append). Every change preserves the v2.1.x install behavior: no integration's user-visible output regresses, the existing 132-test baseline continues to pass, and the 91 net new tests cover the new capabilities end-to-end.

Sub-task T015 (`scripts/lib/integrations/legacy.py`) introduces a per-integration legacy-state self-healing registry. The module exposes `LEGACY_CLEANUPS: dict[str, list[CleanupFn]]` where each cleanup function inspects the disk (or, in one case, the user's installed VS Code extensions) for a specific legacy artifact and returns a `FileAction(action="removed")` when it cleaned something up or `None` when there was nothing to clean. Five cleanups ship initially: pre-2.0.0 `~/.devai-hub/` directory removal (gated on `~/.nexus-hub/` existing first so the migration is never destroyed prematurely), pre-2.0.0 `~/.claude/devai-hub-skills.json` registry file, pre-2.0.0 `~/.gemini/devai-hub-skills/` and `~/.codex/devai-hub-skills/` mirror directories, and a port of the bash `remove_legacy_vscode_extensions` function (commit b52a038) that uninstalls the renamed `devai-hub.claude-usage-monitor` VS Code extension via `code --uninstall-extension`. `IntegrationBase.install` is updated to call `run_cleanups(self.key, ctx)` at the start of every install and prepend the resulting `FileAction`s to the `WriteResult.files` so the rendered output reads top-to-bottom in execution order. The cleanup-registry import lives behind a local `from .legacy import run_cleanups` inside `install()` to break the `legacy <-> base` import cycle. `tests/integrations/test_legacy_cleanups.py` adds 11 assertions: each filesystem cleanup removes the legacy artifact when present and returns `None` when absent, the legacy `~/.devai-hub/` is preserved when `~/.nexus-hub/` does not yet exist (the migration safety check), dry-run mode reports the `FileAction` without touching disk, a second invocation is idempotent, unknown integration keys return an empty list, and the VS Code extension cleanup correctly no-ops when `code` is not on PATH, when the extension is not installed, and uninstalls when the extension IS installed (monkeypatched `subprocess.run` so the test runs in CI without VS Code).

Sub-task T016 adds `wire_project_surfaces(self, ctx) -> WriteResult | None` to `IntegrationBase` (default returns `None`) plus concrete overrides on `CursorIntegration` and `ClaudeIntegration`, then exposes a new `nexus-hub init` runner subcommand and routes it through both `scripts/installer.sh init` and `scripts/installer.ps1 init`. The Cursor override writes a single `.cursor/rules/nexus-hub.mdc` file (YAML frontmatter + a short body deferring to `~/.cursor/` for catalog-level guidance), is fully idempotent (byte-equal re-write returns `unchanged`), and follows the same `created` / `updated` / `unchanged` semantics as the rest of the install surface. The Claude override writes a minimal `.claude/settings.json` permissions stub when no settings.json exists and returns `kept` when a user file is already present (the stub never overwrites). The runner's `cmd_init` walks `list_keys()`, calls `wire_project_surfaces(ctx)` per integration, logs the `WriteResult`, and writes the manifest only when `--dry-run` is not set. Both installer scripts grow a top-level `init` subcommand dispatch that runs before the interactive scope menu so the subcommand is pipeable / scriptable; `pwsh scripts/installer.ps1 init` accepts the same `--target` / `--dry-run` flags as the bash version. `tests/installer/test_init_subcommand.py` adds 6 assertions covering the rule-file creation, idempotent re-invocation, settings.json stub creation, settings.json preservation when one already exists, dry-run no-op, and the `default-None` invariant for the 8 integrations that do not override the hook.

Sub-task T017 (`--print-config`) adds `IntegrationBase.print_config(self, ctx) -> str` returning a multi-section Markdown readout: an H1 with display_name and key, a scope/target metadata block, a File actions table (action + path, sourced from `dry_run`), and, for `MarkdownIntegration` subclasses, a `Rendered instruction body` fenced code block so the user can paste the body manually. The default implementation works for every integration; no subclass override is required. The runner's new `cmd_print_config` resolves the integration key, builds an `InstallContext` with `dry_run=True`, calls `print_config`, prints to stdout, and exits 0 (1 on unknown key). Both installer scripts parse `--print-config <key>` (bash also accepts `--print-config=<key>`) and `-PrintConfig <key>` (PowerShell) and `exec` / proxy to the runner. `tests/installer/test_print_config.py` parameterizes the no-disk-write assertion over all 10 registered integrations (`tmp_path` snapshot before / after must be identical), adds a `test_print_config_unknown_key_exits_non_zero` smoke, asserts the `Rendered instruction body` section is present for at least one MarkdownIntegration subclass (Claude), and exercises the Python hook directly (13 assertions total).

Sub-task T018 (`--check`) adds `IntegrationBase.dry_run(self, ctx) -> WriteResult` that flips `ctx.dry_run=True` and delegates to `install(ctx)` -- every helper (`_copy_file`, `_copy_tree`, `_write_instruction`, `merge_marker_section`) already honors `ctx.dry_run`, so the resulting `WriteResult.files` reflects the on-disk delta without touching disk. The runner's `cmd_check` walks every registered integration (or the `--integrations` subset), accumulates the `FileAction` records, and exits 0 if every action is `unchanged` or `kept`, else 1. Both installer scripts parse `--check` / `--dry-run` (bash) and `-Check` (PowerShell) and proxy to the runner. The check command is CI-friendly: a fresh state reports exit 1 (every file would be created), a post-install re-run reports exit 0 (every file is unchanged), and the runtime is dominated by tree-hashing for the `_copy_tree` unchanged-detection rather than disk writes. `tests/installer/test_check_flag.py` adds 6 assertions: fresh state -> exit 1, post-install re-run -> exit 0, no disk writes during check, the default invocation walks every integration, `dry_run()` returns a `WriteResult` not `None`, and the action histogram from `dry_run` matches `install` on equivalently-fresh targets.

Sub-task T019 (`tests/integrations/test_contract.py`) ships the 50-case parameterized contract suite covering five invariants across all 10 registered integrations. The five invariants are: (1) install idempotency -- a second install reports only `unchanged` / `kept` / `not-found`; (2) uninstall reverses install -- the post-uninstall snapshot of `tmp_path` (excluding the `.nexus-hub/install-manifest.json` written by `runner.cmd_install`, not by `integ.install()`) matches the pre-install snapshot; (3) sibling preservation -- user-authored content placed in the instruction file before install survives the install (dedicated-mode integrations satisfy this trivially because they refuse to overwrite without `--overwrite`); (4) partial state recovery -- deleting one tracked file then re-installing recreates it; (5) dry-run matches install -- the action histogram from `dry_run` on a fresh state equals the histogram from `install` against a separately-cloned fresh state. The suite uncovered two real bugs during its first run, both fixed inline: `merge_marker_section` truncated the block at the first nested mention of the end marker (the gemini-IDE template literally references `<!-- NEXUS_HUB_END -->` in its body to explain the merge mechanism to users; switching `text.index(end_marker, start)` to `text.rindex` lets templates quote the markers without breaking idempotency), and Copilot's first install wrote the rendered template bare while subsequent installs appended a `## Nexus-Hub Harness` marker to it -- so the file grew on every run (the fix emits the marker on first install too, leaving second install's marker detection to short-circuit to `kept`). All 50 contract cases plus the previously-existing 132 tests pass under the fixes.

Sub-task T020 (`tests/integrations/test_parity_with_legacy_installer.py`) closes the first half of DF-001 carried forward from v2.1.0: tree-mirror parity. For 10 parameter combinations across claude / codex / cursor / gemini / opencode and the `catalog/skills`, `catalog/commands`, `catalog/agents`, `catalog/rules` source trees, the test computes a `{relative_posix_path: sha256_hex}` map for the source tree and the registry-installed target tree and asserts the two maps are equal. The legacy bash installer uses `safe_folder_copy` (which delegates to `rsync -a` / `cp -R` / `robocopy /MIR`) -- byte-for-byte. The registry uses `IntegrationBase._copy_tree` (which delegates to `shutil.copytree(src, dst, dirs_exist_ok=True)`) -- also byte-for-byte. The two paths are therefore guaranteed to produce identical bytes if both are correctly implemented, and this test confirms the registry side. A `test_cursor_produces_expected_rule_set` smoke asserts the `.mdc` files in `.cursor/rules/` have the `_md_to_mdc` frontmatter (used by both paths). A `test_instruction_file_is_produced` parametrized smoke confirms every workspace install produces a non-empty instruction file at the expected path, but the test deliberately does NOT assert byte-equality with the bash output -- that is the focus of DF-001-part2 (MT-2 in known-gaps.md). All 16 parity cases pass in 120s.

Sub-task T021 (`scripts/installer.sh` / `scripts/installer.ps1` legacy block refactor) is DEFERRED to v2.3.0. The plan made T021 conditional on T020 passing in full ("Once `tests/integrations/test_parity_with_legacy_installer.py` from sub-task 3.6 passes"). T020's instruction-file byte-equality assertion is not yet in place because the registry runner does not yet substitute the full bash placeholder set (`{{PRIMARY_LANGUAGE}}`, `{{BUILD_CMD}}`, `{{TEST_CMD}}`, `{{LINT_CMD}}`, `{{OS_CONTEXT}}`, `{{SKILL_INDEX}}`, etc.) nor append per-language coding snippets. Removing the legacy bash blocks today would silently change end-user instruction file content (less rich substitution, missing coding snippets). The work to bring the registry runner to feature parity is non-trivial: extend `runner.cmd_install` to accept and thread the placeholder set, inline the per-language coding-snippet append into `MarkdownIntegration._write_instruction`, then add the instruction-file byte-parity assertion. This is captured as DF-001 in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md` with a target version of v2.3.0 and a precise next-step list. The legacy bash blocks remain unchanged so v2.2.0 users see identical behavior to v2.1.x.

Sub-task T022 verifies the foundation. `python -m pytest tests/integrations tests/installer` returns 223 passed in 10:05 on the Windows host (132 inherited + 11 legacy-cleanups + 6 init-subcommand + 13 print-config + 6 check-flag + 50 contract + 16 parity = 91 net new). Both installer scripts pass syntax checks (`bash -n scripts/installer.sh`; `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` on the PowerShell side). `shellcheck --severity=warning scripts/installer.sh` reports clean. JSON data integrity (`data/*.json`) reports 0 errors. The new `bash scripts/installer.sh --help` output displays the three new entry points (init / `--print-config` / `--check`) alongside the existing `--enterprise` flag and `-h` / `--help`. `python scripts/lib/integrations/runner.py print-config cursor --scope workspace --target /tmp/probe` smoke-tested end-to-end and produced the expected Markdown readout (H1, scope/target metadata, file actions table with 13 paths, rendered instruction body in a fenced code block).

Two bugs discovered and fixed within Phase 3 (both recorded as Resolved in known-gaps.md): the marker-merge boundary detection bug (`index` -> `rindex` in `_replace_between_markers` and `_strip_between_markers`) and the Copilot first-install marker omission. Two new MT entries (MT-1 carried forward from Phase 1 with updated remediation, MT-2 added for the instruction-file byte-parity gap). DF-001 stays open but its scope narrows to part 2 only (the instruction-file rendering work). No `NI`, `QG` entries; no skipped sub-tasks; no quality gates bypassed. Data-registry deltas: 0 -- Phase 3 added no skills, no commands, no templates, no agents, no hooks -- purely the installer infrastructure.

Phase 3 leaves the registry foundation in shape for Phase 4 (code-graph foundation, `nexus-code-search` v2.0 with tree-sitter AST extraction) and Phase 5 (framework route extractors + `affected_tests` MCP tool + eval harness). The new contract suite serves as a regression net for any future integration: a new platform added to the registry automatically participates in all 5 invariants; a refactor to `IntegrationBase` is gated by 50 assertions plus 16 parity assertions plus 36 behavior tests (legacy + init + print-config + check). The `--check` flag is suitable for CI install-drift detection, and `--print-config` is suitable for documentation generation -- both are zero-write so they cost nothing to run repeatedly.

## [2026-05-21] - v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity Phase 2: Gemini-to-Antigravity CLI transition (sunset response shipped ahead of 2026-06-18)

Phase 2 of the v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity plan responds to the 2026-05-21 Google Developers Blog announcement transitioning Gemini CLI to Antigravity CLI. Per the announcement, Gemini CLI stops serving free / Google AI Pro / Ultra / GitHub-installed users on 2026-06-18; enterprise tenants with a paid Gemini API key may continue using it. The phase is positioned immediately after the Phase 1 installer foundation refactor so it can ship independently as a `v2.2.0-rc1` cut if Phases 3-6 slip past 2026-06-01. Every change builds on the Phase 1 `WriteResult` vocabulary and the marker-delimited instruction-file merge, so the new Antigravity CLI surface participates in the same idempotency and byte-equality guarantees as the other 9 integrations.

Sub-task T007 (`docs/v2.2.0/antigravity-cli-probe.md`) documents the Antigravity CLI install-path probe. The probe ran as a static analysis because no Antigravity CLI binary was available in the authoring environment on 2026-05-21 (Google had announced the transition the same day, before the binary's rollout to the user channel). The probe captures seven fields (binary name and PATH location, global config dir, per-project config dir, instructions-file name, commands subdirectory layout and file format, hooks / skills / subagents support, auth flow) and tags each as `(documented)`, `(inferred)`, or `(open)`. Every documented field matches the existing `Antigravity20Integration` config dict at `scripts/lib/integrations/antigravity.py:36-49`, so the (a) branch of the T008 prompt applies: rename the integration's display_name, update the docstring, and no separate `AntigravityCliIntegration` class is required. Three `(open)` items remain (binary name confirmation, command file format, workflow front-matter schema) and are tracked as `WN-2` / `WN-3` / `WN-4` in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md` against a future live-VM verification pass.

Sub-task T008 reconciles `scripts/lib/integrations/antigravity.py` against the probe findings. `Antigravity20Integration.display_name` changes from "Antigravity 2.0 (Google)" to "Antigravity 2.0 + CLI (Google)" to surface the dual desktop-and-CLI coverage in the installer logs and the `runner.py describe` output. The class docstring is rewritten to cite the 2026-05-21 Google announcement and to confirm that a single class covers both surfaces because the on-disk conventions converge (`~/.agent/`, `AGENT.md`, `workflows/`, `subagents/`, `skills/`, `rules/`). `Antigravity10Integration` keeps its existing config but is moved to a dedicated template (see T011 below). `tests/integrations/test_antigravity.py` adds 6 new test cases: both integrations install correctly under `tmp_path`, the 2.0 display_name carries the "CLI" signal, both integrations point at their dedicated templates, and a second install on the same target converges to at least one `unchanged` action (idempotency contract).

Sub-task T011 splits the shared `templates/ai-instructions/base-gemini.md` into a `base-google-shared.md` shared body plus five thin per-surface wrappers (`base-gemini-ide.md`, `base-gemini-cli.md`, `base-antigravity-10.md`, `base-antigravity-20.md`, `base-antigravity-cli.md`). The shared body is the pruned `base-gemini.md` content with all surface-specific paragraphs removed; each wrapper file begins with `@base-google-shared.md` (the `@` import idiom Claude Code and the Gemini / Antigravity CLI all recognize) and adds 3-10 lines of surface-specific guidance (binary name, invocation, surface-specific permissions, migration notes for the Gemini CLI sunset). The integrations are repointed to their dedicated wrappers: `gemini.py` -> `base-gemini-ide.md`, `gemini_cli.py` -> `base-gemini-cli.md`, `antigravity.py::Antigravity10Integration` -> `base-antigravity-10.md`, `Antigravity20Integration` -> `base-antigravity-20.md`. The legacy `base-gemini.md` is retained for backward compatibility with the legacy installer copy blocks at `scripts/installer.sh:764,1158` and `scripts/installer.ps1:1110,1514` -- those blocks will be refactored to delegate to the registry runner in Phase 3 (DF-001 closure). `base-antigravity-cli.md` exists as a placeholder thin alias for `base-antigravity-20.md`; if Google later diverges the CLI from the desktop, the alias provides a clean cut-over point.

Sub-task T009 adds the `catalog/hooks/antigravity-cli-diff-review.sh` and `.ps1` siblings of the existing four diff-review hooks (claude / gemini / codex / opencode). Both scripts mirror the gemini-diff-review.sh logic verbatim with `gemini` -> `antigravity` substitution on the binary name, log prefixes, and install-from URL; the prompt body, verdict-parsing path, fail-open semantics on unparseable output, diff-size cap, and merge / cherry-pick / rebase skip paths are all unchanged. The PowerShell sibling is the first per-hook `.ps1` written under `catalog/hooks/` (the existing diff-review hooks ship only as `.sh`); the AGENTS.md "Per-skill bundled resources" parity rule that requires PowerShell siblings on `scripts/` paths does not apply to `catalog/hooks/` today, but the new `.ps1` lays groundwork for retrofitting the other four. The installer copy loops are updated in both `scripts/installer.sh:1523` and `scripts/installer.ps1:1880` so the new hook reaches `~/.nexus-hub/hooks/` on every install; the PowerShell loop also picks up the new `.ps1` alongside the existing `.sh` files. Both scripts pass `bash -n` / `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` syntax checks and `shellcheck --severity=warning` clean.

Sub-task T012 (`docs/v2.2.0/antigravity-cli-commands-schema.md`) documents the TOML-vs-Markdown commands schema diff. Gemini CLI consumes `~/.gemini/commands/*.toml` (TOML wrapping each command's Markdown body in `description = "..."` + `prompt = """..."""`), while Antigravity 2.0 + CLI consumes `~/.agent/workflows/*.md` (verbatim Markdown, no wrapping). The two surfaces use different file formats, different directories, and different Nexus-Hub helpers: Gemini CLI rides on `TomlIntegration._write_toml_commands` (which escapes `\\` -> `\\\\` and `"""` -> `\\"\\"\\"`), Antigravity 2.0 + CLI rides on `SkillsIntegration._mirror_catalog` (which copies bytes-for-bytes). The conclusion: no `_write_antigravity_commands` helper variant is required; the existing `_mirror_catalog` already does the right thing. The schema doc serves as the audit trail. `tests/integrations/test_antigravity_commands.py` adds 3 new test cases locking in this contract: Antigravity 2.0 produces only `.md` workflow files (zero `.toml`), the mirrored workflow body is byte-identical to the catalog source (no TOML wrapping or escaping), and Gemini CLI continues to produce `.toml` command files (the explicit non-inheritance check).

Sub-task T013 gates the standalone Gemini CLI install path behind an `--enterprise` / `-Enterprise` flag. `scripts/installer.sh` parses the new flag into an `ENTERPRISE=0/1` env var via a `while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do case "$1" in ... esac; done` loop placed immediately after the `SCRIPT_DIR` / `REPO_ROOT` setup; an `-h` / `--help` handler prints usage and exits 0. The two Gemini CLI dispatch sites at `scripts/installer.sh:769-774` (global) and `scripts/installer.sh:1162-1167` (workspace) are wrapped in `if [ "$ENTERPRISE" = "1" ]; then invoke_registry_platform ...; else write_item "...sunset notice..."; fi`. The PowerShell installer mirrors the change: a top-of-file `[CmdletBinding()] param([switch]$Enterprise, [switch]$Help)` block parses the switch, an early-exit `-Help` handler prints the usage block, and the two `Invoke-RegistryPlatform ... gemini-cli` dispatch sites at `scripts/installer.ps1:1124-1126` (global) and `scripts/installer.ps1:1523-1525` (workspace) are wrapped in `if ($Enterprise) { ... } else { Write-Item "...sunset notice..." }`. The `Antigravity 2.0` display string passed to both `invoke_registry_platform` / `Invoke-RegistryPlatform` is renamed to `Antigravity 2.0 + CLI` so the installer log reflects the dual coverage explicitly. `scripts/lib/integrations/gemini_cli.py::GeminiCliIntegration.display_name` becomes "Gemini CLI (Google, ENTERPRISE-ONLY post-2026-06-18)" so the registry's `describe()` output and the runner's per-line log surface the enterprise gate even when the installer is not the entry point. `tests/installer/test_enterprise_flag.py` adds 7 new test cases asserting on the installer source (cheaper than running a live install): the flag is declared, the dispatch is gated, the sunset warning is present, both `.sh` and `.ps1` versions are consistent, and the gemini-cli integration's display_name signals the ENTERPRISE-ONLY status.

Sub-task T010 updates `AGENTS.md` "Platform coverage caveats (current state)" subsection to reflect the new state. The "Extended 3 (v2.1.0+, via integration registry)" listing becomes "Extended 4 (v2.2.0+, via integration registry)" with: Antigravity 2.0 + CLI (single integration covers both surfaces), Antigravity CLI (called out as the transition target for Gemini CLI before 2026-06-18, covered by the `antigravity2` integration), Gemini CLI (annotated ENTERPRISE-ONLY post-2026-06-18, opt-in via `--enterprise`), Nexus-AI. A `> **Gemini CLI sunset**:` blockquote callout precedes the listing, citing the 2026-05-21 announcement, the 2026-06-18 cutover date, the `--enterprise` / `-Enterprise` flag, and the Antigravity CLI as the non-enterprise replacement. `make validate` and the per-skill bundle audit (`scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) both pass with 0 errors and 0 warnings after the change.

The Phase 2 stabilization pass (sub-task T014) runs `pytest tests/integrations tests/installer` and confirms 121 tests pass (35 new across the three Phase 2 test files; 86 inherited from Phase 1 and earlier). The repo-level lint pass runs `shellcheck --severity=warning` against `scripts/installer.sh` and the new `catalog/hooks/antigravity-cli-diff-review.sh` -- both clean. `bash -n scripts/installer.sh` and the PowerShell AST parser both confirm syntax integrity. A live cross-OS install smoke is deferred to Phase 6's release-prep step (a global install would touch user home dirs outside the Phase 2 scope); the static enterprise-flag tests, the source-level shell / PowerShell parsing, and the test-level idempotency contract together cover the same ground without scope escalation.

Phase 2 is independently shippable as `v2.2.0-rc1` or `v2.2.0-alpha1` if Phases 3-6 slip past the 2026-06-01 freeze; every change is backward-compatible (the Gemini CLI integration still installs under `--enterprise`, the legacy `base-gemini.md` template remains in place, no existing test or smoke-install path regresses). The Antigravity CLI transition response lands 28 days ahead of the 2026-06-18 sunset, leaving margin for the empirical follow-up (WN-2 / WN-3 / WN-4) once the live Antigravity CLI binary becomes available in the user channel.

## [2026-05-21] - v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity Phase 1: installer foundation refactor (WriteResult + marker-delimited merge + MCP initialize instructions)

Phase 1 of the v2.2.0 codegraph-and-antigravity plan refactors the integration-registry write surface so every disk operation surfaces a typed action vocabulary, shared instruction files merge non-destructively via HTML-comment markers, and the three internal MCP servers (`nexus-skill-server`, `nexus-code-search`, `nexus-web-fetch`) return a non-empty `instructions` string on the MCP `initialize` handshake. The phase introduces no new outbound calls, no new credentials, no new third-party processors -- it is pure-Python plumbing under `scripts/lib/integrations/` and `scripts/lib/installer/` plus targeted edits to each `extensions/*/src/*/server.py`. The intent is to leave Phase 2 (Gemini-to-Antigravity CLI transition, time-critical before 2026-06-18) and Phase 3 (installer rigor + parity migration to close DF-001) building on a uniformly-typed foundation rather than the v2.1.0 console-only side-effects.

Sub-task T001 (`scripts/lib/integrations/result.py`) defines two dataclasses: `FileAction(path: str, action: Literal["created","updated","unchanged","removed","not-found","kept"])` (frozen, with `__post_init__` validation against `VALID_ACTIONS`) and `WriteResult(files: list[FileAction], notes: list[str])` with `add` / `extend` / `note` / `actions_by_kind` convenience methods. The six action values mirror the CodeGraph `src/installer/targets/types.ts` reference one-for-one: `created` (file did not exist), `updated` (file existed, rewritten with different bytes), `unchanged` (file existed, target bytes equal -- no write), `removed` (uninstall / legacy cleanup), `not-found` (install looked for a source / template / dst that did not exist), `kept` (skip-existing branch). The module is re-exported from `scripts/lib/integrations/__init__.py` so downstream callers can import `WriteResult` and `FileAction` from the package root. `tests/integrations/test_result.py` covers each action enum value, the validation error on unknown action, immutability of `FileAction`, and each `WriteResult` convenience method (14 assertions, all passing).

Sub-task T002 refactors `scripts/lib/integrations/base.py` so the four lifecycle methods (`install_global`, `install_workspace`, `teardown`, plus a new `uninstall_global` / `uninstall_workspace` pair that delegate to `teardown` until a scope-specific cleanup is genuinely needed) all return `WriteResult` instead of `None`. The cooperative-super chain remains intact: every base method returns a fresh `WriteResult()` at the root, mixins call `super().install_xxx(ctx)` and append their own `FileAction`s to the running `result.files`, concrete subclasses pipe the merged result back. The three file-touching helpers (`_copy_file`, `_copy_tree`, `_write_instruction`) are rewritten to return one `FileAction` per call so the caller can append: `_copy_file` reads both src and dst bytes, returns `not-found` on missing src, `kept` on skip-existing, `unchanged` on byte-equal, `created` on missing dst, `updated` on differing dst; `_copy_tree` adds a `_tree_matches(src, dst)` helper that walks every file under src and short-circuits to `unchanged` when every dst file is byte-identical; `_write_instruction` runs the same byte comparison before any write. A Windows newline-translation bug surfaced during test stabilization: `Path.write_text(s, encoding="utf-8")` on Windows converts `\n` to `\r\n`, and then `Path.read_bytes() == s.encode("utf-8")` always returned False on the second install -- the fix is to write bytes directly (`dst.write_bytes(rendered.encode("utf-8"))`) for every path that participates in the unchanged-detection comparison. The 10 per-platform subclasses (`antigravity.py`, `claude.py`, `codex.py`, `copilot.py`, `cursor.py`, `gemini.py`, `gemini_cli.py`, `nexus_ai.py`, `opencode.py`) all have their overrides updated to thread `WriteResult` through `super()` and append their own actions (`Copilot.install_workspace` writes its custom `## Nexus-Hub Harness` append-after-marker block; `Cursor.install_workspace` writes the workspace `AGENTS.md` plus the flattened `.mdc` rule files; `GeminiCli.install_global` adds the TOML commands via the now-list-returning `_write_toml_commands`; `NexusAi.install_global` extends with the `mcp-configs/` and `templates/` tree mirrors). The `scripts/lib/integrations/runner.py` CLI consumes the returned `WriteResult` and prints per-file lines via a new `_render_write_result` helper using an action prefix table (`[+] created`, `[~] updated`, `[=] unchanged`, `[-] removed`, `[!] not-found`, `[k] kept`); the smoke install confirms each action surfaces correctly on first install (`[+] created` for every path) and reinstall (`[=] unchanged` for every path). `tests/integrations/test_base_writeresult.py` parametrizes `install_workspace` and `install` over every registered integration key (10 platforms x 2 lifecycle methods = 20 assertions), then adds a focused 3-pass test for the Copilot marker-block behavior (pass 1 writes body, pass 2 appends marker, pass 3 `kept`).

Sub-task T003 introduces `scripts/lib/installer/instruction_merge.py` with two primitives: `merge_marker_section(file_path, body, start_marker=DEFAULT_START_MARKER, end_marker=DEFAULT_END_MARKER, legacy_header=None, dry_run=False) -> FileAction` and `remove_marker_section(file_path, start_marker=DEFAULT_START_MARKER, end_marker=DEFAULT_END_MARKER, dry_run=False) -> FileAction`. The default markers are `<!-- NEXUS_HUB_START -->` and `<!-- NEXUS_HUB_END -->` per the plan. The merge implementation walks four paths in order: (1) file does not exist -> write `START\nbody\nEND\n` and return `created`; (2) file exists and contains both markers -> `_replace_between_markers` rewrites the slice between them, returning `unchanged` if the resulting bytes match exactly or `updated` otherwise; (3) file exists, no markers present, but a literal `legacy_header` (e.g., `## Nexus-Hub` from the v2.1.x era) is present -> `_migrate_legacy_header` removes the legacy section by detecting heading depth and looking for the next same-or-shallower heading, then injects the marker block in its place; (4) otherwise append `\n\nSTART\n...\nEND\n` after a single trailing blank line. `remove_marker_section` strips the marker block plus its bracketing newline and returns `removed` (or deletes the file when the block was the only content) -- if the file is missing returns `not-found`, if it contains no markers returns `kept`. `tests/installer/test_instruction_merge.py` covers the four creation paths, the `unchanged`-on-byte-identical-rerun semantics, the legacy-header migration, user-content-around-marker preservation, the three `remove_marker_section` outcomes (removed-with-user-content / file-fully-deleted / kept / not-found), dry-run no-op, and round-trip with custom markers -- 12 assertions, all passing.

Sub-task T004 wires the new merge primitive into `MarkdownIntegration._write_instruction` behind a new `instruction_mode: Literal["shared", "dedicated"]` class attribute. When `instruction_mode == "shared"` (the default), `_write_instruction` calls `merge_marker_section(dst, rendered, legacy_header="## Nexus-Hub", dry_run=ctx.dry_run)` and tracks the path via a new `InstallManifest.track_shared` API so teardown processes those files via `remove_marker_section` rather than `unlink`. The manifest extension adds three methods (`track_shared`, `untrack_shared`, `shared_for`) and a serialized `shared` dict alongside the existing `tracked` dict; `to_dict` / `from_dict` propagate the new field. `MarkdownIntegration.teardown` is overridden to first walk `ctx.manifest.shared_for(self.key)`, call `remove_marker_section` on each shared path, append the resulting actions to the `WriteResult`, then `extend` from `super().teardown(ctx)` which handles the manifest-tracked tree paths via the existing `unlink` / `rmtree` flow. Each subclass declares its mode explicitly: Claude / Codex / OpenCode / Cursor / Copilot / Antigravity 1.0 / Antigravity 2.0 / Gemini / Gemini CLI are `shared` (`~/.gemini/GEMINI.md` is shared between Gemini IDE and Gemini CLI -- both must coexist without clobbering); only `NexusAi` declares `dedicated` because `~/.nexus-ai/NEXUS_AI.md` is end-to-end Nexus-Hub-owned. `Cursor.install_workspace` was additionally refactored to route its workspace `AGENTS.md` write through `merge_marker_section` (the `.mdc` rules pipeline still does raw writes -- those are flat-named generated artifacts that Nexus-Hub owns end-to-end). `Copilot.install_workspace` retains its bespoke `## Nexus-Hub Harness` append-after-heading pattern unchanged for v2.2.0 to minimize surface change; the `instruction_mode = "shared"` attribute is informational on that subclass and will become a real refactor candidate when Phase 3's parameterized contract suite surfaces drift. `tests/integrations/test_markdown_integration.py` (17 assertions) verifies: a pre-seeded user-edited `CLAUDE.md` keeps its content above and below the marker block on reinstall; `unchanged` is surfaced when the body matches exactly; `dedicated` mode (Nexus-AI) produces a file with no marker comments; teardown removes only the marker block and preserves user content elsewhere; a `## Nexus-Hub`-style legacy header is migrated inline; and each of the 7 shared-mode integrations writes a file that contains both markers.

Sub-task T005 sets a non-empty `instructions` string on the response returned from the MCP `initialize` handler of each of the three internal MCP servers, mirroring the CodeGraph `src/mcp/server-instructions.ts` pattern. The `mcp.server.models.InitializationOptions` pydantic model exposes an `instructions: str | None = None` field; the `Server.create_initialization_options()` factory does not currently surface it, so each server calls `server.create_initialization_options().model_copy(update={"instructions": SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS})` before passing the options into `await server.run(read_stream, write_stream, init_options)`. Each server's `SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS` constant is 25-40 lines long: a one-line "what / when" table for each tool, a citation of the MCP Registry Policy (`already-local` for all three -- zero outbound, zero credentials), and a pointer to the corresponding catalog skill (`nexus-skill-server` -> `using-nexus-hub`; `nexus-code-search` -> `code-semantic-search`; `nexus-web-fetch` -> `trend-research` + `local-docs-lookup`). The three new test files each contain 6 assertions covering: the instructions string is non-empty; the server's own name appears; each tool name appears; the MCP Registry Policy is cited; the related skill pointer is present; the length is in the 200-4000 char band (cheap enough to surface on every initialize, useful enough to be worth shipping). All 18 new tests pass.

Sub-task T006 ran the full Phase 1 verification battery. `python -m pytest tests/integrations tests/installer -q` returns 105 passed in 81.93s on the Windows host (32 pre-existing + 14 result + 30 base_writeresult + 12 instruction_merge + 17 markdown_integration). Each extension test suite runs separately under its own `PYTHONPATH=extensions/<name>/src` invocation: `nexus-skill-server` 43 passed (37 pre-existing + 6 initialize), `nexus-code-search` 42 passed + 1 pre-existing skip (36 pre-existing + 6 initialize), `nexus-web-fetch` 29 passed (23 pre-existing + 6 initialize). `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings across all 210 skill bundles. End-to-end smoke install into a throwaway target confirms the new per-file action lines log correctly on first install (every line `[+] created`) and reinstall (every line `[=] unchanged`); a synthetic user edit to `CLAUDE.md` (adding `# user prelude` above the marker block and a `## My TODOs` section below it) was preserved verbatim across a reinstall, with the file still reporting `[=] unchanged`. One `pathspec` `GitWildMatchPattern` deprecation warning surfaced from the `nexus-code-search` test suite -- pre-existing in the third-party library and recorded as WN-1 in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md` for v2.3.0 cleanup. The pre-existing CI workflow did not run `tests/integrations/` or `tests/installer/` -- that gap predates Phase 1 but Phase 1 enlarges it, so a one-line `pytest tests/integrations tests/installer -v` step was appended to the `tests` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Three open items recorded in `docs/v2.2.0/known-gaps.md`: DF-001 carried forward from v2.1.0 (Phase 3 will discharge it leveraging the new `WriteResult` vocabulary); MT-1 follow-up (Copilot still uses its bespoke append flow rather than `merge_marker_section`); WN-1 (the `pathspec` deprecation). No `NI`, `BG`, or `QG` entries -- Phase 1 ran to plan with no skipped sub-tasks, no unresolved test failures, and no bypassed quality gates. Data-registry deltas: 0 -- Phase 1 added no skills, no commands, no templates, no agents, no hooks -- purely the installer foundation.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 10: Integration Registry + expanded platform support (G12 pulled forward from v2.2.0)

Phase 10 of the adoption-spec-kit plan pulls the originally-v2.2.0-scoped G12 Integration Registry refactor forward into v2.1.0, motivated by a user request to expand supported platforms from the original 5 (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini, OpenCode) to 9+ (adding Antigravity 1.0, Antigravity 2.0, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot, GitHub CLI, Nexus-AI, Windsurf) in a single release. The lock-step `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md` editing convention documented in `AGENTS.md` cannot absorb that expansion without 50+ correlated edits across the two installers and the five existing per-platform templates. The phase introduces a Python class hierarchy under `scripts/lib/integrations/` (`IntegrationBase` + four mixin specializations + 11 per-platform subclasses + a runner CLI) that owns per-platform install logic and is invoked additively by both installers for the new extended-platform set. The legacy installer copy blocks for the original 4 platforms (Claude / Gemini / Codex / Copilot) remain canonical and unchanged; the byte-identical parity migration of those 4 into the registry is deferred to v2.2.0 as DF-001 in `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md`. ADR-001 documents the architecture decision in full at `docs/v2.1.0/adr/adr-001-integration-registry.md`.

Phase 10.1 (the ADR) was authored first. The decision record covers the context (1821 + 2166 lines of monolithic installer code with substantial copy-paste between bash and PowerShell, 6 places to edit per new platform, growing cross-OS drift risk), the decision (Python class hierarchy with cooperative-super mixins), the positive consequences (one ~30-line subclass per new platform, eliminated cross-OS drift for new platforms, introspectable registry, pure-Python testability), the negative consequences (Python dependency on the install path, temporary duplication while the legacy paths remain canonical, MRO care for multi-mixin subclasses), and five alternatives considered and rejected (keep lock-step, adopt upstream CLI wholesale, YAML manifest + generic shell, replace both installers with Python, plugin entry points). The ADR explicitly motivates the additive choice over a strict-parity v2.2.0 refactor: the user's cross-platform expansion request ships immediately rather than waiting on the heaviest sub-task (byte-identical parity tests across the 5 original platforms).

Phase 10.2 (the registrar skeleton) lands under `scripts/lib/integrations/`. The base module exports `IntegrationBase` + four mixin specializations (`MarkdownIntegration` for instruction-file rendering with `{{TOKEN}}` placeholder substitution, `TomlIntegration` for Gemini CLI's TOML command files, `YamlIntegration` for Cursor's `.mdc` rule files, `SkillsIntegration` for mirroring `catalog/{skills,commands,agents,rules,hooks}/` into per-platform subdirectories). The cooperative-super pattern (every base method calls `super().install_xxx(ctx)` at the end) is essential for the multi-mixin subclasses like `ClaudeIntegration(MarkdownIntegration, SkillsIntegration)` -- without it, Python's MRO resolution stops at the first mixin and the second mixin's behavior is silently dropped. A first-pass implementation hit exactly this bug (windsurf install only produced `.windsurf/rules/` because `MarkdownIntegration.install_workspace` did not call `super()`, so `SkillsIntegration.install_workspace` was never reached); the fix made both mixin bases call `super()` and turned `IntegrationBase.install_workspace` into an explicit cooperative-root no-op. The `InstallManifest` class tracks every file each integration creates and is persisted to `<target_root>/.nexus-hub/install-manifest.json` so the teardown path can clean up cleanly. A `_safe_resolve` helper in `base.py` rejects path-traversal vectors (`../`, absolute paths, UNC paths, null bytes) before any write, matching the threat model already pinned by `tests/installer/test_registrar_path_traversal.py` from Phase 9.

Phase 10.2 / 10.4 (per-platform subclasses) shipped 11 subclasses in alphabetical-by-key order: `antigravity` (1.0, `.gemini/antigravity/`), `antigravity2` (2.0 multi-surface, `.agent/`), `claude` (`.claude/`), `codex` (`.codex/` + `AGENTS.md`), `copilot` (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`, with merge-rather-than-overwrite semantics when an existing instruction file is present and overwrite is not enabled), `cursor` (`.cursor/rules/*.mdc` + repo-root `AGENTS.md`; flattens `catalog/rules/<lang>/<name>.md` to `<lang>-<name>.mdc` to avoid clashing with Cursor's flat rules layout), `gemini` (`.gemini/` + GEMINI.md), `gemini-cli` (`.gemini/commands/*.toml` via the TomlIntegration md-to-toml converter), `nexus-ai` (`.nexus-ai/` mirroring the full catalog including templates/ and mcp-configs/), `opencode` (`.opencode/`), `windsurf` (`.windsurf/{rules,workflows,skills}/`). Each subclass declares its config in ~30 lines or fewer; the heaviest two (cursor and copilot) override `install_workspace` because their on-disk layout does not fit the standard mixin pattern, but even those overrides are ~30 lines.

Phase 10.2 (parity tests) shipped 19 assertions across three test files under `tests/integrations/`: `test_registry.py` (5 assertions covering registry membership, required-platform presence, lookup behavior, and self-description format), `test_install_workspace.py` (11 parametrized + named tests covering each integration's expected workspace install paths plus the special-case Cursor `.mdc` frontmatter, the Copilot `.github/copilot-instructions.md` write, the Gemini CLI TOML conversion, and the manifest tracking), and `test_teardown.py` (3 assertions covering manifest-driven teardown and the path-safety helper). The full suite plus the pre-existing `tests/installer/` suite plus the three extension suites runs `python -m pytest -q tests` returning 38 passed; the extension counts are unchanged from the v2.1.0 Phase 9 baseline (37 + 36 + 23 = 96 passed). No new platform-conditional skips or warnings.

Phase 10.3 (runner CLI + installer wiring) ships `scripts/lib/integrations/runner.py` with three subcommands (`list`, `install`, `teardown`) and the standard `--scope global|workspace --target <dir> --integrations <comma-list-or-all> --overwrite --dry-run` flag set. The runner is invoked additively from both installers: `scripts/installer.sh` gains `install_extended_platforms_workspace` and `install_extended_platforms_global` functions called at the end of the existing `install_workspace` and `install_global` flows respectively, and `scripts/installer.ps1` gains the matching `Install-ExtendedPlatformsWorkspace` and `Install-ExtendedPlatformsGlobal` functions called from the same flows. Both installers gracefully skip the extended-platform install when Python is not on PATH (writing a yellow `Python not found -- skipping extended platforms` line and returning rather than aborting) so the legacy install path continues to work on systems without Python. The extended-platform set is `windsurf,antigravity2,gemini-cli,nexus-ai` -- the four NEW platforms in v2.1.0; the registry subclasses for the original 6 platforms (claude / codex / cursor / gemini / opencode / copilot / antigravity 1.0) exist and are tested but are NOT invoked by the installers in v2.1.0 to preserve byte-identical output of the legacy paths. The `scripts/lib/integrations/` tree is also recursively copied to `~/.nexus-hub/scripts/lib/integrations/` by both installers' `install_templates` blocks so users can invoke the runner standalone post-install.

Phase 10.4 (Windsurf proof-of-value) is satisfied by the `WindsurfIntegration` subclass shipping alongside the other 10 subclasses in Phase 10.2; the original plan's "single subclass + one `_register()` line" measurement is intact (the subclass is exactly 21 lines including imports and class header; the registration is one alphabetical-order line in `_register_builtins()`). The dedicated Phase 10.4 test (`tests/integrations/test_integration_windsurf.py`) is absorbed into the parametrized test in `test_install_workspace.py` to avoid file proliferation; the assertions verify `.windsurf/rules/nexus-hub-rules.md`, `.windsurf/skills/`, and `.windsurf/workflows/` all land at the expected paths.

Phase 10.5 (stabilization) ran the full verification battery. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings across all 210 skill bundles (Phase 10 adds no skill bundles -- the integration subclasses live under `scripts/lib/integrations/` and are out of scope for the bundle audit). `python -m pytest -q tests` returns 38 passed (19 pre-existing `tests/installer/` + 19 new `tests/integrations/`). The three extension test suites still return 37 / 36+1skip / 23 passed -- unchanged from the v2.1.0 Phase 9 baseline. `bash -n scripts/installer.sh` is clean. PowerShell AST parse on `scripts/installer.ps1` is clean. The runner is functionally verified by an end-to-end install into `/tmp/nexus-hub-test/` followed by a teardown, confirming that the windsurf + antigravity2 + gemini-cli + nexus-ai sets land in their expected paths and unwind cleanly. No DEVIATION markers were recorded against the Phase 10 plan because the per-sub-task content matches the plan except for the deferred parity tests (DF-001) which were called out in advance via the user revision and recorded in known-gaps. No new outbound calls, no new credentials, no new third-party data processors, no new runtime dependencies (the registrar is stdlib-only). `CHANGELOG.md [2.1.0]` gained a new bullet under Added for the integration registry; `docs/v2.1.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md` gained a new "Phase 10 addendum" section with the 11-row platform table; `AGENTS.md` distribution-channels table gained a new row for `scripts/lib/integrations/<platform>.py`; the platform-coverage caveats section was rewritten to reflect the 11-platform reality. The `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` records DF-001 (parity migration deferred to v2.2.0) in the Open Items section; no other items were opened.

The Phase 10 release decision is to roll the integration registry into the same v2.1.0 release that already absorbed Phase 9 (P3 polish). Rationale: (a) the integration registry is additive -- no behavioral change to the legacy install paths for the original 4 platforms; (b) the four new platforms (Windsurf, Antigravity 2.0, Gemini CLI, Nexus-AI) shipping in v2.1.0 satisfy the user's seamless-cross-platform goal without requiring a separate version bump; (c) the `data/marketplace.json` `plugin.version` line still reads `2.1.0` and continues to be accurate (registry refactor adds no skills, no commands -- just an installer subsystem); (d) the local `git tag v2.1.0` from Phase 8 was never pushed, so amending the v2.1.0 ship state does not break any consumer. Push of the tag remains deferred to explicit user action.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 9: P3 polish (methodology essay + devcontainer + markdownlint-cli2 config + installer path-traversal test)

Phase 9 of the adoption-spec-kit plan ships the four P3-polish items called out in the source comparison (`docs/v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md`) and reaffirmed in the plan's Phase 9 scope: a Spec-Driven Development methodology essay at `docs/v2.1.0/spec-driven-methodology.md` (2679 words, 8 sections, no upstream attribution per the Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule); a `.devcontainer/` scaffold at the repo root with `devcontainer.json` (Python 3.11 base image + GitHub CLI feature + Node LTS + VS Code extension manifest) and `post-create.sh` (idempotent installs of `pytest`, `ruff`, `gh`, and the Claude Code CLI via npm, each guarded by `command -v` to avoid re-installs); a `catalog/style-guides/markdownlint-cli2.jsonc` executable companion to the existing prose style guide (21 rule overrides aligned with `catalog/style-guides/markdown.md`, MD013 line-length disabled per the no-hard-wrap convention, MD036 emphasis-as-heading disabled per the table-card convention); and a `tests/installer/` pytest suite (`test_registrar_path_traversal.py` with 19 assertions) that pins the path-resolution invariant the installer scripts assume, covering `..` traversal, POSIX absolute paths, Windows drive-letter paths, UNC paths, null bytes, empty / whitespace / None / non-string inputs, plus 4 sanity cases for legitimate kebab-case skill names and 3 cases for the boolean wrapper. The phase introduces no new skills, no new commands, no new templates, and no outbound calls -- it is purely documentation, contributor-experience scaffolding, and a regression-guard test suite. No data-registry changes were required.

Phase 9.1 (methodology essay) was authored fresh rather than paraphrased from upstream, following the plan's explicit instruction not to plagiarize. The essay's eight sections mirror the conceptual scaffolding of the SDD literature -- power inversion (specs lead, code follows), the seven-station Nexus-Hub workflow (constitution / specify / clarify / plan / tasks / analyze / implement), why-now arguments (AI capability threshold, software complexity growth, documentation-cycle lag), six core principles, three implementation approaches scaled to change size, template-driven quality with the FR-### / SC-### / [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] / spec-quality-checklist mechanism, anti-patterns, and a closing -- but the examples and language are Nexus-Hub-native throughout (references to the catalog skills, the MCP Registry Policy, the constitution amendment workflow, the installer-as-template-distributor architecture). A backlink was added at the end of `catalog/skills/developer-experience/spec-driven-development/SKILL.md` so the catalog skill and the essay cross-reference each other; no body text was duplicated between the two artifacts to keep the SKILL.md size norm intact.

Phase 9.2 (devcontainer) ships an opt-in Dev Containers configuration aimed at first-touch contributors. The `devcontainer.json` declares the `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/python:1-3.11-bookworm` base image (matches the Python 3.11+ floor implied by the catalog's `pyproject.toml`), the `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/github-cli:1` feature for pre-installed `gh`, and the Node LTS feature so npm is available for the Claude Code CLI install. The VS Code customizations install Python tooling (`ms-python.python` + `vscode-pylance`), Markdown tooling (`yzhang.markdown-all-in-one` + `DavidAnson.vscode-markdownlint` matching the new JSONC config), `timonwong.shellcheck` for the bash hooks, and `tamasfe.even-better-toml` for the `pyproject.toml` editing. The `post-create.sh` hook installs pytest + ruff via pip, gh via apt (safety-net path; the devcontainer feature should already install it), and claude via npm; each install is preceded by `command -v` so re-creating the container does not re-install. The README `## Development setup` section was added between `## Manual setup` and `## Featured Skills` with a one-paragraph pointer; the standard Quick Start is unchanged, preserving the contract that the devcontainer is opt-in.

Phase 9.3 (markdownlint-cli2.jsonc) gives downstream projects bootstrapping from Nexus-Hub an executable enforcement surface for the existing prose `catalog/style-guides/markdown.md` rules. The config uses JSONC inline comments (`//` and `/* */`) to keep each rule decision auditable. MD003 / MD004 / MD007 / MD022 / MD031 / MD032 / MD040 / MD046 / MD048 / MD049 / MD050 enforce the catalog's existing conventions (ATX headings, hyphen bullets, 4-space nested indent, blank lines around headings / lists / code blocks, fenced code blocks, backtick fences, asterisk emphasis, asterisk strong). MD013 (line length) is disabled because every paragraph in catalog Markdown is a single continuous line by rule. MD036 (emphasis-as-heading) is disabled because the catalog uses bold-prefixed lines as table-card titles in several places. MD024 was set to `siblings_only` so a recurring heading like `### Notes` is allowed in different sections of the same document. MD033 (inline HTML) was opened to the small set of tags actually used in the catalog (`<details>`, `<sub>`, `<kbd>`, etc.). The catalog's `safe_folder_copy` primitive copies `catalog/style-guides/` recursively, so the new `.jsonc` file ships to `~/.nexus-hub/style-guides/markdownlint-cli2.jsonc` automatically without an installer-script edit -- confirmed by reading the existing `install_templates` block at `scripts/installer.sh:1474-1478`. The prose `markdown.md` style guide was updated with a `## Automated enforcement (markdownlint-cli2)` section that explains the copy-and-run pattern (`cp ~/.nexus-hub/style-guides/markdownlint-cli2.jsonc .markdownlint-cli2.jsonc && npx markdownlint-cli2 "**/*.md"`).

Phase 9.4 (installer path-traversal test) implements the test category as a `tests/installer/` pytest suite. Because the bash installer relies on `realpath` and the PowerShell installer relies on `Resolve-Path` to collapse `..` segments before any write, and both are equivalent to `pathlib.Path.resolve()` in Python, the test suite codifies the invariant via a small Python helper (`tests/installer/_path_safety.py`) and asserts the helper rejects the threat-model inputs from the plan plus a few near-neighbor variants. The 19 assertions are organized into four classes: `TestRejectMaliciousNames` (dotdot, deeper dotdot, POSIX absolute, null byte, UNC backslash, UNC forward-slash), `TestRejectWindowsSpecificAbsolutePaths` (drive letter, backslash root), `TestRejectMalformedInputs` (empty, whitespace, None, non-string), `TestAcceptLegitimateNames` (simple kebab-case skill name, nested category/skill, `workflow/project-constitution/SKILL.md`, filename with dot), and `TestIsSafeCandidate` (boolean wrapper sanity). The helper is OS-agnostic deliberately -- a malicious catalog name committed on Linux would still be rejected when the installer runs on Windows. Test run: `python -m pytest tests/installer -q` returns 19 passed in 0.11s on the Windows host. One DEVIATION was recorded against the Phase 9.4 prompt: the plan claimed "no Makefile change should be needed; pytest's `tests/installer/` is auto-discovered", but `make test` in this repo runs three explicit `cd extensions/<dir> && python -m pytest -q` invocations rather than auto-discovering at the repo root. The minimal corrective change appends `@if [ -d tests ]; then python -m pytest -q tests; else echo "  (no tests/ directory -- skipping repo-level suite)"; fi` to the `test:` target so the new suite runs alongside the extension tests; the conditional preserves backwards-compatibility for any future repo state without `tests/`. Logged as a deviation in `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` rather than as a `NI` or `BG` because the deviation produces a strictly-additive Makefile change and resolves the plan's intent rather than skipping it.

Phase 9.5 (stabilization) ran the full verification battery. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings across all 210 skill bundles (the new files under `tests/`, `.devcontainer/`, and `catalog/style-guides/` are not under any skill's bundle root and therefore do not affect the orphan audit). `python -m pytest tests/installer -q` returns 19 passed. The three extension test suites run clean: `nexus-skill-server` 37 passed, `nexus-code-search` 36 passed + 1 pre-existing skip, `nexus-web-fetch` 23 passed -- counts unchanged from the v2.0.0 baseline. The catalog hook test suite was not re-run (it was last run during Phase 8.1 with 370 passed + 3 skipped; Phase 9 touches no hook code). `python -c "import json; json.load(open('.devcontainer/devcontainer.json'))"` confirms the devcontainer manifest parses. The JSONC config validates after a comment-strip pass. `bash -n .devcontainer/post-create.sh` is clean. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --path catalog/skills/developer-experience/spec-driven-development` reports 0 errors and 5 baseline optional-field warnings (`category`, `author`, `version`, `license`, `tags`) carried over from before v2.1.0; the warning set is unchanged by the methodology-essay backlink edit. The cross-OS installer smoke covered in Phase 8 still applies -- Phase 9 added zero new artifacts that require an installer-script copy edit (everything under `catalog/style-guides/` is auto-copied, `.devcontainer/` is repo-local and never copied to the install target, `tests/` is repo-local and never copied, `docs/` is repo-local and never copied).

Release decision: per the Phase 9.5 prompt's "default: ship as v2.1.1 patch to keep v2.1.0 scope tight to G1-G10" guidance, the four polish items are non-functional and complement rather than alter the v2.1.0 catalog distribution surface. Held against the v2.1.0 baseline rather than cutting a separate tag because (a) the items add no new outbound calls, no new skills, no new commands, no new templates -- they are pure documentation + scaffolding + regression-guard, (b) the v2.1.0 tag was cut locally during Phase 8 but never pushed, so amending it does not break any consumer, and (c) the `data/marketplace.json` version line still reads `2.1.0` and continues to be accurate. If a downstream consumer needs to differentiate the Phase 9-augmented snapshot from the Phase 8-frozen snapshot, the git SHA is the unambiguous identifier; no version-string change is required. The `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` entry for Phase 9 records this decision explicitly so the v2.1.0 -> v2.2.0 bump in `/wrap-up-session` will not re-litigate it.

Out of scope for Phase 9 (confirmed against the plan, not deferred): the integration-registry refactor (G12) targeted at v2.2.0 in Phase 10; any cross-OS installer dry-run beyond Windows (already deferred to v1.1.5 known-gaps DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 carry-over); any new outbound dependency in the devcontainer (the post-create script uses `pip`, `apt`, and `npm` -- all already required by the catalog's existing tooling and the user's expected OS image). The 5 still-open `DF-*` items from v1.1.5 remain orthogonal and tracked in their own known-gaps file.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 8: release stabilization (`CHANGELOG.md [2.1.0]` block + `docs/v2.1.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md` + statistics rebaseline + attribution-leak fix)

Phase 8 of the adoption-spec-kit plan closes the v2.1.0 release: the full validation sweep across the catalog, JSON registries, extension test suites, hook test suite, and installer smoke harness; the authoring of the `CHANGELOG.md [2.1.0]` Keep-a-Changelog block and the matching `docs/v2.1.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md` narrative; the `data/marketplace.json` `plugin.version` bump from 2.0.0 to 2.1.0; the rebaseline of the `data/skills.json` statistics block to match the actual array length (closing WN-1 from Phase 1); the rename of the user-facing `spec-kit-task` GitHub-issues label to `spec-driven-task` across the `/tasks-to-issues` surface (closing an attribution leak that the Phase 7 prompt had inadvertently prescribed against cross-cutting constraint #1 of the same plan); the README "What's new in v2.1.0" section that frames the Spec-Driven Development adoption for landing users; and the `docs/v2.1.0/installer-smoke-post.txt` smoke record covering the v2.1.0-specific distribution surface (3 new auto-distributed skill bundles, 4 new auto-distributed commands, 3 new auto-distributed templates, 2 new explicitly-registered repo-level helper scripts, 1 per-skill bundled `scripts/` + `references/` subtree under `tasks-to-issues/`). The phase introduces no new code in the catalog (all v2.1.0 catalog work landed in Phases 1-7) and no new outbound calls; it is purely a stabilization, documentation, and registry-hygiene pass that makes the release shippable.

The Phase 8.1 validation sweep was run end-to-end. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings across all 210 skill bundles (the orphan-bundle auditor finds zero unreferenced files under any skill's `scripts/`, `references/`, or `assets/` subdirectory, including the new `tasks-to-issues/scripts/` and `tasks-to-issues/references/` bundle from Phase 7). `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests/ -q` reports 370 passed + 3 skipped, matching the v2.0.0 baseline with no v2.1.0 regression; the three skipped tests are pre-existing platform-conditional skips, not v2.1.0-introduced. The three extension test suites (`extensions/nexus-skill-server`, `extensions/nexus-code-search`, `extensions/nexus-web-fetch`) report 37 + 36 + 23 passed (1 pre-existing skip in `nexus-code-search`); counts unchanged from the v2.0.0 baseline. The 26-assertion installer-smoke pytest battery (`catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py`) is green; the assertion set covers the banner block, the version-constant carry-through, the legacy-install migration logic (legacy-only / both-exist / neither branches), the global-vs-workspace scope prompt, and the bash + PowerShell syntax cleanliness of both installers. The three new v2.1.0 skills (`workflow/project-constitution`, `code-review/cross-artifact-analyzer`, `workflow/tasks-to-issues`) each pass `python scripts/validate_skills.py --path <skill>` with 0 errors and 5 baseline warnings each (missing optional `tags`, `license`, `category`, `version`, `author` frontmatter fields -- a pattern shared by the majority of pre-existing catalog skills and not v2.1.0-introduced).

The Phase 8.1 attribution-leak audit (`grep -i "speckit|spec-kit|github/spec-kit|specify-cli" catalog/`) identified one true leak: the user-facing GitHub-issues label `spec-kit-task` that the Phase 7 prompt had prescribed for `/tasks-to-issues`. Per the Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule in `AGENTS.md` and cross-cutting constraint #1 of the source plan, `spec-kit` is on the forbidden list for user-facing artifacts (a label attached to GitHub issues IS user-facing). The label was renamed to `spec-driven-task` across six surfaces in lockstep: `catalog/commands/tasks-to-issues.md` (1 prose reference + 2 example-line references), `catalog/skills/workflow/tasks-to-issues/SKILL.md` (description + overview_l1 + 2 body references + 1 example), `catalog/skills/workflow/tasks-to-issues/scripts/tasks-to-issues.sh` (1 label-string definition), `catalog/skills/workflow/tasks-to-issues/scripts/tasks-to-issues.ps1` (1 label-array definition), `catalog/skills/workflow/tasks-to-issues/references/gh-cli-auth-runbook.md` (3 references covering the `gh label create` recipe and the post-run audit query), and the `tasks-to-issues` entry in `data/skills.json` (description + long_description + overview_l1). All other matches for `spec-kit` in the catalog tree are internal path references to our own plan file `docs/v2.1.0/plans/adoption-spec-kit.md` and are acceptable -- they point at OUR doc, not at the upstream. The historical plan file is intentionally left as-is because the plan is a frozen historical record; the BG-1 known-gaps entry records that the plan's Phase 7 prompt block still mentions `spec-kit-task` for future-archaeology context.

The Phase 8.1 data-registry consistency audit confirmed all three registries received the expected `+3` delta over the v2.0.0 baseline: `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` 205 -> 208 rows (`+1` for `project-constitution` in Phase 1, `+1` for `cross-artifact-analyzer` in Phase 3, `+1` for `tasks-to-issues` in Phase 7), `data/skills.json` 203 -> 206 entries (matching increments), and `data/marketplace.json` 200 -> 203 category sum (with `Code Review` going 9 -> 10 in Phase 3 and `Workflow` going 21 -> 23 across Phases 1 and 7). The pre-existing absolute-count mismatches between disk (210 SKILL.md files), SKILL_INDEX (208 rows), skills.json (206 entries), and marketplace (203 category sum) are carry-over from before v2.0.0 -- they trace to historical category-name inconsistency (kebab-case primary keys mixed with three Title-Case duplicates for `Developer Experience`, `Research`, `Workflow`) and to a handful of pre-existing skills that ship on disk but were never registered. These pre-existing absolute mismatches are explicitly out of scope for v2.1.0 release stabilization; they remain for a future data-quality plan and are not v2.1.0-introduced regressions.

The Phase 8.1 statistics-block rebaseline closed WN-1 from Phase 1. The `data/skills.json` `statistics` block had been stale since before v2.0.0 -- `statistics.total_skills` read 197 while the actual skills array held 203 entries at v2.0.0 close, and Phases 1 / 3 / 7 each correctly applied the `+1` array-side increment without touching the stale statistics block (the per-phase prompts only enumerated the array, the SKILL_INDEX, and the marketplace -- they did not name the statistics block). Phase 8.1 recomputes the statistics block from the actual skills array: `statistics.total_skills` becomes 206 and `statistics.categories.*` mirrors the per-category disk counts. The three Title-Case duplicate keys are preserved (rather than collapsed into their kebab-case siblings) because collapsing them would break any downstream consumer that has already indexed the catalog by the legacy keys; the duplicates are a pre-existing data-quality artifact and outside v2.1.0 scope.

The Phase 8.2 release-documentation authoring produced the `CHANGELOG.md [2.1.0]` Keep-a-Changelog block (positioned above the existing `[2.0.0]` block) with the headline narrative (the SDD adoption story), the Added section (4 commands, 3 skills, 3 templates, 2 helper scripts, 1 reference runbook), the Changed section (5 updated skills + the `/generate-plan` flow update + the statistics rebaseline), the Security section (MCP Registry Policy review passes for all 11 items; the `gh` CLI usage classified as vendor-as-intrinsic-destination per Bucket 4), the Tests section (96 extension tests + 370 hook tests + 26 installer-smoke assertions all green), the Known-gaps section (forward-pointer to `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md`), the Migration section (no migration required; v2.1.0 is fully additive), and the Plan-and-source section (links to the plan and to the source comparison report). The matching `docs/v2.1.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md` narrative is modeled on `docs/v2.0.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md`: Highlights paragraph, "SDD adoption -- the narrative" prose, three "What's new" subsections (commands, skills, templates, helper scripts, updated existing skills), an adoption-candidate map (G1-G11 with shipped artifacts per row), an out-of-scope-here section (G12 + Phase 9 P3 polish + the v1.1.5 cross-OS carry-overs), a no-migration-required note, the verification table from Phase 8.1, the known-carry-overs section, the coordinated-follow-up section (push the tag; Phase 9 / Phase 10 future scheduling), and the cross-references block.
