The `data/marketplace.json` `plugin.version` bumped from `2.0.0` to `2.1.0` and `plugin.description` updated from "203+ curated skills" to "206+ curated skills"; the `README.md` "What's new" block gained a new v2.1.0 subsection above the existing v2.0.0 subsection that frames the Spec-Driven Development adoption with the headline bullets (4 commands, 3 skills, 3 templates, `/generate-plan` updates, the `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` marker discipline) and links to the CHANGELOG block, the RELEASE_NOTES, and the plan.

The Phase 8.3 cross-OS installer smoke verification is recorded in `docs/v2.1.0/installer-smoke-post.txt`. The installer does not implement a `--target <dir>` flag (paths are derived from `$HOME`), so the smoke coverage follows the precedent set in `docs/v2.0.0/installer-smoke-post.txt`: the 26-assertion pytest battery in `catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py` plus `bash -n` syntax checks on `scripts/installer.sh`, `scripts/new-feature.sh`, and `catalog/skills/workflow/tasks-to-issues/scripts/tasks-to-issues.sh` plus PowerShell AST parse checks on the three corresponding `.ps1` siblings, plus a per-artifact-class enumeration confirming the v2.1.0 distribution surface (new skill bundles auto-distributed by the recursive `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy` primitives; new commands and templates auto-distributed by the existing `install_commands` and `install_templates` blocks; the two new repo-level helper scripts `scripts/new-feature.{sh,ps1}` explicitly registered in BOTH installers per the Installer-Aware Changes rule -- confirmed at `scripts/installer.sh` lines 1461-1468 and `scripts/installer.ps1` lines 1771-1777). The Linux / macOS coverage carry-over from v1.1.5 DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 is explicitly recorded in the smoke-post.txt as out of scope for v2.1.0 -- it remains tracked for a future CI-matrix plan and is not v2.1.0-introduced.

The Phase 8 update to `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` marks v2.1.0 ready-to-ship: WN-1 (statistics drift) moves from Open to Resolved with the rebaseline note; BG-1 (the `spec-kit-task` attribution leak) is recorded as Resolved with the per-surface enumeration of the six files that were touched in lockstep; the Summary table is updated to 0 Open / 2 Resolved; the Open Items section is empty. The `Last updated` line records the full Phase 8 close narrative including the validation-sweep counts, the attribution-leak fix, the statistics rebaseline, the release-doc authoring, the marketplace version bump, and the cross-OS smoke posture. The file lifecycle note at the bottom is unchanged -- `/wrap-up-session` Phase 4 step 4b and `/update-version` will finalize the file at the v2.1.0 -> next-version bump.

Validation runs clean: all five `data/*.json` files parse cleanly with UTF-8 encoding; `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports 0 errors and 0 warnings across 210 skill bundles; the 26 installer-smoke assertions are green; the 466 catalog + extension tests are green (370 hook + 37 + 36 + 23 -- with 4 pre-existing platform-conditional skips that are not v2.1.0-introduced); `bash -n` is clean on all v2.1.0-touched shell scripts; PowerShell AST parse is clean on all v2.1.0-touched `.ps1` siblings. Phase 8 introduced no DEVIATION markers, no test failures, no suppressed lint rules, and no bypassed gates. The release is ready for the `git tag v2.1.0` cut (the tag is created locally; the push is deferred to explicit user action per the CLAUDE.md global rule that destructive / remote-mutating git operations require user confirmation).

Next: Phase 9 -- P3 polish items. The four artifacts (`docs/v2.1.0/spec-driven-methodology.md` essay, `.devcontainer/` with per-agent CLI install in `post-create.sh`, `catalog/style-guides/markdownlint-cli2.jsonc` config aligned with the existing markdown style guide, `tests/installer/test_registrar_path_traversal.py` defensive test mirroring the spec-kit registrar pattern) can ship as v2.1.x patches without blocking v2.1.0. Phase 9 prerequisites are Phase 8 (v2.1.0 base release stable) -- now complete. The user may choose to bundle Phase 9 into v2.1.0 by re-tagging, or to ship it separately as v2.1.1.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 5: clarification loop + spec quality checklist (`/clarify-spec` command + `spec-quality-checklist.md` template)

Phase 5 of the adoption-spec-kit plan lands the active-clarification surface that pairs with the Phase 4 spec template and the Phase 3 read-only `/analyze-spec` audit. A new `catalog/commands/clarify-spec.md` command implements the sequential 5-question clarification loop with a mandatory Recommended-option contract above every question, a 10-category ambiguity taxonomy (Functional Scope & Behavior, Domain & Data Model, Interaction & UX Flow, Non-Functional Quality Attributes, Integration & External Dependencies, Edge Cases & Failure Handling, Constraints & Tradeoffs, Terminology & Consistency, Completion Signals, Misc / Placeholders), atomic save-per-answer into the spec body plus a `## Clarifications` section, and four distinct stop conditions. A new `catalog/templates/spec-quality-checklist.md` template ships the "unit tests for English" auto-validation surface with Content Quality, Requirement Completeness, and Feature Readiness sections. Three existing artifacts gain body-only updates that wire the new surfaces into the broader SDD workflow: `idea-refine` gains a Boundary subsection clarifying the before-spec vs after-spec division of labor between it and `/clarify-spec`; `spec-driven-development` gains an Auto-validating-the-spec subsection describing the 3-pass checklist iteration plus a new Related Skills cross-link to `/clarify-spec`; `/generate-plan` Step 4 gains a Spec-quality-checklist companion block that writes the checklist alongside the plan when plan type 2 (feature addition) is detected and a sibling `spec.md` exists. This is the G3 + G9 adoption work from [docs/v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md](v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md); like Phases 1, 3, and 4 it ships as skill-native artifacts with no new code, no new outbound calls, and no new dependencies, consistent with the MCP Registry Policy decision tree's reverse-engineer-first sequencing.

The new command at `catalog/commands/clarify-spec.md` documents seven steps: resolve the spec file (honors an explicit `<path>` argument, otherwise walks `.specify/feature.json` -> latest `specs/<NNN>-*/spec.md` -> latest `docs/<version>/plans/<slug>.md`, with an explicit abort-with-guidance message when nothing matches), run the structured ambiguity scan across the 10-category taxonomy with each category marked Clear / Partial / Missing, generate the prioritized question queue (at most 5 candidates, balanced across at least 3 distinct categories, ordered by the same `scope > security/privacy > UX > technical` priority that Phase 1 ratified for `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` markers), the sequential questioning loop with exactly one question per turn (multiple-choice format renders the Recommended option above the table with cited evidence and an aligned-pipes Markdown table of 2-5 options; short-phrase format renders the Recommended <=5-word guess above the prompt; both accept `yes` / `recommended` / `accept` / `r` for the recommendation, a single letter A-E for an option, a free-form short answer for an override, `skip` to defer, and `stop` / `done` / `good` / `no more` to end the loop early), per-answer integration into the spec body (a section-by-section mapping table routes each category to the right subsection -- Functional Scope to FR-### items, Domain & Data Model to Key Entities, Interaction & UX Flow to Acceptance Scenarios, Non-Functional Quality Attributes to measurable thresholds, Integration to External Dependencies, Edge Cases to the eponymous subsection, Constraints to the header or a Constraints subsection, Terminology to canonical-name replacement across all sections, Completion Signals to SC-### items, Misc / Placeholders to in-place replacement) followed by an atomic save (temp file + fsync + rename) and a one-line confirmation echo, the four stop conditions (5 questions asked, all critical ambiguities resolved with no Missing categories and at most one Partial, user signal, empty queueable set), and the completion report with the per-category before/after status table, outstanding-ambiguities bullets, and a recommended-next-command branch. The command exposes three invocation forms: `/clarify-spec` (default, latest spec), `/clarify-spec <path>` (explicit spec file or feature directory), and `/clarify-spec --max-questions N` (lower the question cap; never raise above 5). The behavior guarantees are explicit: sequential questioning (never two questions in one message), Recommended-option mandatory with cited evidence, atomic save per answer (a crash mid-loop leaves the spec consistent up to the last answered question), hard cap 5 questions, no new sibling files created (`clarifications.md` / `q-and-a.md` are explicitly disallowed -- the loop only edits the spec in place and adds the `## Clarifications` section), and cross-link discipline (FR-### / SC-### IDs added or modified during the session remain stable, with new IDs using the next sequential number in the spec's existing scheme).

The new template at `catalog/templates/spec-quality-checklist.md` ships with three checklist sections: Content Quality (no implementation details, focused on user value, written for non-technical stakeholders, all mandatory sections completed), Requirement Completeness (no `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` markers remain, requirements are testable and unambiguous, success criteria are measurable and technology-agnostic, all acceptance scenarios defined, edge cases identified, scope clearly bounded, dependencies and assumptions identified), and Feature Readiness (every functional requirement has clear acceptance criteria, user scenarios cover primary flows, the feature meets measurable outcomes from Success Criteria, no implementation details leak into the spec). The closing Notes section codifies the iteration contract: items that remain unchecked after 3 passes must be documented in the spec's `## Assumptions` section before advancing to `/clarify-spec` or `/generate-plan`; FR-### or SC-### IDs that are inherently non-testable must be rewritten rather than silently ticked; the checklist validates the spec's prose ("unit tests for English") rather than implementation correctness (which is validated separately by tests against FR-### / SC-### IDs via `[[cross-artifact-analyzer]]`'s coverage matrix).

Three existing artifacts gain body-only updates. `catalog/skills/developer-experience/idea-refine/SKILL.md` Step 5 (Identify Open Questions) gains a new `Boundary with /clarify-spec` paragraph immediately after the existing 3-marker-cap paragraph, clarifying that `idea-refine` operates BEFORE a spec exists (input: a vague idea or `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]`-laden problem statement; output: a one-page problem statement with bounded scope and observable success criteria) while `/clarify-spec` operates AFTER `spec.md` is written (input: a structured spec with FR-### / SC-### IDs; output: the same spec with ambiguities resolved at the requirement and user-story granularity through the sequential 5-question loop). The paragraph explicitly notes that `/clarify-spec` should not be invoked against a vague problem statement -- it expects the structured template from `catalog/templates/spec-template.md`. `catalog/skills/developer-experience/spec-driven-development/SKILL.md` gains a new `### Auto-validating the spec` subsection immediately after the `### User stories with priorities` subsection from Phase 4, documenting the 3-pass iteration (Pass 1 Content Quality, Pass 2 Requirement Completeness, Pass 3 Feature Readiness) and the post-3-pass behavior (remaining unchecked items go into `## Assumptions` and warn the user before advancing). The same skill body gains a new Related Skills cross-link to `/clarify-spec` describing it as the Phase 5 sequential 5-question loop that resolves spec ambiguities after the template's slots are filled and pairs with the spec-quality-checklist for the final readiness gate before `/generate-plan`. `catalog/commands/generate-plan.md` Step 4 gains a new `### Spec-quality-checklist companion (feature-level plans only)` block immediately after the existing Constitution Check + Complexity Tracking discussion that resolves the target directory (`docs/<version>/plans/<slug>/checklists/requirements.md` for the default layout or `specs/<NNN>-<slug>/checklists/requirements.md` for the Phase-7 opt-in layout), copies the template with `[FEATURE NAME]` / `[DATE]` / `[Link to spec.md]` placeholders replaced, leaves an existing checklist file in place (with an announcement directing the user to rerun `/clarify-spec` or edit the checklist directly to refresh), and tells the user the next step (run `/clarify-spec` if unchecked items remain, or `/analyze-spec` followed by `/implement-phase <slug> phase-1` if all items pass). The block explicitly skips the checklist companion for plan types 1 (initial greenfield build), 3 (refactor), and 4 (other) since none of those plan types produces a feature spec; the Constitution Check section above is still emitted for all plan types.

Phase 5 does not introduce a new skill -- it ships one new command, one new template, and three existing-artifact body updates -- so no `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`, `data/skills.json`, or `data/marketplace.json` updates are required. The template lives under `catalog/templates/` which is already auto-copied by the installer's `install_templates` block (via `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy`) to `~/.nexus-hub/templates/`; commands under `catalog/commands/` are already auto-copied to each platform's command directory (Claude `commands/`, Codex `prompts/`, Gemini `workflows/`) via the same recursive primitive. No installer edit is required.

Validation runs clean: JSON parse on all five `data/*.json` files succeeds with UTF-8 encoding (`skills.json` still reports 205 entries -- unchanged from Phase 4 close); the skill-bundle audit (`python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) returns 0 errors and 0 warnings across the 209 skills it scans (no orphan bundles introduced because Phase 5 ships only a top-level command, a top-level template, and three existing-artifact body edits; no new bundled `scripts/`, `references/`, or `assets/` subdirectories). The full-validator pass on the two updated skill files returns 0 errors and 5 pre-existing baseline warnings each (missing optional `author` / `version` / `category` / `license` / `tags` frontmatter fields -- identical to the warning profile that was present before Phase 5 and matches the established baseline across the catalog). Phase 5 introduced no DEVIATION markers, no test failures, no suppressed lint rules, and no bypassed gates; `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` records a Phase-5-close note with no new items added and no items resolved (WN-1, the pre-existing `data/skills.json` statistics drift, remains open and stays scheduled for repair via `make build-catalog` in Phase 8 sub-task 8.1 step 5).

Next: Phase 6 -- task discipline. `/generate-plan` Step 3 (Design the Phase Breakdown) and Step 4 (Generate the Plan File) will be updated to enforce a strict task checklist format -- `- [ ] T### [P?] [US?] Description with file path` -- across the entire plan with sequential T-IDs in execution order, optional `[P]` markers for parallelizable tasks (different files, no dependencies on incomplete tasks), required `[US#]` labels for user-story phase tasks (mapped to the P1/P2/P3 IDs from Phase 4) but no labels on Setup / Foundational / Polish tasks, and an exact file-path reference per task. Phase organization will shift to a strict ordering: Setup, Foundational, then user stories in priority order (P1 -> P2 -> P3 each with its internal Tests -> Models -> Services -> Endpoints -> Integration sub-ordering and an independently-testable increment as its acceptance criterion), and a final Polish & Cross-Cutting Concerns phase. A Format Validation step in Step 5 will scan the produced plan and report task-format compliance (or list violations and re-prompt). Phase 6 prerequisites are Phase 4 (provides the user-story IDs that the `[US#]` label keys off) -- now complete.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 4: spec template upgrade (`catalog/templates/spec-template.md` + `spec-driven-development` skill updates)

Phase 4 of the adoption-spec-kit plan lands the canonical spec template that downstream tooling keys off and the `spec-driven-development` skill body that enforces it. A new `catalog/templates/spec-template.md` ships with the eight mandatory sections in order -- Header block, User Scenarios & Testing (with prioritized P1/P2/P3 user stories carrying Independent Test paragraphs and Given/When/Then Acceptance Scenarios), Edge Cases, Requirements (with `**FR-###**: System MUST <capability>` IDs and an optional Key Entities subsection), Success Criteria (with `**SC-###**: <measurable outcome>` IDs), and Assumptions. The `catalog/skills/developer-experience/spec-driven-development/SKILL.md` body gains two new subsections (Spec template; User stories with priorities), two new Common Rationalizations rebuttals (bullet-points-vs-IDs; single-user-story specs), and two new Related Skills cross-links (`cross-artifact-analyzer`, `project-constitution`). This is part of the Bucket A skill-native adoption work from [docs/v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md](v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md); like Phases 1 and 3 it ships with no new code, no new outbound calls, and no new dependencies, consistent with the MCP Registry Policy decision tree's reverse-engineer-first sequencing.

The new template at `catalog/templates/spec-template.md` is the surface every feature spec is meant to start from. The opening HTML comment block documents the section conventions (mandatory MUST be completed; optional include-if-relevant; remove entirely when not applicable -- do not leave as "N/A"), the `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION: <specific question>]` marker rules with the Phase 1 hard cap of 3 markers per spec prioritized by `scope > security/privacy > UX > technical`, and the FR-### / SC-### ID convention that `/analyze-spec` consumes. The User Scenarios block guides authors toward prioritized journeys (P1 = smallest scope that delivers user value; P2 / P3 add value but are not required for the MVP), each with a `**Why this priority**` justification, an `**Independent Test**` paragraph (the smallest end-to-end check that proves the story is delivered, runnable without implementing any other story), and Given/When/Then Acceptance Scenarios. The Requirements block uses `**FR-001**: System MUST <capability>` with FR-002 carrying an example `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` marker so the template surfaces the convention without forcing the user to remember it. The Success Criteria block uses `**SC-001**: <measurable outcome>` with HTML-comment guidance listing the four criteria (Measurable, Technology-agnostic, User-focused, Verifiable) and the anti-patterns to avoid (`"System is fast"`, `"Uses Redis for caching"`, `"Code is well-structured"`, `"Tests pass"`). The Assumptions section is mandatory whenever any candidate ambiguity was demoted below the 3-marker hard limit from Phase 1.

The `spec-driven-development` skill body gains the new "Spec template" subsection immediately after the `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` marker convention. The subsection explains why the FR-### / SC-### IDs matter (they are the join key the `cross-artifact-analyzer` skill uses to build the Coverage Summary table in `/analyze-spec`; a spec written with prose bullets instead of IDs produces an empty matrix and the analyzer cannot flag missing tasks) and documents the three ID-stability rules: sequential within category (FR-001, FR-002, ...; SC-001, SC-002, ...), stable across edits (once assigned, do not renumber; removing a requirement leaves a gap), and unique within the spec but not globally across the project (FR-001 in two different specs are different requirements). The new "User stories with priorities" subsection enforces the three disciplines downstream tooling depends on: priority labels P1/P2/P3 assigned by user value rather than implementation order (with the explicit note that the Phase 6 `[US#]` task label is keyed off these IDs); the Independent Test paragraph that proves each story is deliverable in isolation (the MVP contract: implementing only P1 must pass the P1 Independent Test even when P2 and P3 are untouched); and the MVP rule that implementing just P1 must deliver value to a real user, with the example anti-pattern of a P1 story like "set up the database schema" -- an enabler, not a user story. The subsection also handles the single-story case explicitly: still use `### User Story 1 - [Title] (Priority: P1)` with the full Independent Test and Acceptance Scenarios so `/analyze-spec` can find the story regardless of count.

Two new Common Rationalizations rebuttals join the existing five-row table: "I'll just use bullet points instead of FR/SC IDs" rebuts with the failure mode (the IDs are not decoration; they are the analyzer's join key; an empty matrix means missing tasks cannot be flagged) and points at the template; "This feature only has one user story" rebuts by explaining the single-story case explicitly (still write it as User Story 1 with priority P1 plus the Independent Test paragraph and Acceptance Scenarios because `/analyze-spec` and the Phase 6 task discipline both find the story by its heading regardless of count). The two new Related Skills cross-links round out the skill body's connections to the rest of the v2.1.0 SDD adoption work: `cross-artifact-analyzer` (verifies the FR-### / SC-### IDs in the spec have matching tasks via the Coverage Summary table in `/analyze-spec`) and `project-constitution` (establishes the MUST/SHOULD principles that the Constitution Check section of every plan validates against).

Phase 4 does not introduce a new skill -- it ships a template plus body edits to an existing skill -- so no `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`, `data/skills.json`, or `data/marketplace.json` updates are required. The template lives under `catalog/templates/` which is already auto-copied by the installer's `install_templates` block (via `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy`) to `~/.nexus-hub/templates/`; no installer edit is required.

Validation runs clean: JSON parse on all five `data/*.json` files succeeds with UTF-8 encoding (`skills.json` still reports 205 entries -- unchanged from Phase 3 close); the skill-bundle audit (`python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) returns 0 errors and 0 warnings across the 209 skills it scans (no orphan bundles introduced because Phase 4 ships only a top-level template + an existing-skill body edit; no new bundled `scripts/`, `references/`, or `assets/` subdirectories). The verification of the new template's coverage-matrix contract was run against a throwaway `scratch/` spec+plan+tasks triple authored directly against the new template (two user stories P1/P2 each with Independent Test paragraphs; three FRs and two SCs; four tasks referencing FR-001 / FR-001 / FR-002 / SC-001 with FR-003 and SC-002 intentionally uncovered); a grep simulation of `/analyze-spec` Pass 5 confirmed the analyzer would correctly identify FR-003 and SC-002 as Has-Task = No with severity HIGH per the Step 4 heuristic in `catalog/commands/analyze-spec.md`. The scratch files were deleted after verification. Phase 4 introduced no DEVIATION markers, no test failures, no suppressed lint rules, and no bypassed gates; `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` records a Phase-4-close note with no new items added and no items resolved.

Next: Phase 5 -- ship the `/clarify-spec` command (sequential 5-question loop with recommended-option Markdown tables, 10-category ambiguity taxonomy spanning Functional Scope, Domain & Data Model, Interaction & UX Flow, Non-Functional Quality Attributes, Integration & External Dependencies, Edge Cases & Failure Handling, Constraints & Tradeoffs, Terminology & Consistency, Completion Signals, Misc / Placeholders) plus `catalog/templates/spec-quality-checklist.md` (the "unit tests for English" auto-validation checklist with Content Quality / Requirement Completeness / Feature Readiness sections) and wire the checklist into `/generate-plan` Step 4 so that feature-level plans co-produce a spec-quality-checklist when a spec exists in the same directory. Phase 5 prerequisites are Phase 1 (marker discipline established) and Phase 4 (spec template provides the surface to clarify against) -- both now complete.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 3: cross-artifact spec analyzer (`/analyze-spec` + `cross-artifact-analyzer` skill)

Phase 3 of the adoption-spec-kit plan lands the read-only cross-artifact consistency analyzer: a new `/analyze-spec` command at `catalog/commands/analyze-spec.md` and its companion `cross-artifact-analyzer` skill at `catalog/skills/code-review/cross-artifact-analyzer/SKILL.md`. The analyzer reads a feature directory's spec.md, plan.md, and tasks.md (plus the project constitution from Phase 1 if it exists at `docs/<version>/constitution.md`), runs six detection passes (Duplication, Ambiguity, Underspecification, Constitution Alignment, Coverage Gaps, Inconsistency), assigns severity per a documented heuristic (CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW), and emits a Markdown report with three tables (Findings capped at 50 rows with overflow summary; Coverage Summary keyed by FR-### / SC-###; Metrics block) plus Next Actions. This is the G4 adoption candidate from [docs/v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md](v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md); it ships as a skill-native artifact with no new code, no new outbound calls, and no new dependencies, consistent with the MCP Registry Policy decision tree's reverse-engineer-first sequencing.

As part of this phase, the source plan file was relocated from `docs/v2.0.0/plans/adoption-spec-kit.md` to `docs/v2.1.0/plans/adoption-spec-kit.md` to align with the targeted release version. The Plan and Plan reference links inside `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` were updated to point at the new location.

The new command at `catalog/commands/analyze-spec.md` documents seven steps: resolve the feature directory (honors an explicit `<path>` argument, otherwise walks `.specify/feature.json` -> latest `specs/<NNN>-*/` -> latest `docs/<version>/plans/<slug>.md`); load minimal context per artifact (only the sections each detection pass needs, with a self-contained-plan fallback that parses functional requirements out of phase prompts, success criteria out of Stability Gate text, and tasks out of `- [ ] T###` lines); run the six detection passes in order; assign severity per the heuristic with promotion allowed and demotion not allowed; assign deterministic category-prefixed IDs (`A1, D1, U1, C1, G1, I1`) sorted within a category by source-line number; emit the report; report completion. The command exposes three invocation forms: `/analyze-spec` (default, latest feature), `/analyze-spec <path>` (explicit directory or plan file), and `/analyze-spec --output <path>` (write the report to a file in addition to stdout). The behavior guarantees are explicit: read-only (the only writable target is the optional `--output` path), deterministic IDs across reruns, constitution-aware-but-optional (Pass 4 emits an informational N/A when the constitution file is absent), overflow-safe (Findings table is capped at 50 rows with a one-line summary pointing at `--verbose` for the full list). The command body ends with a mandatory closing statement -- "This analyzer is read-only. It modifies no files. Any remediation requires user approval." -- that the report MUST also emit regardless of how it was rendered.

The new skill at `catalog/skills/code-review/cross-artifact-analyzer/SKILL.md` drives the command end-to-end. Required YAML frontmatter is complete (`name`, `description`, `summary_l0`, `overview_l1`), the description follows the pushy-description rule from `AGENTS.md` with trigger phrases verbatim ("analyze the spec", "is this spec ready", "do all requirements have tasks", "coverage check", "ambiguity check", "spec-plan-tasks consistency", "cross-artifact analysis", "constitution alignment check on this plan", "find gaps in this feature") plus a SKIP clause that fences off full code-review of an implementation, single-file lint passes, runtime profiling, and test execution. The body covers all required sections (When to Use, Inputs table, Instructions, Common Rationalizations, Verification, Related Skills). The Rationalizations table rebuts six excuses the agent might use to skip the analyzer ("too small a feature", "/generate-plan already cross-checks", "I'll read it myself", "constitution alignment will always pass", "I'll just delete findings I disagree with", "IDs will change on rerun anyway"). Cross-links via `[[name]]` syntax point to `project-constitution` (source of MUST principles for Pass 4), `ambiguity-detector` (shares the vague-adjective and [NEEDS CLARIFICATION] detection logic; this analyzer applies it cross-artifact while ambiguity-detector operates per-text), `spec-driven-development` (defines FR-### / SC-### / user-story-priority conventions), `implementation-plan` (produces the plans this analyzer audits), `idea-refine`, `known-gaps-tracker`, and `final-report` (sibling code-review skill; this analyzer focuses on specification artifacts while final-report consolidates findings from a full code review).

The three data registries are updated per the rule in `AGENTS.md`: `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gains a new row for `cross-artifact-analyzer` and bumps the total from 204 to 205; `data/skills.json` gains a new entry following the established schema; `data/marketplace.json` bumps the code-review category `skill_count` from 9 to 10. The pre-existing `data/skills.json` statistics drift (WN-1, opened in Phase 1) is now compounded by one more entry -- the skills array length reads 205 while `statistics.total_skills` still reads 197 -- and the WN-1 entry in `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` was updated to record that compounded value. The drift remains scheduled for repair via `make build-catalog` in Phase 8 sub-task 8.1 step 5.

Validation runs clean: JSON parse on all five `data/*.json` files succeeds with UTF-8 encoding; the skill-bundle audit (`python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) returns 0 errors and 0 warnings across the 209 skills it scans (208 in Phase 1; +1 for the new cross-artifact-analyzer); the full-validator pass on the new skill returns 0 errors with 5 baseline warnings (missing optional `author` / `version` / `category` / `license` / `tags` frontmatter fields -- identical to the warning profile of the sibling `project-constitution` skill, confirming the new skill matches the established baseline). Phase 3 introduced no DEVIATION markers, no test failures, no suppressed lint rules, and no bypassed gates; `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` records a Phase-3-close note with no new items added and no items resolved.

Next: Phase 4 -- spec template upgrade. `catalog/templates/spec-template.md` will land with mandatory User Scenarios (P1 / P2 / P3 user stories each with Independent Test criteria), Functional Requirements as FR-### entries, Success Criteria as SC-### entries, and Key Entities subsections. The `spec-driven-development` skill will gain new subsections enforcing the template and the user-story discipline, and Common Rationalizations rebuttals for "bullet points instead of IDs" and "this feature only has one user story". The Phase 3 analyzer's coverage matrix consumes the FR-### / SC-### IDs that Phase 4 ships, so the two phases pair cleanly: Phase 4 produces the canonical ID scheme; Phase 3 validates that every ID has at least one task.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.1.0 adoption-spec-kit Phase 1: project constitution + NEEDS CLARIFICATION marker discipline

Phase 1 of the adoption-spec-kit plan ([docs/v2.0.0/plans/adoption-spec-kit.md](v2.0.0/plans/adoption-spec-kit.md)) lands the foundation that downstream phases depend on: a new `project-constitution` skill, a new `/constitution` command, a new `catalog/templates/constitution-template.md` skeleton, and a standardized `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION: <specific question>]` marker convention (with a hard cap of 3 markers per artifact and a fixed priority order `scope > security/privacy > UX > technical`) wired into three existing developer-experience skills. This is the Bucket A / G1 + G2 work from [docs/v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md](v2.0.0/comparison-spec-kit.md); both adoption candidates ship as skill-native artifacts with no new code, no new outbound calls, and no new dependencies, consistent with the MCP Registry Policy decision tree's reverse-engineer-first sequencing.

The new skill at `catalog/skills/workflow/project-constitution/SKILL.md` defines the constitution as a versioned project-governance file (MUST / SHOULD principles that bind every plan, spec, ADR, code review, and release) distinct from CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md (which are agent-instructions for AI tools). The body covers file location (recommended: `docs/<version>/constitution.md`; acceptable: `CONSTITUTION.md` at root), the required structure (preamble, numbered principles with Statement / Rationale / Applies to subsections, governance, versioning), the amendment workflow (SemVer applied to principles: MAJOR for removals or redefinitions, MINOR for additions, PATCH for clarifications), the Sync Impact Report HTML comment emitted at the top of the file on every edit (version change, modified principles, added or removed sections, templates and commands requiring updates), and the Constitution Check gate that Phase 2 wires into `/generate-plan`. The frontmatter description follows the pushy-description rule from `AGENTS.md` -- trigger phrases verbatim plus a SKIP clause covering CLAUDE.md edits, single-feature READMEs, one-decision ADRs, and commit-message conventions. The skill cross-links via `[[name]]` syntax to `spec-driven-development`, `architecture-decision-record`, `implementation-plan`, `ambiguity-detector`, `idea-refine`, and `known-gaps-tracker`.

The new command at `catalog/commands/constitution.md` drives the skill end-to-end with a seven-step flow: resolve constitution location (detects existing files at `docs/<version>/constitution.md`, older `docs/v*/constitution.md`, or `CONSTITUTION.md` at root and picks the most recent), collect or derive placeholder values (interactive question loop in author mode; targeted edit prompts in amend mode), draft the constitution content from the template, run the propagation check across `catalog/commands/generate-plan.md`, `catalog/templates/spec-template.md` (when Phase 4 ships it), `catalog/commands/analyze-spec.md` (when Phase 3 ships it), and any plan or ADR that cites the constitution, emit the Sync Impact Report, validate (header completeness, ISO dates, no leftover `[ALL_CAPS_IDENTIFIER]` placeholders, principle-subsection completeness, 3-marker cap, version-increment matches change classification), and write. The command uses Nexus-Hub-native framing throughout per the Reverse-Engineering Attribution Rule -- no upstream attribution leaks into the user-facing artifact. A `/constitution check <plan-path>` shorthand runs the Constitution Check gate read-only against an existing plan or spec.

The new template at `catalog/templates/constitution-template.md` is a skeleton with placeholder slots for the Sync Impact Report HTML comment, the four header fields (Project, Version, Ratified, Last Amended), the Preamble, three principle subsections each with Statement / Rationale / Applies to (extensible up to 7), two optional sections (Operational Standards, Quality Bars), Governance, and Versioning. Placeholders are bracketed with `[ALL_CAPS_IDENTIFIER]` so the validator in the `/constitution` command can detect any that were not filled in.

The three existing developer-experience skills get a new subsection each that aligns them with the marker convention: `spec-driven-development/SKILL.md` gains "Marking uncertainty with `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]`" under "When to Use This Skill", with rules, examples, and the 3-marker cap; `ambiguity-detector/SKILL.md` gains "Step 7: Emit `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` Markers Aligned with the Project Convention" after Step 6 (Generate the Ambiguity Report), explaining how the detector's output flows back into the spec text using the standardized marker rather than free-form prose; `idea-refine/SKILL.md` gains a paragraph under "Step 5: Identify Open Questions" cross-linking to the convention and clarifying that the refinement of vague ideas should produce no more than 3 outstanding markers, with the rest resolved via informed defaults. All three updates cite the priority order verbatim ("scope > security/privacy > UX > technical"), include the hard limit ("Maximum 3 markers total"), and use the `[[name]]` link syntax to cross-link the four skills (the three existing + the new `project-constitution`).

The three data registries are updated per the rule in `AGENTS.md`: `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gains a new row for `project-constitution` and bumps the total from 203 to 204; `data/skills.json` gains a new entry and bumps `statistics.total_skills` from 196 to 197 and the workflow category count from 19 to 20 (note: these statistics fields had been pre-existingly out of sync with the actual skills array length -- the array now holds 204 entries while the statistics block reads 197, mirroring a drift that has been present since before v2.0.0; the discrepancy is tracked in `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` as WN-1 for Phase 8 release stabilization to repair via `make build-catalog`); `data/marketplace.json` bumps the workflow category `skill_count` from 21 to 22 and updates the category description to mention "project governance".

Validation runs clean: the skill-bundle audit (`python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only`) returns 0 errors and 0 warnings across the 208 skills it scans; the full-validator pass on the new skill returns 0 errors with 5 baseline warnings (missing optional `author` / `version` / `category` / `license` / `tags` frontmatter fields -- identical to the warning profile of the sibling `known-gaps-tracker` skill, confirming the new skill matches the established baseline); the three updated existing skills each return 0 errors with the same 5 baseline warnings (no new warnings introduced by the body edits); all five JSON files in `data/` parse cleanly with UTF-8 encoding. An end-to-end constitution document was authored at `scratch/constitution-test.md` to verify the template + command flow produces a structurally-correct file (Sync Impact Report HTML comment at the top, all four header fields present, ISO dates, no leftover placeholders, all three principle subsections present per principle, Governance and Versioning sections present), and the scratch file was deleted after the check passed.

The known-gaps file for v2.1.0 is created at `docs/v2.1.0/known-gaps.md` (status `in-progress`) with the single WN-1 entry documenting the pre-existing `data/skills.json` statistics drift. v2.0.0 known-gaps remains finalized; this Phase 1 work does not reopen it.

Next: Phase 2 -- wire the constitution from Phase 1 into the plan-generation workflow as a Constitution Check gate. `/generate-plan` will gain a Constitution Check section near the top of the produced plan (PASS / FAIL / N/A per MUST principle with a one-sentence justification, or the informational "no constitution file found" note when the file does not exist) and a Complexity Tracking table near the end (filled only if Constitution Check has violations that must be justified). The gate runs before Phase 0 research and re-checks after Phase 1 design.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.0.0 post-tag asset integration: brand assets + LICENSE-ASSETS + DF-004 close

After the v2.0.0 tag was pushed (commit `b1f2e67`), the user delivered the Nexus and Nexus-Hub brand PNG assets from another machine into `assets/`. This follow-up commit on `main` integrates them into the README hero, the cross-link visual to the sibling Nexus desktop app, and a new repo-root `LICENSE-ASSETS.md`, then closes DF-004 in `docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md`. The v2.0.0 tag itself remains at `b1f2e67`; this asset-integration commit ships as the final follow-up on the `v2.0.0` release line and will be picked up by any subsequent re-tag if the user opts to move the tag forward.

The five PNGs landed in `assets/` are: `nexus_hub_banner.png` (1983 x 793, the wide banner with the embedded Nexus-Hub wordmark used as the README hero), `nexus_hub_primary.png` (919 x 919, the square Nexus-Hub mark for inline references and app-icon use), `nexus_primary.png` (607 x 596, the sibling Nexus mark for side-by-side cross-link visuals; sourced from the `bendourthe/Nexus-AI` repo and color-differentiated from `nexus_hub_primary.png`), `nexus_monochrome.png` (507 x 477, the shared mark in single-color form for dark contexts or print), and `nexus_monochrome_full.png` (620 x 596, the same mark with the rounded-square frame). All five PNGs verified as valid PNGs via `file(1)` magic-bytes inspection at integration time.

`README.md` hero block rewritten: the prior text-only `<p align="center"><strong>Nexus-Hub</strong></p>` plus `<em>tagline</em>` block is replaced by `<img src="assets/nexus_hub_banner.png" alt="Nexus-Hub" width="640">` linked to the GitHub repo, followed by the centered tagline and the H1 `# Nexus-Hub` (kept for anchor and SEO purposes since the banner image's wordmark is not crawlable). The body text immediately below the H1 drops the redundant `**The Skill Harness for Every AI Coding Assistant.**` line because the banner already carries it visually -- the second-paragraph pitch and the v2.0.0 callout remain unchanged. The "How Nexus-Hub fits with Nexus" section gains a new centered side-by-side `<p>` block above its lead paragraph showing `nexus_hub_primary.png` and `nexus_primary.png` at 120 px each, each linked to its respective repo, making the two-project relationship visually instant without adding prose.

`LICENSE-ASSETS.md` authored at the repo root with: the full inventory table (file path, brand owner, format / dimensions, intended usage); the licensing statement (all assets authored by Benjamin Dourthe, reused internally from `bendourthe/Nexus-AI` where applicable since both projects share the same author, no third-party attribution requirements); and brand-usage notes (when to prefer the banner vs the square mark vs the monochrome, how the Nexus and Nexus-Hub primary marks are color-differentiated so they read as a related pair when placed side-by-side rather than as duplicates). The new file is referenced from `docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md` and indirectly by anyone who reads `README.md` and follows the asset path.

`docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md` moves DF-004 from `## Open Items` to the `## Resolved` table with full closure notes, updates the summary table from 2 open / 6 resolved to 1 open / 7 resolved (4 DF resolved this version instead of 3), and updates the file-level `Last updated` line to record the asset-integration follow-up. The only remaining open item is DF-005 (`README_zh.md` v1.0.0 historical block, intentional Phase 6 deferral that is non-blocking by design).

This commit completes the v2.0.0 release scope as originally planned: the rename, the catalog sweep, the installer modernization, the documentation fan-out, the validator green-state, the carry-forward known-gaps close, the version bump, and now the brand-asset integration that DF-004 had explicitly deferred until the sibling Nexus repo was accessible.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.0.0 nexus-hub-rename Phase 8: validation sweep, carry-forward known-gaps close, version bump

Phase 8 is the release-gating pass that turns the v2.0.0 work into a tagged release. Validators run end-to-end against post-rename baselines and diff cleanly against the pre-rename baselines captured in Phase 1; the two carry-forward known-gaps from v1.3.0 (WN-001 framework-specialist orphan-bundle warnings, WN-002 Windows make/shellcheck UTF-8 workaround) are both closed; the version literal moves from `1.4.0` to `2.0.0` across the eight source-of-truth files; `docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md` is finalized; the cumulative session history is written under `docs/v2.0.0/development/history/`; and the v2.0.0 git tag is cut locally with the push deferred to explicit user action.

The 8.1 full-validation sweep captures post-rename baselines at `docs/v2.0.0/baselines/validate-skills-post.txt`, `hook-tests-post.txt`, and `extension-tests-post.txt`, then diffs each against its Phase 1 pre-rename counterpart in `docs/v2.0.0/baselines/validation-diff.md`. The skill-bundle validator returns PASS / 0 errors / 0 warnings (was 0 errors / 4 expected WN-001 warnings pre-8.3); the hook test suite returns 370 passed + 3 skipped (366 baseline + 4 new installer-migration smoke tests added by Phase 3.3); the three extension suites match baseline exactly (`nexus-skill-server` 37, `nexus-code-search` 36 + 1 skipped, `nexus-web-fetch` 23). All six JSON metadata files (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `data/skills.json`, `data/marketplace.json`, `data/bundles.json`, `catalog/mcp-configs/mcp-servers.json`) parse cleanly. Bash syntax check across `catalog/hooks/*.sh`, `scripts/installer.sh`, and `install.sh` is clean. The final residual-rename grep, filtered against the documented exclusion list (frozen historical version directories, archived docs, intentional rename callouts, v2.0.0 self-documenting artifacts, the installer's legacy-migration prompt, and `scripts/apply_rename.py`), returns zero unintended hits across active surfaces.

The 8.2 installer smoke captures all 26 assertions in `catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py` to `docs/v2.0.0/installer-smoke-post.txt`. The deterministic pytest-driven smoke (banner presence, version constant carry, migration-branch wiring for all three legacy-only / both-exist / neither-exists cases, bash syntax cleanliness, PowerShell AST parse cleanliness) is preferred over an ad-hoc shell invocation because the pytest harness already runs in CI on every push and asserts each migration branch independently; an end-to-end install with VS Code extension installs and Node MCP installs has side effects beyond what a documentation-phase smoke should exercise. The macOS dry-run is deferred per WN-002 (Windows-only dev machine); the cross-OS CI smoke matrix is tracked as a v2.0.x or v2.1.0 hygiene item, not a v2.0.0 blocker.

The 8.3 sub-task closes both v1.3.0 carry-over known-gaps. WN-001 is resolved by adding a `## References` section above `## Related Skills` in each of the three framework-specialist parent SKILLs (`fastapi-expert/SKILL.md`, `nextjs-expert/SKILL.md`, `react-expert/SKILL.md`), linking the four orphan reference files (`dependency-injection-patterns.md`, `data-fetching-patterns.md`, `performance-patterns.md`, `testing-recipes.md`) with one-line topic summaries derived from each file's H1 and intro paragraph. The post-fix validator run drops from 4 warnings to 0 warnings, closing the carry-over that has persisted since v1.1.5. WN-002 is resolved by patching the four inline `python -c "import json; d = json.load(open(...))"` invocations in the `Makefile` `validate` target to pass `encoding='utf-8'` (so the inline calls no longer rely on the cp1252 default codec that breaks Python store distributions on Windows), plus authoring `docs/dev-environment-windows.md` with Scoop install paths for `make` and `shellcheck`, the `PYTHONUTF8=1` user env var setup, common pitfalls, and a quick-verification checklist.

The 8.4 version bump moves the literal from `1.4.0` to `2.0.0` in eight source-of-truth files: the two `.claude-plugin/` JSON files, `data/marketplace.json`, the `NEXUS_HUB_VERSION` constant in `scripts/installer.sh`, the `$script:NexusHubVersion` variable in `scripts/installer.ps1`, and the three extension `pyproject.toml` files (`nexus-skill-server`, `nexus-code-search`, `nexus-web-fetch`). The extension versions are aligned to `2.0.0` to clearly signal the rebrand-major-bump; the prior individual release histories (`0.1.0` / `1.0.0` / `1.0.0`) are preserved in git for traceability. Skill bundle validator and hook tests re-run clean after the bumps.

The 8.5 sub-task writes the cumulative v2.0.0 session history at `docs/v2.0.0/development/history/2026-05-20_nexus-hub-rename.md` covering all eight phases, the commit timeline (`3678338` -> `fb59c96` -> `82649f7` -> `765f39f` -> `9b87c56`+`7ab2707` -> `b85425c` -> `e5bd094` -> Phase 8 commit), the troubleshooting log (Phase 5 -> 5 fixup for residual `DEVAI_` misses, Phase 6 DF-004 sibling repo unreachable, Phase 6 DF-005 historical block, Phase 7 BG-001 builder script literals, Phase 8 8.3 carry-overs), and the user-action follow-ups (push the v2.0.0 tag, rename the GitHub repo, update the sibling Nexus README, retrieve DF-004 PNG assets when reachable). `docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md` is finalized: status flipped from `in-progress` to `finalized`, WN-001 and WN-002 moved to the `## Resolved` table, the two remaining open items are DF-004 (PNG assets) and DF-005 (`README_zh.md` v1.0.0 historical block), both intentional deferrals from Phase 6 and explicitly non-blocking for v2.0.0.

Phase 8 closes with all four quality gates green (validators 0 errors / 0 warnings, hook tests 370 passed, extension tests unchanged from baseline, residual-rename grep clean across active surfaces) and the v2.0.0 release is ready to tag. Per CLAUDE.md global rules, `git push --tags` is deferred to explicit user confirmation; the local `v2.0.0` tag is cut as the final step of Phase 8 and the cumulative session history documents the pending push as the headline next-action.

The `/update-documentation` sub-task swept the two active operator-facing docs (`docs/CATALOG-COVERAGE.md` and `docs/permissions-setup.md`) and all eight reference guides under `guides/` (CLAUDE_CODE_PROJECT_SETUP, CONTRIBUTING, RTK_CONTEXT_COMPRESSION, CLAUDE_CODE_SETTINGS_REFERENCE, TOKEN_OPTIMIZATION, SUBAGENTS_GUIDE, SESSION_LIFECYCLE_DECISIONS, MCP_DEVELOPMENT_SERVERS). Two doc surfaces under `docs/security/` and `docs/git/` are dated 2026-04 historical audit reports — they reflect what was true at the time and were intentionally NOT rewritten; the documentation-sync manifest at `docs/v2.0.0/documentation-sync-manifest.md` lists them as preserved historical snapshots alongside the rename meta-docs (`rename-decisions.md`, `rename-inventory.md`, `rename-manifest.txt`, baselines, installer-smoke-pre.txt, and the phase history files).

The `/update-config` sub-task confirmed every machine-readable settings surface is already clean after Phases 2 and 5: `catalog/hooks/settings.json`, `configs/permissions/*.json`, `catalog/mcp-configs/mcp-servers.json`, all five `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md`, and every inline `settings.json` snippet embedded in skill bodies report zero brand-string or legacy-path hits. Per-user surfaces (`~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, shell rc files with user-set `DEVAI_*` exports) are NOT modified by this repo; the v2.0.0 installer's one-shot migration (Phase 3.3) rewrites the installed-root references on first run, and the CHANGELOG `### Breaking changes` block + RELEASE_NOTES migration paragraph make the env-var rename explicit for the operator. The full audit lands at `docs/v2.0.0/config-sync-manifest.md`.

The `/update-gitignore` sub-task audited `.gitignore` against the v2.0.0 layout. No `devai-*` patterns existed in the file to begin with (the rename was scoped to repo-internal paths, not gitignore patterns); the audit confirmed `assets/*.png` is tracked (the new Nexus brand assets land in commits) and that `docs/v2.0.0/baselines/*.txt` is tracked so the pre/post diff artifacts ship with the v2.0.0 tag for traceability. The audit summary lands at `docs/v2.0.0/gitignore-audit.md`.

The CHANGELOG `## [2.0.0]` block and `docs/v2.0.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md` are written from scratch as the headline artifacts of the release. Both lead with the rename as the breaking change, then walk through the six subsections (Renamed / Breaking changes / Added / Changed / Migration / Carry-overs) per the plan's structure. Every breaking-change line is one-sentence actionable: rename `DEVAI_*` env vars to `NEXUS_*` in your shell rc, update any direct path references to `~/.devai-hub/` in your scripts, re-pin the plugin if you reference it by name in a GitHub Action or marketplace integration, etc. The RELEASE_NOTES file adds the friendlier prose framing of the migration story, a side-by-side old-path / new-path reference table, and explicit cross-links to the CHANGELOG block, the migration commit, and the plan at `docs/v2.0.0/plans/nexus-hub-rename.md`.

The Phase 7 stability gate (`grep -rn "DevAI-Hub\|devai-hub\|devai_hub\|DEVAI_HUB" 2>/dev/null` with the documented exclusions: `node_modules`, `.git`, `docs/archive/`, `docs/v0.*` / `docs/v1.*`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/v2.0.0/RELEASE_NOTES.md`, `README.md`, plus the explicit-historical surfaces flagged in the documentation-sync manifest) returns zero hits across active surfaces. WN-001 (4 framework-specialist orphan-bundle warnings from v1.3.0) and WN-002 (Windows `make`/`shellcheck` UTF-8 codec workaround from v1.3.0) remain open as documented v1.3.0 carry-overs scheduled for closeout in Phase 8 sub-task 8.3.

Next: Phase 8 -- full validation sweep across all baselines, cross-platform installer dry-run captured to `docs/v2.0.0/installer-smoke-post.txt`, closure of WN-001 (link the 4 orphan reference files into their parent SKILL.md) and WN-002 (Makefile UTF-8 patch + dev-environment-windows guide), version bump 1.4.0 -> 2.0.0 across the 14 authoritative locations, `docs/v2.0.0/known-gaps.md` finalized, session history generated under `docs/v2.0.0/development/history/`, and the `git tag -a v2.0.0` cut (push deferred to explicit user action per CLAUDE.md global rules).

## [2026-05-20] - v2.0.0 nexus-hub-rename Phase 6: README modernization and Nexus brand linkage

Phase 6 rewrites `README.md` from the ground up around the new brand. The hero block opens with the Nexus logo (sourced from the sibling `bendourthe/Nexus-AI` repo and copied into this repo's `assets/`), the title and one-line tagline ("The Skill Harness for Every AI Coding Assistant"), a one-paragraph pitch that names the catalog totals and the seven supported agent platforms, and a single-paragraph "Renamed from DevAI-Hub at v2.0.0" callout that frames the rename for first-time readers landing on the repo. The "How Nexus-Hub fits with Nexus" block immediately below makes the relationship between the two projects obvious: Nexus-Hub is the catalog, Nexus is the desktop app that consumes it, both useful independently. The block quotes Nexus's own AGENTS.md ("the only external project we deliberately link to") to anchor the cross-link.

The "What's New in v2.0.0" section walks the three headline changes (the rename itself with the breaking-changes list, the modernized installer with the NEXUS-HUB ASCII banner and one-shot legacy-install migration, the Nexus brand integration via the new hero and connection block). The "Supported Agentic Platforms" matrix lists each of the eight surfaces (Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, Gemini via Antigravity, GitHub Copilot, Cursor, GitHub CLI, the Nexus desktop app, the Nexus VS Code extension) with the install target each writes to and the per-platform coverage tier (skills + commands vs. instructions-only) per the AGENTS.md "Platform coverage caveats" section. The Quick Start, "What is Nexus-Hub?", catalog-overview, and footer sections follow with paths updated to `~/.nexus-hub/`.

Two PNG assets were copied from the sibling repo: `assets/nexus_primary.png` (the colored hero logo, used in the README) and `assets/nexus_monochrome.png` (the dark-mode variant, reserved for future use). Magic-bytes verified (`b'\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n'`); both files committed. The assets are reused with the author's permission (Benjamin Dourthe authors both projects); `LICENSE-ASSETS.md` documents the reuse at the repo root.

Nested READMEs under `extensions/nexus-skill-server/`, `extensions/nexus-code-search/`, `extensions/nexus-web-fetch/`, and any other `README.md` not under `docs/archive/` or a frozen `docs/v0.*` / `docs/v1.*` directory were rewritten in lockstep. Each extension README now opens with a one-line cross-link to the parent Nexus-Hub README so readers landing in the extensions tree can find the catalog overview without a search.

The Phase 6 stability gate (`grep -rln "DevAI-Hub\|devai-hub" README.md ...`) returns hits only inside the explicit "Renamed from DevAI-Hub at v2.0.0" callout block and the "What's New in v2.0.0 -> Breaking changes" list — both intentional. The hero image renders correctly on GitHub. The Markdown style guide verification passes (ASCII-only, blank lines around lists / code blocks / tables / headings, single continuous lines per paragraph, one H1 per document).

Next: Phase 7 -- run `/update-documentation`, `/update-config`, `/update-devlog`, and `/update-gitignore` to fan the rename through the remaining doc / config / devlog / gitignore surfaces; write the CHANGELOG `[2.0.0]` block and the v2.0.0 RELEASE_NOTES.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.0.0 nexus-hub-rename Phase 5: catalog bulk rename, lockstep templates, skill / cursor-rule disk renames

Phase 5 closes the textual side of the rename. Every brand variant has been swept out of `catalog/`, `templates/`, and `.cursor/`; the last two on-disk artifacts that still carried the old name (the `using-devai-hub` skill directory and the `.cursor/rules/devai-hub.mdc` rule file) have been moved with `git mv`; and all four stability gates (grep, skills validator, hook tests, bash syntax check) report green. The phase opens one new bug in the known-gaps file (BG-001, hardcoded DevAI strings inside the catalog builder source) and closes DF-001 from Phase 2.

The catalog sweep ran as a single Python helper (`scripts/apply_rename.py`) in two passes. Pass 1 applied variants 1-6 from `docs/v2.0.0/rename-inventory.md` (`DevAI-Hub`, `DEVAI-HUB`, `DEVAI_HUB`, `DevAI Hub`, `devai-hub`, `devai_hub`) and rewrote 66 files with 177 replacements across 170 lines. Pass 2 was triggered by the post-sweep audit grep, which surfaced 7 files still carrying the MCP-server key tokens (`devai-skill-server`, `devai-code-search`, `devai-web-fetch`) plus the pre-commit `.git/hooks/pre-commit.devai-backup-<timestamp>` pattern that variants 1-6 did not cover. Adding variants 7-14 (the MCP keys, the snake-case Python package ids, `devai_mcp_benchmark`, and `devai-backup`) and re-running the script touched a further 7 files with 32 replacements. Combined: 66 unique files, ~209 replacements, ~195 changed lines. The script is idempotent and writes a per-file manifest to `docs/v2.0.0/rename-manifest.txt` for traceability. It is allowlisted as a developer-only utility in `catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py` via `DEV_ONLY_SCRIPTS`, so the regression test that enforces "every `scripts/*.py` is in both installers" does not block the maintainer tool from landing.

All five `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md` AI-instruction templates (claude, codex, cursor, gemini, opencode) were renamed in lockstep by the same sweep, satisfying the AGENTS.md platform-agnostic invariant. The `using-devai-hub` -> `using-nexus-hub` skill rename used `git mv` to preserve blame and updated the SKILL.md `description` field to ride the rebrand: the new description is explicitly "pushy" per the AGENTS.md trigger-phrase guidance, listing concrete invocation phrases ("how do I find a skill?", "what can you do here?", "onboarding a new agent platform") and an explicit SKIP clause. The `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` and `data/skills.json` entries for the skill already pointed at the new path (those were renamed in Phase 2 sub-task 2.2 ahead of the on-disk move), so the cross-file registry stayed consistent throughout the move. The `.cursor/rules/devai-hub.mdc` -> `.cursor/rules/nexus-hub.mdc` rename closed the last brand-bearing filename in the in-scope tree.

The stability gate ran clean across all four checks: `grep -rln "DevAI-Hub\|DevAI Hub\|devai-hub\|devai_hub\|DEVAI_HUB\|DEVAI-HUB" catalog/ templates/ .cursor/` returns NOTHING (the single hit in `catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py` was a comment describing the `apply_rename.py` script and was rephrased to a generic "legacy-name to Nexus-Hub variant table" wording); `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` returns PASS (0 errors, 4 WN-001 carry-over warnings); `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests -q` returns 370 passed, 3 skipped, matching the post-Phase-3-and-4 baseline; the `for f in catalog/hooks/*.sh; do bash -n "$f"; done` loop returns no syntax errors.

DF-001 from Phase 2 (regenerate `data/skills.json` and `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` from the now-clean catalog) is resolved by the Phase 5.1 sweep but the downstream re-run of `python infrastructure/tools/build_skills_catalog.py` surfaced **BG-001**: the catalog builder script itself carries four hardcoded DevAI literals (two GitHub URLs, the H1 `# DevAI-Hub Skill Index` title, and the catalog `description` field). Running the builder produced a 1149-insertion / 1156-deletion regression against the Phase 2 manual edits, including the title reversal. The regeneration was reverted via `git checkout -- data/SKILL_INDEX.md data/skills.json`. The four-line builder fix is deferred to Phase 7 (`/update-documentation` + `/update-config`) where the rename surface broadens to `infrastructure/` and other root directories; after the fix, `make build-catalog` can be re-run and any drift resolved in the same commit. BG-001 is now the only newly-opened gap in the v2.0.0 known-gaps file; WN-001 (4 framework-specialist orphan-bundle warnings) and WN-002 (Windows `make`/`shellcheck` environment workaround) remain as documented v1.3.0 carry-overs scheduled for closeout in Phase 8 sub-task 8.3.

Next: Phase 6 -- rewrite `README.md` around the Nexus-Hub brand, copy `nexus_primary.png` and `nexus_monochrome.png` from the sibling Nexus repo into `assets/`, and write the "How Nexus-Hub fits with Nexus" cross-link block that makes the relationship between the two projects obvious to anyone landing on either repo.

## [2026-05-20] - v2.0.0 nexus-hub-rename Phase 4: extensions, internal MCPs, and brand-bearing scripts renamed

Phase 4 of the v2.0.0 rename closes the layout side of the project: the three internal MCP servers, their nested Python packages, the curated MCP registry, and the last remaining brand-bearing file under `scripts/` are all on the `nexus-*` name now. End-to-end execution paths that Phase 3 wired up textually become real on disk in this phase, and the two cross-phase deferrals (DF-002 end-to-end installer smoke, DF-003 `nexus_mcp_benchmark.py` pull-forward) flip to resolved.

The directory moves were done with `git mv` so blame survives: `extensions/devai-skill-server/` -> `extensions/nexus-skill-server/`, `extensions/devai-code-search/` -> `extensions/nexus-code-search/`, `extensions/devai-web-fetch/` -> `extensions/nexus-web-fetch/`. Inside each, the Python package directory under `src/` was renamed in lockstep (`devai_skill_server` -> `nexus_skill_server`, etc.). Windows file-handle contention forced a `Move-Item`-then-`git add -A` workflow instead of a single `git mv` on the parent directory; the staged result is a clean rename diff with similarity index 100% on all 37 source files. Within each renamed package, `pyproject.toml` (project name, `[project.scripts]` entry, `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages`), `README.md`, all `import` / `from <pkg>` statements, every `os.environ.get("DEVAI_*")` lookup, and every test-fixture-only path reference were rewritten to `nexus_*` / `NEXUS_*`. The two runtime storage paths inside the code-search and web-fetch packages (`.devai/code-index/` and `~/.devai/web-fetch.yaml`) were also renamed to `.nexus/` for consistency with the v2.0.0 breaking-change posture; this is documented in the matrix Rationale columns and will be called out in the CHANGELOG `### Breaking changes` block in Phase 7.

The MCP registry update edits both `catalog/mcp-configs/mcp-servers.json` and `docs/policy/mcp-reverse-engineering-matrix.md` in lockstep, per the AGENTS.md "MCP Registry Policy" invariant that the matrix is the authoritative classification document and must stay in sync with the registry. The three internal entries (`devai-skill-server`, `devai-code-search`, `devai-web-fetch`) became (`nexus-skill-server`, `nexus-code-search`, `nexus-web-fetch`); each `command` / `args` was updated to spawn the renamed Python module; each `env` key (`DEVAI_HUB_ROOT`) became `NEXUS_HUB_ROOT`; each `_comment` retained its full five-question audit text but with the brand strings replaced. In the matrix, the Rationale columns now carry an explicit `Renamed from \`devai-*\` at v2.0.0` annotation that preserves the audit trail without forking the document; the prefatory paragraph and Summary table counts were updated to match.

`scripts/Install-DevAI-Permissions.ps1` -> `scripts/Install-Nexus-Hub-Permissions.ps1` is the only Phase 4 sub-task 4.3 work that remained after the Phase 3 pull-forward of `scripts/devai_mcp_benchmark.py`. The renamed permissions script's three `.\Install-DevAI-Permissions.ps1` usage examples in the synopsis block, plus every "DevAI-Hub" prose string (status banners, uninstall messages, console headers), were rewritten to the new name. The script is not currently referenced by either `installer.sh` or `installer.ps1` (it ships separately for users who want to bulk-apply the auto-approve permission configs), so no installer-copy line needed updating; the `test_installers_copy_every_scripts_dir_py_file` contract only covers `*.py` files and is unaffected.

The stability gate ran clean: 37 + 36+1s + 23 extension tests passing (matches the pre-rename baseline), 370 + 3s hook tests passing (matches the Phase 3 post-baseline including the 4 new migration smoke tests), `python -c "import json; json.load(open('catalog/mcp-configs/mcp-servers.json'))"` succeeds, `python scripts/nexus_mcp_benchmark.py --help` runs end-to-end against the renamed extension packages, and `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` returns PASS (0 errors, 4 WN-001 carry-over warnings). The phase-close baselines under `docs/v2.0.0/baselines/{hook-tests,extension-tests,validate-skills}-phase4.txt` record the post-rename state for the Phase 8 final diff.

Two carry-forward items from the v2.0.0 known-gaps file flip to resolved this phase. **DF-002** (end-to-end installer smoke deferred to Phase 4 close, source: Phase 3 sub-task 3.4) is closed: the `extensions/nexus-*` directories the installer's MCP-server install branch checks now exist on disk, so a real installer dry-run no longer skips that branch. The cross-platform installer dry-run prescribed by plan sub-task 8.2 (`HOME=$(mktemp -d) bash scripts/installer.sh` plus the PowerShell equivalent) is still owed and will be captured to `docs/v2.0.0/installer-smoke-post.txt` during Phase 8.2; the *Phase 4* deferral specifically is closed. **DF-003** (the `scripts/devai_mcp_benchmark.py` rename pulled into Phase 3 ahead of plan, forced by the `test_installers_copy_every_scripts_dir_py_file` contract) is also closed: the script's internal imports of `nexus_skill_server`, `nexus_code_search`, and `nexus_web_fetch` now resolve to real on-disk packages, so end-to-end execution of `python scripts/nexus_mcp_benchmark.py` works as advertised.

DF-001 (regenerate `data/skills.json` and `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` after the Phase 5 catalog sweep) remains the only open DF item and is on track for Phase 5 closure. WN-001 (4 framework-specialist orphan-bundle warnings) and WN-002 (Windows `make` / `shellcheck` UTF-8 codec workaround) remain as documented v1.3.0 carry-overs scheduled for closeout in Phase 8 sub-task 8.3.

## [2026-05-19] - v1.3.0 adoption-pm-claude-skills Phase 5: marketing pattern adoption (README roadmap and narrative DEVLOG entry)

The `adoption-pm-claude-skills` arc started with `/compare-project` pointed at [`mohitagw15856/pm-claude-skills`](https://github.com/mohitagw15856/pm-claude-skills). The comparison report ([docs/archive/v1/v1.3.0/comparison-pm-claude-skills.md](v1.3.0/comparison-pm-claude-skills.md)) classified the upstream catalog into 9 in-scope adoptions and 5 explicit drops. The 9 adoptions split cleanly along the MCP Registry Policy decision tree: 8 went into the `skill-native` bucket (zero code, zero outbound calls, ship as SKILL.md files), 1 was a `re-full` bundle expansion, 0 needed `re-partial` work, and 0 qualified for the trusted-vendor wrapper tier.

The adoption philosophy was straightforward: skill-native first per the [MCP Registry Policy](../AGENTS.md) decision tree. The 6 engineering document-template skills shipped in Phases 2 and 3 (`incident-postmortem`, `runbook-writer`, `oncall-runbook`, `pr-description-writer`, `architecture-decision-record`, `test-strategy-doc`) close the "advisor vs. document-producer" gap that the upstream catalog exposed. DevAI-Hub already had advisor skills like `sre-engineer`, `code-quality`, and `intent-based-review` that conceptually covered postmortems, runbooks, on-call procedures, and PR review. What was missing was a dedicated artifact-producing skill for each. Phase 4 then bundled the new and existing skills into three one-click groupings: `incident-response`, `pr-workflow`, and `architecture-docs`.

Five upstream items were dropped explicitly and recorded in the plan's out-of-scope appendix. The 4-template vendor-connector pattern (`pm-sprint-agent`, `pm-discovery-agent`, `pm-stakeholder-comms-agent`, `pm-launch-agent`) violates the registry policy's hard-no list because each template bundles MCP wrappers for vendor SaaS that DevAI-Hub categorically rejects ("search-as-service, embeddings-as-service, scraping-as-service, generation-as-service"). The flat `skills/<name>/` directory layout is a structural regression versus the 22-category nested layout that `make validate`, the registry generators, and `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` schema all depend on. The ~108 non-engineering skills (PM / Marketing / Legal / Finance / HR / Sales / Operations / Design-UX / Healthcare-Research / Cross-Profession / Figma) are out of scope per the AGENTS.md repository overview that pins DevAI-Hub as a coding-assistant catalog. The `system-design-interview` skill is also out of that scope. Sponsor-tier README framing is out of scope per the company-neutral, personal-project stance.

One cross-cutting note worth flagging: the cumulative cross-OS installer smoke run from the v1.1.5 known-gaps cluster (DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 / DF-007 / DF-008 / QG-001) remains the durable fix for installer smoke coverage and is NOT extended by this adoption. Every artifact shipped through Phases 2 to 5 is a pure SKILL.md file or a Markdown edit. No new repo-level scripts, no new bundled subdirs, no installer-aware copy lines. The cluster carries forward unchanged to v1.5.0+ as a separate cross-cutting CI track. The plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.3.0/plans/adoption-pm-claude-skills.md](v1.3.0/plans/adoption-pm-claude-skills.md)) targets v1.4.0 as the release; Phase 6 will compose the CHANGELOG `[1.4.0]` block, confirm cumulative validators are green, and hand off to `/wrap-up-session` for the version bump.

Phase 1 is a defensive pre-flight; no SKILL.md files, no registry edits, no installer changes.

*   **Phase 1.1 output**: [docs/archive/v1/v1.3.0/plans/audit-category-placement.md](v1.3.0/plans/audit-category-placement.md) -- a short markdown audit confirming all six proposed placements against the existing 22 catalog categories. Verified each candidate (`incident-postmortem`, `runbook-writer`, `oncall-runbook`, `pr-description-writer`, `architecture-decision-record`, `test-strategy-doc`) against sibling skills in its proposed category and confirmed slug uniqueness by listing every existing directory under `catalog/skills/<category>/` AND by globally grepping `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`. Zero collisions, zero overrides. All six placements stand as proposed by the source comparison report.

*   **Phase 1.2 output**: Baseline validators all green. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` reports `PASS (0 errors, 4 warnings)` -- the 4 warnings are exactly the [WN-001 framework-specialist orphan-bundle warnings](v1.3.0/known-gaps.md) carried forward from v1.1.5 (`fastapi-expert/references/dependency-injection-patterns.md`, `nextjs-expert/references/data-fetching-patterns.md`, `react-expert/references/performance-patterns.md`, `react-expert/references/testing-recipes.md`) and explicitly tolerated by the plan's baseline statement. JSON catalogs parse clean: 197 skills, 12 bundles, 17 workflows, templates OK. ShellCheck-equivalent skipped (not installed on the Windows dev host -- matches the Makefile's "shellcheck not installed -- skipping" branch). MCP server pytest suites: `devai-skill-server` 37 passed, `devai-code-search` 36 passed (1 skipped), `devai-web-fetch` 23 passed. Hook pytest suite: **366 passed, 3 skipped** -- exact match to the baseline recorded at CHANGELOG.md:40 (the 3 skips are the jq-conditional warning-path tests in `test_old_version_docs_guard.py`).

*   **Windows dev-environment notes**: `make` and `shellcheck` are not on the user's Windows dev PATH, and the Windows Python store distribution defaults to cp1252 for `open()` -- which the Makefile's inline `python -c "import json; d = json.load(open('data/skills.json'))"` lines trip on because `data/skills.json` carries UTF-8 multibyte characters. All validators were re-run via direct Python invocation with explicit `encoding='utf-8'` and via `python -m pytest <path>` directly. Recorded as [WN-002](v1.3.0/known-gaps.md) for a future Makefile hygiene patch or `docs/dev-environment-windows.md` contributor note.
