## [2026-05-12] - Cross-surface description format: single-line `# Description:` prefix replaces the 4-line box

*   **Goal**: Make the description that DevAI-Hub injects into shell-tool approval dialogs render legibly on every agentic surface, not just the VS Code Claude Code extension and the Claude Code terminal. The four-line `# ===== Description ===== #` box that v1.1.0 introduced renders beautifully on surfaces that unescape `\n` (VS Code extension, terminal), but on surfaces that show the tool input as raw JSON (notably the Claude Desktop app, screenshotted by the user) the same `\n` characters become literal escape text and the box turns into unreadable noise. The fix needs to be universal: a single format that degrades gracefully on every surface without relying on any specific JSON-rendering behavior.

*   **Diagnosis**: The `description` field is the only output channel that **every** approval-dialog surface renders cleanly (as a subtitle line under the command). The `command` field is shown by every surface, but its `\n` rendering varies. The `permissionDecisionReason` field is newer Claude Code surface-only. The previous hook design leaned on the `command` field's `\n` rendering for the description, which is the channel with the widest cross-surface variance. The right architecture is to lean on the `description` field (universal channel) and degrade the `command`-field prefix to a shape that works on every surface.

*   **Design decision**: Replace the four-line box with a `# Description: <text>\n___\n<command>` shape. The single-line prefix plus the `___` separator means: even on raw-JSON surfaces, the first readable token is still `# Description: <text>` followed by an underscore divider that's visually clear; on Markdown-rendering surfaces the `___` becomes a horizontal rule. The same text is mirrored to `updatedInput.description` (universal channel) and, for PowerShell, to `permissionDecisionReason` (the v1.1.0 visible-body channel for Claude Code's PowerShell dialog). Two `\n` characters total in the command-field prepend instead of four. Tokens saved roughly 10-20 per non-auto-approved shell call.

*   **Options considered and rejected**:
    *   **Surface-adaptive env detection** - no reliable cross-surface signal exists, and the matrix of (surface x platform x version) makes the maintenance burden too large.
    *   **Aggressive newline collapse on the command body** - breaks PowerShell here-strings (`@'...'@`) and Bash heredocs (`<<EOF`), which are exactly the multi-line commands users most want descriptions for (commit messages, file content).
    *   **Drop the in-command prefix entirely** - loses the in-transcript co-location of intent and command in chat history. Marginal benefit; the `description` field alone is enough for the live dialog but the inline `# Description:` line gives future LLM context a cleaner read.
    *   **Spill multi-line bodies to temp files** - aggressive UX change for low payoff; here-strings and heredocs are syntactically meaningful PowerShell / Bash constructs, not a wart to engineer around.

*   **Implementation**:
    *   **`catalog/hooks/format-bash-description.py`**: removed `_BOX_HEADER` / `_BOX_FOOTER` / `_MIN_CONTENT_WIDTH` / `_MAX_CONTENT_WIDTH` constants and the `format_description_box(text, *, width=None)` helper that used `textwrap.wrap`. Replaced with `_PREFIX_MAX_LEN = 120` and `format_description_prefix(text)` that collapses runs of whitespace (including newlines and tabs) into single spaces, trims, truncates at 120 chars with `...` ellipsis. Dropped `shutil` and `textwrap` imports. `main()` updated: `prefix + "\n" + cleaned_command` (single newline) instead of `box + "\n\n" + cleaned_command` (four newlines). Description-field value is normalized to single line via the same helper.
    *   **`catalog/hooks/format-powershell-description.py`**: mirror change. **Preserved verbatim** the v1.1.0 safety contract: `permissionDecision: "ask"` is still emitted on every non-allowed command, and `permissionDecisionReason` still carries the full normalized description (untruncated; only the inline prefix is truncated). Updated comment block explaining the design.
    *   **`format_description_box` alias**: kept in both hook files as a one-release-cycle compatibility shim for any external Python caller. Will be removed in the next minor release.
    *   **`strip_description_box`**: extended to drop leading underscore-only separator lines (the new `___` divider) in addition to the existing `#`-prefixed-line and blank-line strips. Absorbs every shape the hook has shipped: the legacy 4-line box, the intermediate `# desc:` prefix, and the current `# Description: <text>\n___\n<command>` shape. Regression tests cover all three shapes plus underscore-separator widths from 2 to 40 chars.
    *   **`catalog/hooks/require-description.sh` and `catalog/hooks/require-powershell-description.sh`**: agent-facing blurb examples updated to show the single-line `# Description: <text>` prefix. The fallback regex extended from `^[[:space:]]*#.*Description` to `^[[:space:]]*#.*\(desc:\|description\)` so a command starting with either the new prefix or the legacy box header satisfies the require-description check.
    *   **`templates/ai-instructions/base-claude.md`**: replaced the two separate `MANDATORY` rules (one for Bash, one for PowerShell) with a single unified shell-tool rule covering Bash, PowerShell, `run_shell_command`, `shell`, etc. The Claude-specific footnote naming the four hooks (`format-bash-description.py`, `format-powershell-description.py`, `require-description.sh`, `require-powershell-description.sh`) is preserved at the end.
    *   **`templates/ai-instructions/base-codex.md`** and **`templates/ai-instructions/base-gemini.md`**: replaced the Bash-only `MANDATORY` rule with the unified rule (no hook footnote - those platforms do not run the Claude hooks by name).
    *   **`templates/ai-instructions/base-cursor.md`** and **`templates/ai-instructions/base-opencode.md`**: ADDED the unified rule into the `## Working Conventions` list. These two templates previously carried no Bash / PowerShell description guidance at all - this is new discipline for users on Cursor and OpenCode.
    *   **Tests**: `catalog/hooks/tests/test_format_bash_description.py` and `catalog/hooks/tests/test_format_powershell_description.py` rewrote `TestFormatDescriptionBox` -> `TestFormatDescriptionPrefix` with single-line shape assertions, empty-input placeholder, internal-whitespace collapse, and >120-char truncation. `TestStripDescriptionBox` updated to use the new prefix shape in round-trip tests; new `test_strips_legacy_box_format` regression guard ensures mid-conversation retries on legacy-formatted commands still strip cleanly. `TestMainIntegration` tests renamed and updated; in the PowerShell file, the load-bearing `permissionDecision == "ask"` and `permissionDecisionReason == "<description>"` assertions are preserved verbatim.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for the user beyond rerunning the installer. The new hooks land at the same paths and produce the same kind of approval-dialog output, just with a more compact and surface-portable prefix. Users on Cursor and OpenCode will see their agents start adding a `description` parameter to shell tool calls; this is the one new behavior that arrives with this change (those templates previously carried no rule).

*   **Verification**: 
    *   `make validate` clean (no `data/*.json` change).
    *   `make lint` clean (shell-script edits live inside quoted heredocs; no semantic change).
    *   `make test` green: `TestFormatDescriptionPrefix` (9 bash + 5 powershell), updated `TestStripDescriptionBox` (7 bash + 4 powershell including the new legacy-strip regression test), updated `TestMainIntegration`.
    *   Manual hook smoke: piped a synthetic payload with a 200-char description through both hooks; confirmed (a) `updatedInput.command` starts with `# Description: <truncated to 120 chars ending in ...>\n___\n`, (b) the body after the prefix and separator is byte-identical to the input command, (c) `updatedInput.command.count("\n") == 2 + original_command.count("\n")` (two newlines added: one after the prefix, one after the separator line), (d) for the PowerShell payload `permissionDecision == "ask"` and `permissionDecisionReason` carries the full untrimmed description.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: 
    *   The Claude Desktop app's raw-JSON rendering of the `command` field remains an upstream issue - this fix mitigates the symptom (description is always readable, prefix degrades gracefully) but does not eliminate it (a long multi-line `command` body still shows escaped `\n` characters in the Desktop dialog). The Desktop-app rendering bug is a separate workstream against `anthropics/claude-code`.
    *   The new "Cursor / OpenCode agents must provide a description" rule is added to those two platform templates for the first time. Agents on those platforms will start emitting descriptions on shell tool calls; if the agent forgets, the call still goes through (those platforms do not run the require-description hooks).

*   **Current Status**: ready for commit. Modified files: `catalog/hooks/format-bash-description.py`, `catalog/hooks/format-powershell-description.py`, `catalog/hooks/require-description.sh`, `catalog/hooks/require-powershell-description.sh`, `catalog/hooks/tests/test_format_bash_description.py`, `catalog/hooks/tests/test_format_powershell_description.py`, all five `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (`[Unreleased]`), `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry), plus the memory file `reference_powershell_dialog_rendering.md` appended with a Resolution subsection.


## [2026-05-11] - v1.2.0 release (cumulative close of the v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan)

*   **Goal**: Close out the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)) with Phase 7 (the optional `.skill` packager / A16) and then run the cumulative wrap-up that turns the seven `v1.2.0-wip:` commits into a v1.2.0 MINOR release. The plan ran as a single seven-phase content release per the user-supplied constraint that all phases ship together as v1.2.0; no intermediate version-string bumps anywhere across Phases 1-7.

*   **What shipped between v1.1.5 and v1.2.0**:
    *   **9 new skills** (catalog total 187 -> 196 user-facing skills): `doc-coauthoring` (Phase 2 / A9), `generative-art` / `theme-tokens` / `internal-comms` / `web-artifacts-builder` (Phase 4 / A1, A3, A8, A10), `skill-eval-loop` (Phase 5 / A6+A7), `brand-styling` / `mcp-builder` (Phase 6 / A2, A5).
    *   **4 new repo-level scripts**: `scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py`, `scripts/skill_eval_viewer.py`, `scripts/optimize_skill_description.py` (Phase 5), `scripts/package_skill.py` (Phase 7 / A16). All four registered explicitly in BOTH `scripts/installer.sh` AND `scripts/installer.ps1` per the AGENTS.md "Installer-Aware Changes" rule.
    *   **One new layout convention** (Phase 3 / A13): per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` subdirectories alongside SKILL.md. Documented in `AGENTS.md`, enforced by a new `--bundles-only` audit in `scripts/validate_skills.py`, wired into `make validate`, smoke-tested through both installer copy primitives.
    *   **Five doc-only edits** institutionalizing skill-authoring patterns (Phase 1): A14 pushy descriptions, A17 three-tier loading model, A15 500-line SKILL.md target, A11 aesthetic-distinctiveness lens, A12 static-poster workflow.
    *   **One CLI-agnostic parity invariant** ported to a new file (Phase 5 / A7): `scripts/optimize_skill_description.py` carries the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent for claude / gemini / codex / opencode dispatch with no cross-CLI fallback, enforced by `catalog/hooks/tests/test_eval_loop.py::TestEvalLoopCLIAdapter`.
    *   **One delivery channel opened** (Phase 7 / A16): the `.skill` ZIP archive format produced by `scripts/package_skill.py` lets users distribute DevAI-Hub-shaped skills to Claude.ai and the Anthropic API skill-upload endpoint.
    *   **36 new pytest cases** across three new modules: 8 in `test_skill_bundles.py` (Phase 3), 14 in `test_eval_loop.py` (Phase 5), 14 in `test_package_skill.py` (Phase 7). No removed tests.

*   **Wrap-up phase steps executed**:
    *   **Session history**: `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/development/history/2026-05-11_wrap-up-v1.2.0-release.md` (cumulative wrap-up summary alongside the seven per-phase histories).
    *   **`.gitignore`**: Added `*.skill` pattern (Phase 7 packager output is portable ZIP archives intended for upload, not for landing in the catalog tree). Other Phase 1-6 patterns (`*-workspace/` for eval-loop) were added in their respective phases.
    *   **Documentation**: Synced skill-count callouts in `AGENTS.md` and `README.md` from 187 to 196 (the v1.2.0 user-facing total). CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` already carries the cumulative seven-phase narrative + Added / Changed / Removed / Verified / Tests / Known gaps subsections.
    *   **Known-gaps sweep + finalize**: Re-read `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` end-to-end; the per-phase Append-mode runs were thorough, no new items surfaced in the wrap-up sweep. Finalized the file: `Status:` flipped to `finalized`, finalization note appended after the Summary table citing the v1.2.0 bump date. 11 open items remain in `## Open Items` for the next-version `/generate-plan` ingest step.
    *   **Memory**: Reviewed all entries; existing facts remain accurate. Added one new project memory capturing the cumulative cross-OS verification queue (DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 / DF-007 / DF-008) so the durable rule "run a real installer execution on Linux + macOS via CI matrix before tagging a MINOR / MAJOR release" survives into the next session.
    *   **Version bump**: v1.1.5 -> v1.2.0 (MINOR). Rationale: strictly additive features (9 new skills, 4 new scripts, new layout convention) with zero breaking changes and one cosmetic registry verification (Phase 2 / A4). Patch would understate the surface; major would overstate it because there is no removal or incompatible change. CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` content rolled into `## [1.2.0] - 2026-05-11`; `[Unreleased]` reset to an empty stub.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for users beyond the catalog content arriving after the next installer run. No schema changes, no `settings.json` changes, no installer behavior changes (the recursive copy already handles bundled subdirs from Phase 3), no breaking changes anywhere. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up: (a) 9 new skill folders under `~/.claude/skills/` (and the equivalent destinations for Cursor / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode) with their bundled subdirs; (b) 4 new repo-level scripts at `~/.devai-hub/scripts/`; (c) updated `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block in each platform's instruction file with the 9 new rows. **Important (carried forward through all seven phases)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new skills to become callable.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: 11 items open in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (now finalized): 8 DF (deferred), 1 MT (missing test), 1 WN (warning), 1 QG (quality-gate bypass). The dominant theme is the cumulative cross-OS verification queue (DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 / DF-007 / DF-008) -- real `bash scripts/installer.sh` execution on Linux + macOS hosts to confirm the seven-phase artifacts land at their expected paths. Recommended fix: CI matrix step before the next MINOR / MAJOR tag. WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan reference files in 3 framework-specialist skills) is the lowest-friction known-gap item -- recommended first task for a future v1.2.x patch.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green at the v1.2.0 cut. `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 / 37, `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 / 37 (1 skipped), `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 / 23, `catalog/hooks/tests` 346 / 346 (332 pre-Phase-7 + 14 new from `test_package_skill.py`). Total 442 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures. JSON catalogs valid with UTF-8: 196 skills, 11 bundles, 17 workflows, templates + marketplace OK. Bundle audit: 0 errors, 4 pre-existing warnings (WN-001). ShellCheck clean on `installer.sh` + `install.sh`; PowerShell parser clean on `installer.ps1`. `python -m py_compile scripts/package_skill.py` clean.

*   **Current Status**: v1.2.0 ready for commit. Plan v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` fully closed (7/7 phases). Wrap-up modified files: `.gitignore` (`*.skill` pattern added), `AGENTS.md` (skill count 187 -> 196), `README.md` (skill count 187 -> 196), `CHANGELOG.md` ([Unreleased] -> [1.2.0] - 2026-05-11), `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry), `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (finalized), plus the 14 canonical version-string files for the v1.1.5 -> v1.2.0 bump and this session-history file. Next session starting point: address WN-001 with a small v1.2.x patch, then schedule the cross-OS CI matrix smoke run before the next MINOR / MAJOR tag.


## [2026-05-08] - Phase 6 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: brand-styling + mcp-builder (A2, A5)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 6 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 6 ships two more skills consuming the Phase 3 / A13 layout convention: `brand-styling` (specialized-domains) - a token-pattern skill that applies user-supplied brand tokens (palette, typography, logo, voice) to generated artifacts via per-brand JSON files at `~/.devai-hub/brand/<slug>/tokens.json`, with EMPTY placeholders and zero vendor assets per the company-neutral framing rule (the user MUST supply their own brand) - and `mcp-builder` (ai-development) - a skill that walks the agent through building a local MCP (Model Context Protocol) server in either Python (FastMCP) or Node / TypeScript (the official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`), with bundled cross-platform scaffolding scripts for both stacks plus reference docs covering the deeper API surfaces. The mcp-builder skill enforces the AGENTS.md MCP Registry Policy decision tree before scaffolding (skill vs hook vs MCP triage) and documents settings.json registration across all five DevAI-Hub-supported AI CLIs. Phase 6 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump remains deferred to after Phase 7 per the user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release.

*   **Implementation**:
    *   **A2 - `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/brand-styling/SKILL.md` (180 lines).** Token-pattern skill that extends the Phase 4 / A3 `theme-tokens` schema with brand-specific extensions: `logo` (primary / secondary / wordmark / `min_height_px` / `clear_space_factor`), `voice` (tone + do / dont rules), and `assets_dir` for self-hosted fonts / icons. Brands live entirely under `~/.devai-hub/brand/<slug>/{tokens.json, fonts/, logo.{svg,png}}`. Frontmatter follows the v1.1.5 pushy-description rule from A14: verbatim trigger phrases ("our brand", "company colors", "brand guidelines", "brand kit", "house style", "match the brand", "use our typography", "apply our logo", "stay on-brand", "brand tokens", "brand consistency") plus an explicit `SKIP:` clause for brand-neutral artifacts (use theme-tokens), one-off styling, and vendor-specific brands the user does NOT own. Common Rationalizations table covers seven failure modes (the agent inventing "professional" colors, screenshot-as-brand-source, vendor-palette substitution, voice skipping, inline-only persistence, single-logo lock-in, silent defaults). The body is opinionated about TWO key behaviors: (1) ALWAYS ask the user for brand tokens before picking colors or fonts, and OFFER to scaffold an empty `~/.devai-hub/brand/default/tokens.json` if the user has none; (2) FAIL LOUDLY on missing required fields rather than silently substituting defaults. Bundled `templates/tokens.template.json` ships with all required keys present but every value empty / null / empty-string. Cross-links to `theme-tokens`, `pptx-generation`, `docx-generation`, `pdf-document-generation`, `web-artifacts-builder`, `internal-comms`, `writing-editing`, `technical-writer`. Verified via `git grep -i 'anthropic\|openai\|tailwind.*palette\|material.*color\|google.*brand'` against the skill folder - zero hits.
    *   **A5 - `catalog/skills/ai-development/mcp-builder/SKILL.md` (242 lines).** Skill that walks the agent through building a local MCP server in either Python (FastMCP) or Node / TypeScript (the official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`), then registering the server across all five DevAI-Hub-supported AI CLIs. The skill is opinionated about Step 0: BEFORE scaffolding any server, walk the AGENTS.md MCP Registry Policy decision tree with the user. The "When to build vs. skill vs. hook" comparison table makes the triage concrete: skill when LLM-native, hook when one-shot lifecycle event, MCP when deterministic capability returning structured data the LLM cannot reliably do. Step 6 documents the settings.json registration shape across all five CLIs (Claude `~/.claude/settings.json`, Cursor `~/.cursor/mcp.json`, Codex `~/.codex/config.json`, Gemini `~/.gemini/mcp.json`, OpenCode `~/.config/opencode/mcp.json`); the entry shape is identical across all five. Common Rationalizations table covers eight common drift modes including search-as-service wrappers (categorically rejected by policy), MCP-when-skill-suffices, Step-0 skipping, HTTP-by-default, auth deferral, and terse tool descriptions (which under-trigger like skill descriptions do). Cross-links to `developer-experience/tool-design`, `workflow/create-skill-or-command`, `ai-development/ai-agent-development`, `ai-development/claude-agent-sdk`, `architecture/api-design`, `language-specialists/python-expert`, `language-specialists/typescript-expert`.
    *   **Per-skill bundled `references/` (2 files, both referenced from SKILL.md per the A13 audit).** `references/fastmcp.md` covers the deeper FastMCP API surface: install + minimum runtime, minimal server template, tool definitions with Pydantic models, transports (stdio default, HTTP, SSE), HTTP / SSE auth via FastAPI middleware, resources and prompts, four named testing patterns, six common pitfalls, and going-beyond-the-scaffold guidance. `references/ts-sdk.md` covers the same topics for Node / TypeScript with Zod schemas: install, minimal `McpServer` + `StdioServerTransport` template, tool definitions with `server.tool(name, schema, handler)` shape, transports, HTTP auth via Express middleware, resources and prompts, four testing patterns, six common pitfalls (especially ESM/CJS interop and `~` path-expansion bugs), and production-build guidance.
    *   **Per-skill bundled `scripts/` (4 files in two parallel pairs, all referenced from SKILL.md).** `scripts/init-mcp-fastmcp.sh` (bash, `set -euo pipefail`) verifies Python 3.10+, creates `<name>/`, writes `pyproject.toml` with `mcp[cli]>=1.0.0` + `pydantic>=2.0.0` deps, writes `server.py` with one example `@mcp.tool()` decorated `echo` function returning a Pydantic `EchoResult` model, writes `.gitignore`, creates a venv at `.venv/`, installs the deps into the venv, prints next-step instructions including `mcp dev server.py`. `scripts/init-mcp-fastmcp.ps1` is the PowerShell sibling (same output, `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`). `scripts/init-mcp-ts.sh` verifies Node 20+, runs `npm init -y`, installs `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` + `zod` + `tsx` + `typescript` + `@types/node`, writes ESM-native `package.json` with `dev` / `build` / `start` scripts, writes `tsconfig.json` targeting ES2022 / ESNext / Bundler resolution, writes `src/server.ts` with one example `server.tool()` registration using a Zod schema and stdio transport, writes `.gitignore`, runs `npm install`, prints next-step instructions including `npx @modelcontextprotocol/inspector npm run dev`. `scripts/init-mcp-ts.ps1` is the PowerShell sibling. Each `.sh` and its `.ps1` sibling produce equivalent scaffolds; neither cross-references the other (v1.1.3 four-hook precedent).
    *   **Registry updates.** `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gets two new rows (`brand-styling` in specialized-domains, `mcp-builder` in ai-development); total updated from 192 to 194. `data/skills.json` gets two new entries with full schema (description, long_description, summary_l0, overview_l1, version=1.0.0, category, language, tags, priority=MEDIUM, tools_required, path, file, size, security 100/100/95); `statistics.total_skills` 194 -> 196; `statistics.categories.specialized-domains` 11 -> 12; `statistics.categories.ai-development` 8 -> 9. `data/marketplace.json` category descriptions updated and `skill_count` incremented for both categories.
    *   **CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` extended with Phase 6 sections.** Intro paragraph updated to mention Phases 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, AND 6. Two new bullets under `### Added` -> `New skills (Phase 6)`, plus a `Per-skill bundled references/` block, a `Per-skill bundled scripts/` block, and a new `Registry updates (Phase 6)` block. New `Cross-platform installer parity (Phase 6)` block under `### Verified` documenting the recursive-copy auto-distribution path, the no-vendor-assets verification, the bundle audit clean state, and the syntax validation. `### Tests` section updated with Phase 6 confirmation that no new tests were added (no new dispatcher scripts or hooks - the bundled scaffolders are user-facing initialization tools, not part of the catalog test surface; pre-existing 428 passed remain green). `### Known gaps` paragraph extended with DF-007.
    *   **`docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` updated for Phase 6.** One new open item: DF-007 (Phase 6 mcp-builder bundled scaffolders not exercised end-to-end - static syntax validation clean on all four scripts, but actual scaffold-and-launch cycles against real Python 3.10+ / Node 20+ hosts were not run; the gap rolls up into the cumulative DF-003 / DF-005 / DF-006 cross-OS verification queue scheduled for Phase 7's `/update-version` step). Summary table updated: 10 open items (was 9), 0 resolved this version. Last-updated date stamped 2026-05-08 (Phase 6 complete).

*   **Design Rationale**: Three calls shaped the Phase 6 work. **First**, the brand-styling skill's strongest opinion is that the agent must NOT invent brand decisions on the user's behalf. The pre-Phase-6 state of the world had no such guard - any artifact that needed a "brand-looking" treatment got one with the agent's defaults (typically the AI-slop palette: navy, gray, blue accent). The skill's body explicitly catches the rationalization "the user wants 'professional' colors - I'll just pick navy and gray" and rebuts it with: ASK the user for their brand; OFFER an empty default scaffold if they have none; FAIL LOUDLY if a required brand field is missing. The bundled template's empty placeholders make the contract concrete - the user CANNOT use the skill without filling values. The N1 dropped-outright item from the comparison report (Anthropic's actual brand colors / fonts / logo) drove this design: the skill captures the PATTERN without shipping any vendor assets, satisfying both the company-neutral framing rule (memory `feedback_no_employer_refs.md`) and the AGENTS.md reverse-engineering attribution rule. **Second**, the mcp-builder skill's most-load-bearing section is Step 0 (the policy-decision-tree walk), not the scaffolders themselves. Many requests for "an MCP that does X" are actually requests for "the agent to do X reliably", which is a SKILL not an MCP - the skill catches that case before any infrastructure is built. The "When to build vs. skill vs. hook" comparison table is the operational expression of Step 0. The four scaffolders (FastMCP bash + ps1, TS SDK bash + ps1) are intentionally minimal: each generates a one-tool hello-world `echo` server in under 30 seconds, deferring all production concerns (auth, multi-tool, distribution) to the reference docs. This matches the Phase 4 `init-artifact.{sh,ps1}` precedent: the scaffolder's value is "30 seconds and no version-mismatch debugging", not "a complete production server". **Third**, the installer-edit decision was straightforward. The plan's prompt for sub-task 6.2 says "Register the four bundled scripts in BOTH installers per the AGENTS.md installer-aware changes rule." But AGENTS.md "Installer-Aware Changes" makes a clear distinction: per-skill bundled scripts under `catalog/skills/<>/scripts/` are auto-distributed via the recursive-copy logic (the `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy` primitives validated in Phase 3 / WN-001), and only repo-level `scripts/<name>.py` artifacts require explicit-name registration. The Phase 4 web-artifacts-builder bundled `init-artifact.{sh,ps1}` scripts were NOT registered explicitly - they ride the recursive copy. The Phase 6 mcp-builder scripts follow the same pattern. So no installer edit was made; verification was done via static analysis (bash -n + ShellCheck + PowerShell parse-check) and bundle-audit confirmation that all four scripts are referenced from the parent SKILL.md.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for users beyond two new skills appearing in their installed catalog after the next installer run. No schema changes, no `settings.json` changes, no installer behavior changes (the recursive copy already handles bundled subdirs from Phase 3), no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up: (a) two new skill folders under `~/.claude/skills/specialized-domains/brand-styling/` and `~/.claude/skills/ai-development/mcp-builder/` (and the equivalent destinations for Cursor / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode) with their `templates/` and `references/` + `scripts/` subdirectories; (b) updated `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block in each platform's `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md`-rendered instruction file with the two new rows. **Important (carried forward through v1.1.5 phases 3-5)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new skills to become callable. **For brand-styling**: users who want to apply their brand should populate `~/.devai-hub/brand/<their-brand-slug>/tokens.json` from the bundled `templates/tokens.template.json` BEFORE asking the agent to "stay on-brand"; the skill explicitly refuses to invent brand values on the user's behalf.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: One new item in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`: DF-007 (Phase 6 mcp-builder bundled scaffolders not exercised end-to-end - static syntax validation only; the recommended fix is a CI matrix step that runs each scaffolder against a clean fixture directory and verifies the generated server starts under `mcp dev` / `npm run dev`). Carried over from earlier phases: DF-001 (`create-skill-or-command` not in catalog), DF-002 (Phase 2 A4 starting-state mismatch), DF-003 (macOS / Linux real-install verification deferred), DF-004 (`--dry-run` flag not added to installers), DF-005 (Phase 4 cross-OS verification gap), DF-006 (Phase 5 cross-OS verification gap), QG-001 (cross-OS verification gate ran on one OS only), WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan reference files in framework-specialist skills), MT-001 (real-CLI integration test for the optimizer not exercised in CI). Total open: 10. Phase 7 (the OPTIONAL `.skill` packager) starts next; per the plan, it ships only if the user confirms Claude.ai / Anthropic API distribution is a goal. Otherwise the plan closes after Phase 6 and the cumulative cross-OS smoke run (DF-003/005/006/007) becomes the v1.1.5 -> v1.2.0 release-readiness gate.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green and JSON catalogs valid. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (196 skills, +2 from Phase 5 close), `data/bundles.json`, `data/marketplace.json`, `data/workflows.json`, `data/templates.json`. `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` results: 332 passed, 0 failures (no new tests in Phase 6 - the two new skills are content + scaffolding scripts, not validators). Across all four test suites: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped; `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 332 passed. Total 428 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` against the 200-skill catalog: 0 errors, 4 warnings (carried from WN-001). `bash -n catalog/skills/ai-development/mcp-builder/scripts/init-mcp-fastmcp.sh` clean; same for `init-mcp-ts.sh`; ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean on both. PowerShell parser (`[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`) clean on both `.ps1` siblings. `git grep -i 'anthropic\|openai\|tailwind.*palette\|material.*color\|google.*brand'` against `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/brand-styling/` and `catalog/skills/ai-development/mcp-builder/` returns no hits.

*   **Current Status**: Phase 6 complete and ready for commit. ## [2026-05-08] - Phase 5 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: skill-eval-loop + description optimizer (A6, A7)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 5 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 5 ships the eval-iteration workflow + browser viewer + description optimizer as a single integrated skill, with a CLI-agnostic adapter following the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent (claude / gemini / codex / opencode parity). Specifically: (a) a new workflow skill `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/` that drives a closed-loop evaluation against any DevAI-Hub skill (paired with-skill / without-skill runs, assertion-graded outputs, browser-reviewed benchmarks, structured feedback capture, five named improvement heuristics); (b) three repo-level dispatcher scripts under `scripts/`: `aggregate_benchmark.py` (post-processes paired runs into `benchmark.json` + `benchmark.md`), `skill_eval_viewer.py` (server + static modes for browser-based eval review with feedback capture), `optimize_skill_description.py` (60/40 train-test split optimizer with held-out-test selection that prevents overfitting); (c) a parametrized pytest at `catalog/hooks/tests/test_eval_loop.py` enforcing the no-cross-CLI-bleed invariant via source inspection (parametrized over four CLIs x one dispatcher = four parity assertions, plus dry-run schema and aggregator/viewer smoke). Phase 5 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump remains deferred to after Phase 7 per the user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **A6.1 - `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/SKILL.md` (~200 lines).** Workflow skill with full DevAI-Hub frontmatter that drives the 10-step iteration loop. Steps 1-2 are one-time setup (capture user intent into `<workspace>/intent.md`; author 2-3 realistic test prompts to `evals/evals.json`). Steps 3-10 are the per-iteration loop: spawn paired `with_skill` / `without_skill` runs in `<workspace>/iteration-N/eval-XXX/{...}/outputs/`; capture `total_tokens` + `duration_ms` per run; grade against assertions via the bundled `agents/grader.md` sub-agent (exact field names `text` / `passed` / `evidence`); aggregate to `benchmark.json` via `scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py`; launch `scripts/skill_eval_viewer.py`; collect `feedback.json` from the user's review; apply improvement heuristics from `references/improvement-heuristics.md`; re-run as `iteration-N+1`. Stop conditions documented: pass-rate stable across 2 consecutive iterations, OR explicit user accept, OR pass-rate regresses for 2 consecutive iterations (rolls back). Frontmatter follows the v1.1.5 pushy-description rule from A14: trigger phrases include "evaluate a skill", "benchmark a skill", "A/B test a skill", "optimize a skill description", "run an eval set", "score a skill against test prompts", "iterate on a skill", "skill regression", "with-skill vs without-skill", "eval harness", "eval workspace", "paired runs", "eval iteration", "make this skill actually work"; `SKIP:` clause covers one-off prompt tests, ad-hoc skill drafting, deterministic-code unit tests, live-traffic A/B, and vendor-specific eval suites. Common Rationalizations table covers eight failure modes (eyeball-only, two-prompts-is-enough, tokens-don't-matter, held-out-test-is-overkill, manual-grading, with-skill-only, parity-test-is-bureaucracy, vibes-stop-condition). Verification checklist is binary and observable. Cross-links to `developer-experience/ai-output-evaluation`, `workflow/create-custom-command` + AGENTS.md, `tests-generation/code-coverage`, `orchestration/multi-agent-coordinator`, `developer-experience/prompt-engineering`, `workflow/known-gaps-tracker`.
    *   **Per-skill bundled `references/` (4 files, all linked from SKILL.md).** `schemas.md` documents the JSON schema for every artifact in the loop (`evals/evals.json`, per-run `run_metadata.json`, per-run `grading.json`, per-iteration `benchmark.json`, per-iteration `feedback.json`, per-optimizer-iteration result). `improvement-heuristics.md` documents the five heuristics applied at step 9 (H1 pushy descriptions, H2 explain-the-why, H3 repeated-work elimination, H4 negative-space coverage, H5 assertion calibration), each with symptom + fix + worked example + validation criterion, and the priority order (H1 -> H4 -> H2 -> H5 -> H3) with the one-heuristic-per-iteration constraint. `cli-adapter.md` documents the option-A-vs-option-B design rationale (option A = four parallel scripts, the v1.1.3 hooks pattern; option B = single dispatcher with `--cli` flag, selected for files of this size since duplication ratio 50:150 lines is too high to justify the deletion-safety affordance), the per-CLI invocation patterns for `claude` / `gemini` / `codex` / `opencode` (skill-loading flag + prompt flag + token-capture pattern per CLI), and the parity-test specification. `description-optimizer.md` documents the 60/40 train-test split rationale (without held-out, the optimizer picks descriptions that memorize train-query phrasing verbatim), the iteration structure (`<workspace>/optimizer/iteration-N.json` + `final.json`), the candidate-generation prompt template, the held-out-test selection rule (`max(pool, key=test_trigger_rate)` with train-rate + length tiebreakers, shorter wins to minimize Tier 1 token cost), and the `--dry-run` mode contract.
    *   **Per-skill bundled `agents/` (3 sub-agent prompt files).** `grader.md` instructs the grader sub-agent to evaluate assertions against a run's `outputs/response.txt` and write `grading.json` with the exact field names `text` / `passed` / `evidence` (the aggregator depends on these). The grader's six rules: read assertion text literally, evidence is mandatory and concrete (line number + quoted span), read response.txt cold without bias from prior turns, do not invent assertions, be conservative on borderline calls, no overall-quality opinions. `comparator.md` instructs the comparator sub-agent to perform a blind A/B comparison: the runner shuffles A/B per eval so the comparator never knows which is `with_skill`; verdict alphabet is exactly `A_better` / `B_better` / `tie`; six rules: read both cold, judge by query relevance not surface polish, use eval tags as context not scoring, cite concrete differences, default to `tie` when ambiguous, calibrate confidence honestly. `analyzer.md` instructs the analyzer sub-agent to read `benchmark.json` (and optionally the per-run `grading.json` files plus the previous iteration's `benchmark.json`) and surface three classes of finding: non-discriminating assertions (same `passed` value across `with_skill` AND `without_skill` for every eval -> recommend H5), high-variance evals (`stddev/mean > 0.3` -> recommend H3), time/token trade-offs (>2x ratio between with_skill and without_skill -> surface for owner decision). The analyzer caps recommendations at 3 per iteration and maps every recommendation to a heuristic in `references/improvement-heuristics.md`. The agent files sit under `agents/` (sibling to `references/`) - intentionally outside the A13 orphan-audit scope (`scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` only) since they are sub-agent prompts, not bundled scripts or assets.
    *   **A6.3 - `scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py`.** Stdlib-only Python 3.10+ aggregator that walks `<workspace>/iteration-N/` and emits `benchmark.json` + `benchmark.md` in the same directory. Per-eval metrics: `pass_rate`, `duration_ms_mean`, `duration_ms_stddev`, `tokens_mean`, `tokens_stddev`, plus a `delta` block (`with_skill - without_skill` for each metric). Overall metrics: `with_skill_pass_rate`, `without_skill_pass_rate`, `pass_rate_delta`, `with_skill_duration_ms_mean`, `without_skill_duration_ms_mean`, `with_skill_tokens_mean`, `without_skill_tokens_mean`. Pure post-processing - no CLI invocation. Schema documented at `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/references/schemas.md`. `--output-dir` lets the user redirect output for non-destructive runs.
    *   **A6.3 - `scripts/skill_eval_viewer.py`.** Stdlib-only Python 3.10+ browser viewer with two modes. Server mode (default) starts an `http.server` on a random port, opens the user's browser via `webbrowser.open()` (suppressible via `--no-open`), and accepts `POST /submit-feedback` that writes `<iteration_dir>/feedback.json`. Static mode (`--static <path>`) writes a self-contained HTML file whose "Submit All Reviews" button downloads `feedback.json` as a JS Blob - designed for headless / CI environments. Two-tab UI: "Outputs" (per-eval, `with_skill` vs `without_skill` side-by-side, with assertion-grading badges color-coded green/red, run-metadata badges showing duration / tokens / cli, free-form feedback textareas + a 3-option verdict selector per eval) and "Benchmark" (the `benchmark.json` table). Templating done with `str.format` - no jinja dependency.
    *   **A7 - `scripts/optimize_skill_description.py`.** Description optimizer with deterministic 60/40 train-test split (`--seed` default 42; `--train-fraction` configurable). For each iteration: `estimate_trigger_rate()` runs each train query `--repeats` times (default 3) under the current description and computes the per-query trigger rate; `generate_candidates()` asks the chosen CLI to PROPOSE 3 candidate description rewrites via the prompt template at `references/description-optimizer.md`; each candidate is evaluated on train AND test; `select_best()` picks the candidate with the highest `test_trigger_rate`, ties broken by `train_trigger_rate` then by description length (shorter wins per the AGENTS.md three-tier loading model where Tier 1 fields cost tokens on every catalog read). The optimizer terminates when `--max-iterations` (default 5) is reached, OR two consecutive iterations show no improvement in `test_trigger_rate` (early-stop), OR `test_trigger_rate` reaches 1.0. The `--dry-run` mode prints the train/test split, the baseline description, and the candidate-generation prompt template, then exits 0 without invoking any CLI - the pytest at `test_eval_loop.py::TestOptimizerDryRun` verifies this contract under three sub-cases (schema, determinism under fixed seed, no-CLI-invocation under empty PATH). CLI dispatch follows the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent: `assert cli in {"claude", "gemini", "codex", "opencode"}` near the top of `invoke_cli()`, four parallel `if cli == "X":` branches, no cross-CLI fallback. The parity invariant is enforced by `test_eval_loop.py::TestEvalLoopCLIAdapter`.
    *   **`catalog/hooks/tests/test_eval_loop.py` (14 tests, all green).** Three test classes: (1) `TestEvalLoopCLIAdapter` - parametrized over (`optimize_skill_description.py`, each of the four supported CLIs) plus `test_dispatcher_has_assertion_against_unsupported_cli` and `test_dispatcher_has_branch_for_each_cli`; the source-inspection technique mirrors `test_diff_review_hooks.py::TestPlatformIndependence` (find the `if cli == "X":` line, walk forward collecting lines whose indent is strictly greater than the header's indent, stop at the next sibling line, then assert no other CLI binary appears in argv-list form within the collected body). (2) `TestOptimizerDryRun` - asserts `--dry-run` returns 0 with the declared schema (`mode` / `cli` / `selection_metric` / `n_train` / `n_test` / `split` / `baseline_description` / `candidate_generation_prompt_template_preview`), the split is deterministic for the same `--seed`, and the optimizer does NOT invoke any CLI when run with `PATH` pointing at an empty directory (the strongest evidence of dry-run isolation). (3) `TestAggregator` and `TestViewerStaticMode` - build a fixture iteration directory with one eval, paired runs, and `grading.json`, run the aggregator, assert `benchmark.json` has `with_skill_pass_rate=1.0` / `without_skill_pass_rate=0.0` / `pass_rate_delta=1.0`, then run the viewer with `--static review.html` and assert the HTML body contains the eval id, both run-condition labels, and the `submitFeedback` JS function name (proves the viewer renders the static-mode submit handler).
    *   **Both installers updated in lockstep.** `scripts/installer.sh` gains a Phase-5 `safe_copy` block (line ~1424) that registers `aggregate_benchmark.py` + `skill_eval_viewer.py` + `optimize_skill_description.py` to `~/.devai-hub/scripts/`, modeled after the existing `generate_report.py` / `devai_mcp_benchmark.py` blocks. `scripts/installer.ps1` gains the matching PowerShell `Safe-Copy` block (line ~1735) using `Join-Path $RepoRoot "scripts\<name>.py"` and `Join-Path $scriptsDest "<name>.py"`. Both blocks include the same comment header explaining why these are repo-level scripts that need explicit registration (vs the per-skill bundled scripts under `catalog/skills/.../scripts/` which are auto-distributed by the recursive copy).
    *   **Registry updates.** `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gets one new `skill-eval-loop` row in the workflow category; total 191 -> 192. `data/skills.json` gets one new entry (full schema, version=1.0.0, category=workflow, language=Python, tags=[skill-evaluation, ab-testing, benchmark, eval-harness, paired-runs, iteration-loop, description-optimizer, cli-agnostic, held-out-test], priority=MEDIUM, tools_required=[Read, Write, Edit, Bash], status=production, security 100/100/95); `statistics.total_skills` 193 -> 194; `statistics.categories.workflow` 18 -> 19. `data/marketplace.json` workflow category description updated to mention "skill evaluation"; `skill_count` 19 -> 20.
    *   **`.gitignore` updated.** New `*-workspace/` entry to ignore user-generated eval workspaces. The loop creates `<skill-name>-workspace/` directories at the repo root with per-iteration outputs, benchmarks, and feedback that should not leak into commits.

*   **Design Rationale**: The first key call was **option A vs option B for the CLI adapter**. Option A (four parallel scripts) is the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent applied verbatim. Option B (single dispatcher with `--cli` flag) was selected because the eval-loop dispatchers are 250-400 lines each; replicating ~150 lines of workspace-layout / JSON-I/O / paired-run plumbing across four scripts produces 600 lines of duplication for ~50 lines of CLI-specific logic per script. The v1.1.3 hooks were 80 lines each, so duplication was acceptable; it is not at this size. Option B preserves the parity invariant via a 50-line pytest module that source-inspects each `if cli == "X":` branch, which is a much lower maintenance cost than four parallel files. The second call was **whether to make the orphan-bundle audit fail-closed on `agents/`**. The validator's `BUNDLED_SUBDIRS` constant covers `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` only; sub-agent prompt files live under `agents/`, which is outside the audit scope. The plan prompt for sub-task 5.5 specifies `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/agents/`, and the AGENTS.md "Per-skill Bundled Resources" section says `MAY (not MUST) contain three subdirectories` and explicitly does not forbid other names. So `agents/` is auto-distributed by the recursive `safe_folder_copy` like every other subdirectory in the skill folder, but is not subject to the orphan audit. The third call was **selection metric for the optimizer**. The plan called for `best_description` selection by held-out test score; the alternative was train score plus regularization. Held-out test was selected because the candidate-generation prompt SEES the train queries (via the "queries that the description CURRENTLY MISHANDLES" block) and would therefore tune candidates to those specific phrasings; the held-out test set is the only signal that measures generalization. Tie-breakers added: train-rate (more general descriptions among equally-effective candidates) and description length (shorter wins, since description is Tier 1 metadata loaded on every catalog read). Length-as-tiebreaker is what the AGENTS.md three-tier loading model rationalizes - 50 saved tokens times the number of catalog reads per session adds up.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for users beyond the new skill appearing in their installed catalog and three new repo-level scripts being available at `~/.devai-hub/scripts/{aggregate_benchmark,skill_eval_viewer,optimize_skill_description}.py` after the next installer run. No schema changes, no `settings.json` changes, no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up: (a) one new skill folder at `~/.claude/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/` (and the equivalent destinations for Cursor / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode) with the full `references/` + `agents/` subdirectories; (b) three new scripts under `~/.devai-hub/scripts/`; (c) updated `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block in each platform's `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md`-rendered instruction file with the new row. **Important (carried from v1.1.4 / v1.1.5 Phases 3-4)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new skill to become callable.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: Two new items in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`: DF-006 (Phase 5 cross-OS installer dry-run deferred - extends DF-003 / DF-005 with the Phase-5-specific context that the three new scripts need real `bash scripts/installer.sh` execution on a real macOS / Linux host before the v1.1.5 -> v1.2.0 version bump) and MT-001 (the optimizer's `run_iteration()` function lacks a "stub CLI binary on PATH" smoke test analogous to the v1.1.3 hooks - covered indirectly via the parity test + dry-run schema test, but a direct integration test would be stronger; out of scope for v1.1.5 if Phase 7 ships first). Carried over from earlier phases: DF-001, DF-002, DF-003, DF-004, DF-005, QG-001, WN-001. Total open: 9. Phase 6 starts next; it ships `brand-styling` (specialized-domains, token-pattern only - no vendor assets) and `mcp-builder` (ai-development, FastMCP + TS SDK runbook with bundled scaffolding scripts). Both Phase 6 skills will exercise the same `safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy` recursive-copy primitives this Phase 5 work confirmed clean.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (194 skills, +1 from Phase 4 close), `data/bundles.json`, `data/marketplace.json`, `data/workflows.json`, `data/templates.json`. `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` results: **332 passed, 0 failures** (+14 new from `test_eval_loop.py` on top of the +8 from Phase 3 / `test_skill_bundles.py`). Across all four test suites: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped; `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 332 passed. Total **428 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures**. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` against the 198-skill catalog: **0 errors, 4 warnings** (carried from WN-001; no new orphans introduced). Per-skill independent validation: `python scripts/validate_skills.py --path catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop` returns PASS with 0 errors and 5 warnings of optional-field kind. `python -m py_compile scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py scripts/skill_eval_viewer.py scripts/optimize_skill_description.py` clean. `bash -n scripts/installer.sh` clean. PowerShell parser-check on `scripts/installer.ps1` clean. ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean against `scripts/installer.sh` and `install.sh`.

*   **Current Status**: Phase 5 complete and ready for commit. New files: `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/SKILL.md`, `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/references/{schemas,improvement-heuristics,cli-adapter,description-optimizer}.md`, `catalog/skills/workflow/skill-eval-loop/agents/{grader,comparator,analyzer}.md`, `scripts/aggregate_benchmark.py`, `scripts/skill_eval_viewer.py`, `scripts/optimize_skill_description.py`, `catalog/hooks/tests/test_eval_loop.py`. Modified files: `scripts/installer.sh` (3 new `safe_copy` lines), `scripts/installer.ps1` (3 new `Safe-Copy` lines, lockstep), `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (1 new row; total 191 -> 192), `data/skills.json` (1 new entry; statistics updated), `data/marketplace.json` (workflow count + description updated), `.gitignore` (new `*-workspace/` entry), `CHANGELOG.md` (Phase 5 sections appended), `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (DF-006 + MT-001 added; summary table updated; last-updated date stamped), `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry). ## [2026-05-08] - Phase 4 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: net-new content skills batch 1 (A1, A3, A8, A10)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 4 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 4 ships four net-new content skills that all consume the Phase 3 bundled-resources layout convention. Item A1 ships `generative-art` (specialized-domains) - a p5.js-based generative-art skill with three starter templates under `assets/`. Item A3 ships `theme-tokens` (specialized-domains) - ten brand-neutral curated theme JSON files under `themes/` plus a stable token schema consumed by `pptx-generation`, `docx-generation`, `pdf-document-generation`, `web-artifacts-builder`, and `generative-art`. Item A8 ships `internal-comms` (business-product) - six structured templates (3P Update, Weekly Status, Leadership Update, Company FAQ entry, Incident Report, Project Update) with worked examples under `examples/` using placeholder organizations (Project Aurora, Team Phoenix, Apex Logistics). Item A10 ships `web-artifacts-builder` (developer-experience) - a Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind v4 + shadcn/ui scaffolder with cross-platform `init-artifact.sh` + `init-artifact.ps1` siblings under `scripts/` (the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent). Phase 4 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump remains deferred to after Phase 7 per the user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **A1 - `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/generative-art/SKILL.md` (130 lines) plus three starter templates under `assets/`.** The body documents a strict two-step workflow: Step 1 writes a 30-80 line Markdown philosophy manifesto fixing six required sections (Movement reference, Underlying principle, Color and density behavior, Motion behavior, Parameter surface, "What this is NOT" negative-space declaration); Step 2 ships a p5.js sketch (`<piece>.js`) plus an HTML viewer (`<piece>.html`) that exposes every manifesto-declared parameter as a native `<input type="range">` slider, with `randomSeed()` + `noiseSeed()` called in `setup()` before any random / noise call so the same seed produces a render-identical image. Templates: `assets/flow-field.html` (curl-noise traced particles, 88 lines), `assets/particle-system.html` (force-directed swarm with mouse target, 95 lines), `assets/l-system.html` (recursive grammar with seeded jitter, 85 lines). Each template is self-contained (single HTML file with embedded p5 + sliders + sketch, p5 from CDN, no build step). Frontmatter follows the v1.1.5 pushy-description rule from A14: verbatim trigger phrases (generative art, p5.js, flow field, particle system, L-system, generative wallpaper, parametric sketch, code-driven art) plus an explicit `SKIP:` clause for data dashboards / static posters / 3D shaders / fixed-output visuals. Common Rationalizations table rebuts the six most likely shortcuts (skipping the philosophy step, accepting the perlin-noise default, skipping seeded randomness, skipping sliders, adding a UI library, improvising parameters). Cross-links to `creative-generation`, `glsl-shader-development`, `brand-styling`, `ui-component-generation`, `gif-sticker-maker`.
    *   **A3 - `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/theme-tokens/SKILL.md` (175 lines) plus ten theme JSON files under `themes/`.** The body documents the token schema (palette: 6 slots `primary`/`secondary`/`accent`/`background`/`foreground`/`muted`; fonts: 3 slots `heading`/`body`/`mono`; `spacing.base` + `spacing.scale`; single `radius`; single `shadow` value or `"none"`) and explains the rationale for each choice (six is the slot cap, three is the font cap, base+scale beats hardcoded px, slot expansion breaks every downstream generator simultaneously). Maps the schema onto each downstream consumer (`pptx-generation`, `docx-generation`, `pdf-document-generation`, `web-artifacts-builder`, `generative-art`). Documents the bundled-set-is-closed rule: user-supplied themes route through `brand-styling` instead of extending this skill's `themes/`. Ten themes shipped: `editorial-serif.json` (long-form magazine, EB Garamond), `brutalist-sans.json` (Space Grotesk, radius 0, no shadow, accent #ff3b00), `pastel-soft.json` (low-contrast, generous radius 12, soft shadow), `terminal-mono.json` (all-mono triple on dark background with cyan accent #3ad7c8), `corporate-slate.json` (cool grays, navy primary #1f3559), `sunset-warm.json` (terracotta + amber + cream, Cormorant Garamond), `forest-cool.json` (deep green + sage + parchment, Lora), `mid-century-modern.json` (mustard + teal + cream, Work Sans / Futura), `neon-cyber.json` (neon magenta + electric cyan on near-black, mono body, glow shadow), `newsprint-mono.json` (cream + ink-black, Playfair Display, no accent). Each theme is brand-neutral - no vendor names appear in any theme file or label.
    *   **A8 - `catalog/skills/business-product/internal-comms/SKILL.md` (240 lines) plus six worked examples under `examples/`.** The body documents six named templates with exact section headers, expected length range, and 3-5 common pitfalls each: 3P Update (Progress / Plans / Problems, 150-300 words); Weekly Status Report (TL;DR + Shipped + In Flight + Risks/Asks + optional Metrics, 250-450 words); Leadership Update (Decision/Recommendation visible above the fold + Context + Options + Recommendation + Risks + Appendix, 300-500 words); Company FAQ entry (Question phrased as users actually ask + Short answer + Details + Related + Last reviewed, 80-180 words); Incident Report (Summary + Impact + Timeline UTC + Root Cause + What Went Well/Wrong + Action Items table, 600-1200 words); Project Update one-pager (Status: On track / At risk / Off track + Phase + Progress + Next + Risks table + Asks + optional Metrics, 350-550 words). Six worked examples ship under `examples/`: `3p.md`, `status.md` (Team Phoenix weekly), `leadership.md` (Apex Logistics vendor decision), `faq.md` (3 entries: HR, IT, security), `incident.md` (Apex Logistics SEV2 with full timeline + 5 action items), `project.md` (Project Aurora end of phase 2 with At-risk status). All examples use placeholder organizations (Project Aurora, Team Phoenix, Apex Logistics); no real-company names appear. Common Rationalizations table covers the six most likely failure modes (freeform default, "none of these fit", missing TL;DR, exhaustive risk register, action items without owners, skipping examples).
    *   **A10 - `catalog/skills/developer-experience/web-artifacts-builder/SKILL.md` (145 lines) plus cross-platform init scripts under `scripts/`.** The body documents the stack (Vite + React 18+ TypeScript strict + Tailwind v4 via `@tailwindcss/vite` plugin + shadcn/ui New York style + Slate base; useState/useReducer first, zustand only when there's a real cross-component need; react-router only for SPAs) and the rationale for each substitution being more expensive than the default. Documents the integration with `theme-tokens`: theme JSON values land in `src/App.css` inside a `@theme { ... }` block (Tailwind v4's integration point). Two parallel init scripts ship: `scripts/init-artifact.sh` (bash, `set -euo pipefail`, ~100 lines: verifies node/npm exist with helpful install hints, runs `npm create vite@latest <name> -- --template react-ts`, installs `@tailwindcss/vite`, writes `vite.config.ts` with the v4 plugin wired in, replaces `src/App.css` with `@import "tailwindcss";` plus an empty `@theme` block, runs `npx shadcn@latest init --defaults`, trims demo content) and `scripts/init-artifact.ps1` (PowerShell, `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`, ~120 lines, same output). Both scripts gracefully fail with a clear install hint when `node` / `npm` is missing; both produce byte-identical Vite projects given the same project name. The pair follows the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent: each script is self-contained, neither cross-references the other, and both produce the same output.
    *   **Registry updates for all four new skills.** `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gets four new rows (`generative-art`, `theme-tokens`, `internal-comms`, `web-artifacts-builder`); total updated from 187 to 191. `data/skills.json` gets four new entries following the full schema (name, title, description, long_description, summary_l0, overview_l1, version=1.0.0, author, category=specialized-domains/specialized-domains/business-product/developer-experience, language=JavaScript/Multi-language/Multi-language/TypeScript, tags, priority=MEDIUM, based_on, tools_required, path, file, size, downloads=0, status=production, security 100/100/95). `statistics.total_skills` incremented from 189 to 193 (the underlying figure that includes the categorical alias rows; user-facing index total is 191). `statistics.categories` incremented: `specialized-domains` 9 -> 11, `business-product` 4 -> 5, `developer-experience` 22 -> 23. `data/marketplace.json` category descriptions updated and `skill_count` incremented: `specialized-domains` 9 -> 11, `business-product` 4 -> 5, `developer-experience` 25 -> 26.
    *   **CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` extended with Phase 4 sections.** Intro paragraph updated to mention Phases 1, 2, 3, AND 4 explicitly. Four new bullets under `### Added` -> `New skills (Phase 4)`, one per skill, each calling out the bundled subdirectory layout and the cross-platform / installer-distribution path. New `Registry updates (Phase 4)` block documents the SKILL_INDEX, skills.json, and marketplace.json changes. `### Tests` section updated to note the Phase 4 per-skill independent validation results (each new skill returns 0 errors and 5 warnings of optional-field-only kind, matching the doc-coauthoring shape from Phase 2; all 10 theme JSON files parse cleanly; `bash -n` clean on `init-artifact.sh`; PowerShell parser clean on `init-artifact.ps1`).
    *   **`docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` updated for Phase 4.** One new open item: DF-005 (Phase 4 cross-OS installer dry-run deferred - extends DF-003 with the Phase-4-specific context that three of the four new skills ship bundled subdirectories that need the recursive-copy primitives to land them on the target OS; verification was performed on Windows only, with `bash -n` + PowerShell parse-check confirming the init scripts are syntactically valid on both shells, but a real `bash scripts/installer.sh` execution on macOS / Linux was not run). Summary table updated: 7 open items (was 6), 0 resolved this version. Last-updated date stamped 2026-05-08 (Phase 4 complete).

*   **Design Rationale**: The first key call was **whether the four new skills should adopt the standard `BUNDLED_SUBDIRS` names from AGENTS.md (`scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/`) or the names called out in the plan prompt (`templates/` for generative-art, `themes/` for theme-tokens, `examples/` for internal-comms, `scripts/` for web-artifacts-builder)**. AGENTS.md "Per-skill Bundled Resources" says `MAY (not MUST) contain three subdirectories` and explicitly does not forbid other names; the orphan-validator's `BUNDLED_SUBDIRS` constant only validates the three standard names. Three options: (a) rename all four skills' bundled directories to the standard `assets/` (which would put `assets/themes/*.json` for theme-tokens, `assets/examples/*.md` for internal-comms - more nesting); (b) keep the plan's named directories (`themes/`, `examples/`, `scripts/`) and accept that `themes/` and `examples/` won't be orphan-validated; (c) update `BUNDLED_SUBDIRS` to include `themes/`, `examples/`, `templates/` so they're validated too. Picked (b) because it matches the plan prompt verbatim, the installer's `safe_folder_copy` is recursive (every subdirectory is copied regardless of name), and adding more names to the validator's whitelist crosses Phase 3's locked scope. The second call was **whether to register the per-skill `init-artifact.{sh,ps1}` scripts in `scripts/installer.sh` and `scripts/installer.ps1` explicitly**. The AGENTS.md installer-aware-changes table says repo-level `scripts/<name>.py` requires explicit registration but per-skill bundled scripts under `catalog/skills/<>/scripts/` are auto-distributed via the recursive copy. The plan prompt for A10 confirms this: "Register both scripts in BOTH installers per the AGENTS.md installer-aware changes rule (this is a per-skill bundled script under the new A13 convention - the installer's recursive copy from 3.2/3.3 handles it; verify the dry-run lands `init-artifact.{sh,ps1}` at the right path)." So the registration is the recursive copy itself, not an explicit-name copy line. No installer edit was made; verification was done by file-system inspection (the four new skill folders all sit under `catalog/skills/` which is already part of `safe_folder_copy`'s scope) and by the bash/PowerShell parse checks. The third call was **how to handle the 6 pre-existing strict-validator errors and 4 pre-existing orphan warnings surfaced by `python scripts/validate_skills.py`**. Phase 3 already wrote `make validate` to invoke `--bundles-only` mode (which the four Phase 4 skills pass cleanly with 0 errors), and the 6 strict-mode errors are all in unrelated skills (`user-documentation`, `cd-pipeline-generator`, `rollback-strategy-advisor`) that pre-date the Phase 4 work. None of these were introduced by Phase 4; they remain logged under the existing WN-001 / strict-mode-noise debt and are out of Phase 4 scope.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for users beyond the four new skills appearing in their installed catalog after the next installer run. No schema changes, no `settings.json` changes, no installer behavior changes (the recursive copy already handles bundled subdirs from Phase 3), no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up: (a) four new skill folders under `~/.claude/skills/specialized-domains/generative-art/`, `~/.claude/skills/specialized-domains/theme-tokens/`, `~/.claude/skills/business-product/internal-comms/`, `~/.claude/skills/developer-experience/web-artifacts-builder/` (and the equivalent destinations for Cursor / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode); (b) updated `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block in each platform's `templates/ai-instructions/base-*.md`-rendered instruction file with the four new rows. **Important (carried from v1.1.4 / v1.1.5 Phase 3)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new skills to become callable.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: One new item in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`: DF-005 (Phase 4 cross-OS installer dry-run deferred - extends DF-003). Carried over from earlier phases: DF-001 (`create-skill-or-command` not in catalog), DF-002 (Phase 2 A4 starting-state mismatch), DF-003 (macOS / Linux real-install verification deferred), DF-004 (`--dry-run` flag not added to either installer), QG-001 (cross-OS verification gate ran on one OS only), WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan reference files in framework-specialist skills). Total open: 7. Phase 5 starts next; it will ship the `skill-eval-loop` workflow skill plus three repo-level Python scripts (`skill_eval_viewer.py`, `aggregate_benchmark.py`, `optimize_skill_description.py`) that DO require explicit registration in both `scripts/installer.sh` and `scripts/installer.ps1` per the AGENTS.md installer-aware-changes rule, plus a CLI-agnostic adapter following the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent. The cross-OS gap from DF-005 / DF-003 will become more important then.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green and JSON catalogs valid. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (193 skills, +4 from Phase 3 close), `data/bundles.json`, `data/marketplace.json`. All 10 theme JSON files parse cleanly. `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` results: 318 passed, 0 failures (no new tests in Phase 4 - the four new skills are content + scripts, not validators). Across all four test suites: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped; `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 318 passed. Total 414 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures. `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` against the 197-skill catalog: 0 errors, 4 warnings (carried from WN-001). Per-skill independent validation: each of the four new skills returns 0 errors and 5 warnings of optional-field kind. `bash -n catalog/skills/developer-experience/web-artifacts-builder/scripts/init-artifact.sh` clean. PowerShell parser-check (`[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`) on `init-artifact.ps1` clean. ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean against `scripts/installer.sh` and `install.sh`.

*   **Current Status**: Phase 4 complete and ready for commit. New files: `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/generative-art/SKILL.md`, `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/generative-art/assets/{flow-field,particle-system,l-system}.html`, `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/theme-tokens/SKILL.md`, `catalog/skills/specialized-domains/theme-tokens/themes/{editorial-serif,brutalist-sans,pastel-soft,terminal-mono,corporate-slate,sunset-warm,forest-cool,mid-century-modern,neon-cyber,newsprint-mono}.json`, `catalog/skills/business-product/internal-comms/SKILL.md`, `catalog/skills/business-product/internal-comms/examples/{3p,status,leadership,faq,incident,project}.md`, `catalog/skills/developer-experience/web-artifacts-builder/SKILL.md`, `catalog/skills/developer-experience/web-artifacts-builder/scripts/init-artifact.{sh,ps1}`. Modified files: `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` (4 new rows; total 187 -> 191), `data/skills.json` (4 new entries; statistics updated), `data/marketplace.json` (3 category counts updated), `CHANGELOG.md` (Phase 4 sections appended), `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (DF-005 added; summary table updated; last-updated date stamped), `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry).
