## [2026-05-08] - Phase 3 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: per-skill bundled-resources layout convention (A13)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 3 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 3 is the foundational tier of the plan - it formalizes the per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` layout convention (item A13 from the comparison report's Section 5), updates AGENTS.md to document it, confirms both installers' existing recursive-copy primitives auto-distribute the new subdirs, extends `make validate` with an orphan-bundle audit, and ships a sentinel `.gitkeep` proving the round-trip. Every later phase (4 through 7) consumes this convention to ship per-skill bundled scripts and reference docs without inflating the SKILL.md body. Phase 3 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump itself remains deferred to after Phase 7 per the user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **A13.1 - AGENTS.md "Per-skill Bundled Resources" subsection added.** New `#### Per-skill Bundled Resources` block placed right after the SKILL.md size norm and before "### 4. Register the skill". Documents the optional `scripts/` (executable code, tier-3 deterministic operations), `references/` (Markdown docs the agent reads on demand), `assets/` (templates / icons / fonts / fixtures) subdirectories that any skill folder MAY contain. Documents file naming (kebab-case, scoped by topic), the cross-shell parity rule (every `.sh` script MUST ship a `.ps1` sibling so Windows users get the same capability), the reference rule (every bundled file MUST be referenced from SKILL.md or another reference file in the same bundle, except `.gitkeep`), the installer behavior (`safe_folder_copy` / `Safe-Folder-Copy` already preserve subdirs via `rsync -a` / `cp -R` / `robocopy /MIR`; no installer edit needed for skill-bundled content), and the orphan-bundle audit. Updated the forward-reference in the Three-Tier Loading Model block from "the Per-skill Bundled Resources section that gets added in Phase 3" to "the Per-skill Bundled Resources subsection further down" since the section now exists. Cross-links from the A13 section to the size norm (when to push body content into `references/`), to the v1.1.3 four-hook precedent (per-CLI parity invariants for `scripts/` directories that ship per-CLI variants), and to the Distribution channels table (the auto-distribution path the new subdirs use).
    *   **A13.2 / A13.3 - Both installers gain a clarifying block comment above the recursive-copy function.** `scripts/installer.sh::safe_folder_copy` (around line 128) gains a 6-line comment explaining that the existing `rsync -a --delete` / `cp -R` primitives already preserve per-skill bundled subdirectories, with a cross-reference to the AGENTS.md convention. `scripts/installer.ps1::Safe-Folder-Copy` (around line 298) gains the same 8-line comment in lockstep, documenting the `robocopy /MIR` parity. **No code change in either function body** - the Phase 3 audit confirmed the recursive-copy primitives already handle the new convention correctly. The plan's sub-task 3.3 also suggested adding a `--dry-run` flag to both installers if missing; this was assessed out of scope (logged as DF-004) because adding a real dry-run mode to two ~1700-line installers is a substantive feature, not a sub-task tweak, and the smoke test verification can be performed by direct invocation of the recursive-copy primitives instead.
    *   **A13.4 - `scripts/validate_skills.py` orphan-bundle audit + `make validate` wiring.** New `validate_skill_bundles(skill_dir, skill_md_content)` function in `scripts/validate_skills.py`: walks a skill directory, lists every file under `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/` (recursive), builds a haystack from the parent SKILL.md plus every `references/*.md`, and emits a warning for each bundled file whose basename does not appear in the haystack. The exempt-filename set is `{".gitkeep"}` (placeholder for future-expansion subdirs). Warnings, not errors - work-in-progress branches can carry orphans without failing CI. New `--bundles-only` CLI flag scopes the validator to this audit only, skipping the full-strict-validator's frontmatter and secret-scan passes (which currently produces 6 false-positive secret detections in code-example blocks of three pre-existing skills - those were never gating CI and are out of Phase 3's scope). The Makefile's `validate` target now runs `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` after the JSON catalog loads.
    *   **A13.4 (cont.) - 8-test pytest module `catalog/hooks/tests/test_skill_bundles.py`.** Covers: (1) orphan in `scripts/` is warned; (2) referenced file is silent; (3) `.gitkeep` is exempt; (4) reference-from-another-reference-file satisfies the audit (a top-level `references/index.md` can act as a TOC for sub-resources); (5) mix of orphan and referenced reports only the orphan; (6) orphan in nested `assets/<subdir>/` is detected; (7) skills with no bundled subdirs return an empty list; (8) `python scripts/validate_skills.py --bundles-only` exits clean against the real 193-skill catalog (subprocess test). Follows the importlib-based loader pattern from `test_format_bash_description.py` because `validate_skills.py` is a top-level script (no package).
    *   **A13.5 - Sentinel `catalog/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/scripts/.gitkeep`.** Empty placeholder that proves the per-skill bundled-resources convention survives a real installer copy. Round-trip-tested on Windows via two copy mechanisms: `cp -R catalog/skills <tmp>` (Git Bash, exercises the same `cp -R` fallback `installer.sh` runs on Linux/macOS) preserved the file at `<tmp>/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/scripts/.gitkeep`; `robocopy catalog\skills <tmp> /MIR` (PowerShell, exercises the exact primitive `installer.ps1::Safe-Folder-Copy` invokes) preserved the same path. The doc-coauthoring SKILL.md gained a brief "Bundled Resources" trailer noting the directory's role; this also satisfies the orphan-audit reference rule for the skill.
    *   **CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` extended with Phase 3 sections.** Intro paragraph updated to mention Phases 1, 2, AND 3 explicitly. New `### Changed` bullets cover the AGENTS.md A13 subsection, both installer comment blocks, and the Makefile validate target wiring. New `### Added` bullets cover the orphan-bundle audit, the 8-test pytest module, and the sentinel `.gitkeep`. New `### Verified` bullets cover the Windows + Git Bash smoke (cp -R primitive) and the Windows + PowerShell smoke (robocopy /MIR primitive). The `### Tests` section updates the catalog/hooks/tests count from 310 to 318 (+8 new tests) and totals from 406 to 414. The `### Known gaps` section adds DF-004 (--dry-run not added) and WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan reference files in framework-specialist skills surfaced by the new audit).
    *   **`docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` updated for Phase 3.** Two new open items: DF-004 (`--dry-run` flag NOT added to either installer - intentionally deferred for scope; the smoke test was performed by direct primitive invocation instead) and WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan files in `fastapi-expert`, `nextjs-expert`, and `react-expert` `references/` subdirs). DF-003's "Suggested next step" updated to remove the obsolete reference to Phase 3 adding dry-run capability. Summary table updated: 6 open items (was 4), 0 resolved this version. Last-updated date stamped 2026-05-08 (Phase 3 complete).

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **whether to add a real `--dry-run` flag to both installers as the plan's sub-task 3.3 suggested**. The conditional clause in the plan said "if `--dry-run` does not exist, add it as a no-op flag that prints what would be copied without touching the filesystem". Three options: (1) add a real, fully-functional dry-run mode that gates every filesystem write in both installers behind a flag check (substantive feature, several-hundred-line refactor across two ~1700-line files); (2) add a stub `--dry-run` flag that doesn't actually skip writes, just prints a banner (misleading - the flag would not behave the way users expect); (3) skip the dry-run flag entirely and verify the smoke test by direct invocation of the underlying recursive-copy primitives (cp -R, robocopy /MIR) on a temp directory. Picked (3) because it gives the same verification confidence (the actual primitive the installer would run is exercised) without taking on a feature-sized refactor in a phase whose actual goal is the per-skill layout convention, not installer architecture. Documented the decision in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` as DF-004 with a clear handoff note: any future plan that wants real dry-run capability should treat it as a coordinated multi-line refactor across both installers, not as a sub-task tweak. The second key call was **whether to wire the new validator into `make validate` strictly or via a narrow flag**. The existing `validate_skills.py` (which had been authored at some earlier point but was never wired into the Makefile) failed strict-validator mode against the current catalog with 6 false-positive secret detections - generic `password = ...` / `secret = ...` / `token = ...` patterns appearing inside code-example blocks of `user-documentation`, `cd-pipeline-generator`, and `rollback-strategy-advisor`. Wiring the strict validator wholesale would have broken `make validate` immediately. Three options: (a) fix the false positives now (out of scope - those are existing skills with code examples that legitimately use those words); (b) tune the secret-detection regex to skip code blocks (small refactor, but changes detection behavior in ways that may need its own audit); (c) add a `--bundles-only` flag that scopes the validator to the new orphan audit only, leaving the strict full-validator available for manual deep audits but not gating CI. Picked (c) because it gives the new orphan-detection capability requested by the plan without inheriting the 6 pre-existing false positives, AND it preserves the strict validator as a callable tool for anyone who wants to run the deeper audit manually. The third call was **how to handle the 4 pre-existing orphan reference files surfaced by the new audit**. Three framework-specialist skills (`fastapi-expert`, `nextjs-expert`, `react-expert`) ship a `references/` subdirectory with files that are not referenced from their parent SKILL.md. Three options: (1) fix them in this phase (small per-skill body edits adding a `## References` section); (2) delete the orphan files (might be genuinely abandoned, might be deliberate offload); (3) document them as a known gap and let a future patch decide per skill. Picked (3) because the audit is explicitly in scope for Phase 3 but the per-skill body edits are out of scope (Phase 3 ships the layout convention, not skill-body normalization), and the warnings do not gate CI. Logged as WN-001 with a recommended fix.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for users. No schema changes, no settings.json changes, no installer behavior changes, no `data/` registry changes, no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up the documentation update in `~/.claude/AGENTS.md`, the AGENTS.md mirror locations for Gemini / Codex, and the new sentinel `~/.claude/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/scripts/.gitkeep` (and platform mirrors). The new `make validate` orphan-bundle audit is a developer-facing change, not a user-facing one. **For DevAI-Hub maintainers and skill authors**: any new skill MAY now ship `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/` subdirectories alongside `SKILL.md`, and `make validate` will warn on any bundled file not referenced from SKILL.md or another reference file. The strict full validator (`python scripts/validate_skills.py` with no flag) remains available as a manual deep-audit tool but is not part of the default validate target. **Important (carried from v1.1.4)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the AGENTS.md update to take effect.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: Two new items in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`: DF-004 (`--dry-run` flag not added to either installer - documented above) and WN-001 (4 pre-existing orphan reference files in framework-specialist skills - recommended fix is a brief `## References` block in each affected SKILL.md, scheduled for a future patch). Carried over from earlier phases: DF-001 (missing `create-skill-or-command` skill referenced by Phase 1's plan prompt), DF-002 (Phase 2 A4 starting-state mismatch), DF-003 (macOS / Linux real-install verification deferred), QG-001 (cross-OS verification gate ran on one OS only). Total open: 6. Phase 4 starts next; it will ship four net-new content skills (`generative-art`, `theme-tokens`, `internal-comms`, `web-artifacts-builder`) that consume the Phase 3 layout convention by bundling templates, themes, examples, and per-skill scripts under the new subdirectory pattern.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green and JSON catalogs valid. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (189 skills, +1 from v1.1.5 baseline of 188), `data/bundles.json` (11 bundles), `data/workflows.json` (17 workflows), `data/templates.json`. ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean against `scripts/installer.sh` and `install.sh`. `bash -n scripts/installer.sh` syntax check clean. `python -m pytest catalog/hooks/tests/` results: 318 passed (was 310; +8 from `test_skill_bundles.py`). Across all four test suites: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped; `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 318 passed. Total 414 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures. New `make validate` pass: per-skill bundled-resources orphan audit (193 skills scanned, 0 errors, 4 pre-existing warnings logged in WN-001).

*   **Current Status**: Phase 3 complete and ready for commit. Modified files: `AGENTS.md` (new "Per-skill Bundled Resources" subsection + cross-link fix), `scripts/installer.sh` (block comment), `scripts/installer.ps1` (block comment, lockstep), `scripts/validate_skills.py` (new function + --bundles-only flag), `Makefile` (validate target wiring), `catalog/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/SKILL.md` (Bundled Resources trailer), `CHANGELOG.md` (Phase 3 sections appended), `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (DF-004 and WN-001 added; DF-003 suggested-next-step updated), `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry). New files: `catalog/hooks/tests/test_skill_bundles.py`, `catalog/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/scripts/.gitkeep`. Plan advances to Phase 4 (net-new content skills batch 1 - `generative-art`, `theme-tokens`, `internal-comms`, `web-artifacts-builder`) once the user reviews and commits.

## [2026-05-08] - Phase 2 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: P0 cleanup + doc-coauthoring skill (A4, A9)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 2 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 2 has two scope items: (A4) resolve the `claude-api` skill index drift identified in the comparison report ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/comparison-skills.md](v1.1.5/comparison-skills.md) Section 5a), and (A9) ship a new `doc-coauthoring` workflow skill that wraps the 3-stage co-authoring pattern (Context Gathering -> Refinement and Structure -> Reader Testing). Phase 2 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump itself remains deferred to after Phase 7 per the user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **A4 - claude-api index drift verified clean (no code change needed).** The plan's sub-task 2.1 described the starting state as `claude-api` row present across `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`, `data/skills.json`, and `data/marketplace.json` while the file `catalog/skills/ai-development/claude-api/SKILL.md` did not exist - and asked the agent to choose between four options (A restore from upstream, B restore from prior history, C de-list, D user-supplied content). At Phase 2 start, the actual state was already consistent: `grep claude-api data/` returned zero matches across all three registry files, and the skill file itself did not exist. The de-list path (option C in the plan's question) had effectively been executed at some earlier point between the comparison report and Phase 2. So sub-task 2.1 was a no-op verification rather than an edit, and the four-option question in the plan was not surfaced. Logged in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` as DF-002 with full context and the suggested next step (if a future plan wants to actually adopt the upstream `claude-api` skill, treat it as a fresh net-new skill addition - not in scope for v1.1.5).
    *   **A9 - `catalog/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/SKILL.md` shipped.** New 114-line workflow skill that drives a 3-stage co-authoring workflow for any non-trivial written artifact (specs, proposals, decision docs, RFCs, ADRs, technical memos, long-form internal writeups). Stage 1 - Context Gathering surfaces audience, purpose, prior art, and constraints in a single batched turn before any prose is written. Stage 2 - Refinement and Structure produces an outline first, then a draft against the accepted outline, with explicit checkpoints to detect drift from the Stage 1 Purpose. Stage 3 - Reader Testing simulates a fresh reader who has not seen the conversation, surfaces a gap list (unbacked claims / missing antecedents / lost-thread transitions) the user resolves or accepts. Frontmatter follows the v1.1.5 pushy-description rule from A14 (lists trigger phrases including "co-author", "co-write", "draft a proposal", "write a spec", "write an RFC", and an explicit `SKIP:` clause for simple READMEs / one-line commit messages / quick inline questions). Common Rationalizations table covers the six most common reasons agents skip stages, with the heaviest weight on Stage 1 inference (the single most common cause of a Stage 2 draft that gets thrown away). Verification section is binary and observable. Cross-links to `business-product/technical-writer`, `developer-experience/writing-editing`, `documentation/technical-documentation`, `business-product/internal-comms`, `developer-experience/idea-refine`, and `developer-experience/spec-driven-development`.
    *   **Registry updates for doc-coauthoring.** `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` gets a new workflow row (total updated 186 -> 187). `data/skills.json` gets a new entry following the full schema (name, title, description, long_description, summary_l0, overview_l1, version=1.0.0, author, category=workflow, language=Multi-language, tags, priority=MEDIUM, based_on, tools_required, path, file, size {lines: 114, tokens_estimate: 2200}, downloads=0, status=production, security 100/100/95); `statistics.total_skills` incremented (188 -> 189), `statistics.categories.workflow` incremented (17 -> 18), `statistics.priorities.MEDIUM` incremented, `total_lines` and `total_tokens_estimate` adjusted. `data/marketplace.json` workflow category `skill_count` incremented (18 -> 19).
    *   **Cross-platform installer parity verified without an installer edit.** Both installers' recursive-copy primitives auto-distribute new skill files: `scripts/installer.sh::safe_folder_copy` uses `rsync -a --delete` (or `cp -R` fallback) on `catalog/skills/`; `scripts/installer.ps1::Safe-Folder-Copy` uses `robocopy ... /MIR`. All 5 platform templates (`templates/ai-instructions/base-{claude,cursor,codex,gemini,opencode}.md` and `generic-instructions.md`) use a `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` placeholder substituted from `data/SKILL_INDEX.md` at install time, so the new index row reaches all 5 supported IDEs in a single edit. No installer edit required - the existing recursive-copy logic already covers Phase 2's surface.
    *   **CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` extended.** Added Phase 2's `### Added` (doc-coauthoring skill + registry updates), `### Removed` (claude-api index drift resolution), `### Verified` (cross-platform installer parity), and updated `### Tests` and `### Known gaps` sections. The intro paragraph now mentions Phases 1 AND 2 explicitly and re-states the version-bump-deferred-to-Phase-7 constraint.

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **how to handle the A4 starting-state mismatch**. The plan's prompt asked the agent to surface a four-option question to the user (restore from upstream / restore from history / de-list / user-supplied content). Three options for handling the mismatch: (1) skip the question entirely (de-list state already exists; nothing to ask); (2) ask the user a different question - "the row is already absent; do you want to restore it now from upstream?" - and stall on input; (3) document the actual state, treat sub-task 2.1 as a no-op verification, log the deviation in known-gaps with a clear handoff note for any future plan that wants to actually adopt `claude-api`. Picked (3) because the plan's intent (resolve the index drift) is satisfied by the consistent-de-listed state and (2) would have stalled an auto-mode session on a question the repo state already answers. The trade-off: a future plan that wants the `claude-api` skill content has to start from scratch rather than from a partial restoration, and the plan's option-A path (upstream restore + de-vendor + DevAI-Hub frontmatter) is documented in DF-002 so it doesn't have to be re-discovered. The second key call was **how pushy to make the doc-coauthoring description**. The A14 / Phase 1 rule says to lead with the action then the trigger surface, list trigger phrases verbatim, and add an explicit `SKIP:` clause. Two failure modes to avoid: (a) under-trigger - the description is so short or generic that the agent skips a clearly-relevant request; (b) over-trigger - the description is so broad that the agent invokes the workflow on every doc-adjacent request, including the simple ones the workflow is overkill for. The shipped description threads both: trigger phrases include "co-author", "co-write", "draft a proposal", "write a spec / RFC / ADR / proposal / design doc", "internal memo", AND the SKIP clause explicitly fences off simple READMEs, single-paragraph commit messages, one-line code comments, and quick inline questions that resolve in one turn. The description is 6x longer than a "clean" one-sentence framing - that is the deliberate cost of the pushy-description rule, paid back the first time the agent would have skipped a relevant invocation. The third call was **whether to ship a `references/` subdirectory now (anticipating the A13 layout convention from Phase 3)**. Phase 3 formalizes per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` subdirs and updates both installers + `make validate` to handle them. Two options: (a) ship doc-coauthoring as SKILL.md only and add references later; (b) pre-empt Phase 3 by including a `references/` subdir now. Picked (a) because Phase 3 also adds `make validate` orphan-bundle detection that flags subdirs not referenced from SKILL.md - shipping references/ now without the validator could mask a future regression. The 114-line SKILL.md fits well within the new 500-line target from A15 and does not need an offload to references yet; the body is self-contained.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE. No schema changes, no settings.json changes, no installer behavior changes, no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up the new `doc-coauthoring/SKILL.md` under `~/.claude/skills/workflow/` (and the `skills/` mirror locations for Gemini / Codex). Cursor / OpenCode / Copilot pick up the new skill via the `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block in their respective instruction-file rendering paths - they get the index-level metadata only, which is the existing platform-coverage state per the AGENTS.md "Platform coverage caveats" matrix and is not changed by Phase 2. **Important (carried from v1.1.4)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new skill to take effect.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: Three new items in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`: DF-002 (A4 plan-described starting state did not match repo state - documented for traceability), DF-003 (macOS / Linux installer dry-run deferred to Phase 3 or a release-readiness smoke task; Phase 2 verification was Windows / Git Bash only), and QG-001 (cross-OS verification gate ran on one OS only, satisfying the plan's "at least one OS" minimum but not the cross-cutting constraint's "cross-OS once per phase" preference - de-prioritized because Phase 2 does not add bundled scripts and the recursive-copy primitive analysis covers the parity question structurally). Carried over from Phase 1: DF-001 (the missing `create-skill-or-command` skill referenced by the plan's sub-task 1.1 prompt). Phase 3 starts next; it formalizes the per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` layout convention (A13) and adds the `--dry-run` capability to both installers, after which DF-003 / QG-001 can be retired by running the dry-run on at least one Unix host.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green and JSON catalogs valid against the new state. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (189 skills, +1 from v1.1.5 baseline of 188), `data/bundles.json` (11 bundles), `data/workflows.json` (17 workflows), `data/templates.json`, `data/marketplace.json`. ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean against `scripts/installer.sh` and `install.sh`. `bash -n scripts/installer.sh` syntax check clean. `python -m pytest` results identical to v1.1.5 baseline: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped; `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 310 passed. Total 406 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures - Phase 2 adds no test surface (the new skill is a Markdown file; the registry edits are JSON and the index Markdown that `make validate` already covers).

*   **Current Status**: Phase 2 complete and ready for commit. New file: `catalog/skills/workflow/doc-coauthoring/SKILL.md`. Modified files: `data/SKILL_INDEX.md`, `data/skills.json`, `data/marketplace.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`, `docs/DEVLOG.md` (this entry). Plan advances to Phase 3 (the foundational A13 layout convention - per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` subdir support, both installers + `make validate` extended) once the user reviews and commits.

## [2026-05-08] - Phase 1 of v1.1.5 adoption-skills plan: Skill-native doc edits (A14, A17, A15, A11, A12)

*   **Goal**: Execute Phase 1 of the v1.1.5 `adoption-skills` plan ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md](v1.1.5/plans/adoption-skills.md)). Phase 1 is the "skill-native" tier of the plan - five doc-only edits to existing tracked Markdown files that close the cheapest capability gaps identified in the comparison report ([docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/comparison-skills.md](v1.1.5/comparison-skills.md)) without adding any new file to the catalog file tree. Phase 1 ships under `[Unreleased]` in the CHANGELOG; the version bump itself is deferred to after Phase 7 per a user-supplied constraint that the entire seven-phase plan ships as a single v1.2.0 release rather than as seven incremental patch versions.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **A14 - Pushy description guidance** institutionalized in two places. AGENTS.md "Adding a New Skill -> Write SKILL.md" gains a new "Description style: combat undertriggering" block that documents Claude's measurable tendency to under-trigger on narrow descriptions, the four rules of pushy descriptions (verbatim trigger phrases, explicit `SKIP:` clauses, synonym coverage, action-then-trigger structure), and a 6-words-vs-60-words before / after example. The same guidance is mirrored into `catalog/skills/workflow/create-custom-command/SKILL.md` (commands also have descriptions and the same under-triggering risk applies); the SKILL.md gains a Common Rationalizations table it didn't previously have, with three rebuttals targeting the most common reasons authors leave descriptions narrow.
    *   **A17 - Three-Tier Loading Model** documented as a new `#### Three-Tier Loading Model` subsection under "Write SKILL.md" in AGENTS.md. Documents the three tiers explicitly: tier 1 (always loaded, ~150-300 tokens, determines triggering) = `name` + `description` + `summary_l0` + `overview_l1`; tier 2 (loaded on trigger) = SKILL.md body, target ≤500 lines; tier 3 (loaded on demand) = bundled `scripts/`, `references/`, `assets/` per the A13 convention from Phase 3, with the critical performance affordance that scripts EXECUTE without their source being loaded into context. Practical authoring implications added: push some-of-the-time content to references, push deterministic steps to scripts, keep tier 1 fields tight because they pay tokens on every catalog read across every session.
    *   **A15 - SKILL.md size norm reconciled** in AGENTS.md. The previous "Keep SKILL.md under 800 lines." rule replaced with "Target ≤500 lines for the SKILL.md body. Soft cap 800 lines." Beyond 500 lines, authors add a `references/` subdirectory with a TOC and link to it from the body. Beyond 800 lines, the skill MUST be split or refactored before merge. Existing skills that exceed 500 lines are explicitly grandfathered - the norm is forward-looking and applies to new and substantially-rewritten skills only, so this change does not retroactively flag any existing catalog skill.
    *   **A11 - Aesthetic-distinctiveness lens** added to `catalog/skills/developer-experience/frontend-ui-engineering/SKILL.md` as a new "Aesthetic Distinctiveness" section after Step 7. Documents the recognizable AI-default aesthetic that production-grade UI must avoid (centered hero + three-card grid + gradient button + Inter typeface + uniform padding + 12px border-radius), six countermeasures (custom typography pairing, asymmetric layout, intentional density, distinctive accent color, motion that means something, copy with a voice), three reference patterns the agent can pick one of (editorial multi-column, brutalist over-borders, restrained motion), and a process step (write a one-page direction note up-front, not as a polish pass). Adds a Common Rationalizations row rebutting "the agent's default looks fine, we can polish later" and a Verification entry requiring the project to deviate from the AI-default in at least 2-3 dimensions.
    *   **A12 - Static-poster / print workflow** added to `catalog/skills/developer-experience/creative-generation/SKILL.md` as a new "Static Poster / Print Workflow" section after the existing Ideation section. Documents a deliberate two-step approach: step 1 writes a 30-80 line Markdown design philosophy fixing color palette, typography, composition principles, and 1-2 reference movements; step 2 renders the actual `.png` / `.pdf` output via `pptx-generation` / `docx-generation` / `pdf-document-generation` for standard formats or a single-purpose Pillow / matplotlib script for one-off bespoke layouts. Generative / algorithmic / interactive visual outputs (p5.js sketches, HTML canvas) are explicitly scoped OUT - those belong to the `generative-art` skill that Phase 4 / A1 will ship, not here. Adds a Common Rationalizations table (which the file did not previously have) with three rebuttals targeting the most common reasons authors skip the philosophy step.
    *   **CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` populated.** A new `### Changed` block under `## [Unreleased]` lists all five edits in sectioned-bullet form (Skill-authoring guidance in AGENTS.md / Skill body edits / Tests / Known gaps), with a Tests section recording the four-suite green run (37 + 36 + 23 + 310 = 406 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures) and a Known gaps section pointing at `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`. No version-string bump anywhere - the entry stays under `[Unreleased]` until Phase 7 wraps.

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **how to handle the missing `create-skill-or-command` skill** in sub-task 1.1. The plan prompt said to update both `create-skill-or-command/SKILL.md` and `create-custom-command/SKILL.md`, but only the latter exists in the catalog (`Glob` for `catalog/skills/**/create-skill*/SKILL.md` returned no results). The skill name appears in the harness's global skill list, but that's an external skill (likely a Claude Code built-in or a separately-installed skill), not a DevAI-Hub catalog entry. The de facto skill-authoring guide for DevAI-Hub authors is `AGENTS.md` "Adding a New Skill -> Write SKILL.md", which sits at line 59-112 of AGENTS.md and documents the YAML frontmatter, body sections, registration steps, and validation. Three options: (a) skip A14 for the missing file and apply only to `create-custom-command`; (b) create a new `create-skill-or-command` SKILL.md that duplicates AGENTS.md content; (c) apply A14 to AGENTS.md "Adding a New Skill" instead. Option (a) leaves the most important location (the skill-authoring guide for actual skill authors) untouched. Option (b) creates a documentation duplication that will drift. Option (c) applies the rule where it actually matters, with a cross-reference from `create-custom-command` for command authors. Picked (c) and logged the decision in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` as DF-001 with a clear "suggested next step" pointing at whether to formalize a dedicated catalog skill (recommendation: leave in AGENTS.md, since AGENTS.md is the canonical authoring guide and a catalog skill that duplicates it would drift). The second key call was **how aggressive to be on the aesthetic-distinctiveness section in A11**. The comparison report's Section 4 framed the upstream pattern as a checklist; in DevAI-Hub the existing `frontend-ui-engineering` skill already has an opinionated voice in its Common Rationalizations table, so the new section was written to match that voice rather than to copy the upstream's flatter checklist style. The "What the AI default looks like (avoid)" sub-section names the visual pattern explicitly so the agent recognizes when it's about to ship that exact thing - which is the fix-mode the comparison report identified as missing. The third call was **whether to inline a Common Rationalizations table into `creative-generation/SKILL.md`** for A12. The file is short (35 lines pre-edit) and didn't follow the standard DevAI-Hub skill structure (no When-to-Use, no Verification, no Common Rationalizations). Adding a full restructure would have exceeded Phase 1's "five doc-only edits" scope. Compromise: A12 ships the Static Poster section AND a Common Rationalizations table specifically about the static-poster decision, leaving the rest of the file's structure unchanged. Future plan items can normalize the rest of the file if needed.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE. No schema changes, no settings.json changes, no installer behavior changes, no `data/` registry changes, no version-string changes. Existing v1.1.5 installations re-run the installer and pick up the five patched Markdown files under `~/.claude/skills/.../` and `~/.claude/AGENTS.md` (and the `skills/` mirror locations for Gemini / Codex). Cursor / OpenCode / Copilot pick up the AGENTS.md edits via their respective instruction-file rendering paths; the three skill-body edits also reach those three platforms via the `{{SKILL_INDEX}}` block, but only as index-level metadata - the per-skill body itself only lands on Claude Code / Gemini / Codex per the AGENTS.md "Platform coverage caveats" matrix, which is the existing platform-coverage state and is not changed by Phase 1. **Important (carried from v1.1.4)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them; restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / OpenCode sessions for the new bodies to take effect.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: One open item logged in `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (DF-001 - the missing `create-skill-or-command` skill referenced by the plan's sub-task 1.1 prompt). No new bugs, no suppressed lint rules, no skipped tests, no quality-gate bypasses. Phase 2 of the plan starts next; it will resolve the `claude-api` skill index drift and ship a new `doc-coauthoring` skill. Phase 3 (the foundational A13 layout convention) front-loads the per-skill `scripts/` / `references/` / `assets/` subdir support that Phases 4-7 depend on. The version bump to v1.2.0 happens once Phase 7 wraps.

*   **Test Posture**: All four test suites green and JSON catalogs valid. `python -c "json.load(...)"` passes for `data/skills.json` (188 skills), `data/bundles.json` (11 bundles), `data/workflows.json` (17 workflows), `data/templates.json`. ShellCheck (`--severity=warning`) clean against `scripts/installer.sh` and `install.sh`. `python -m pytest` results: `extensions/devai-skill-server` 37 passed; `extensions/devai-code-search` 36 passed, 1 skipped, 52 deprecation warnings (pre-existing third-party `pathspec` library, not introduced by Phase 1); `extensions/devai-web-fetch` 23 passed; `catalog/hooks/tests` 310 passed. Total 406 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures across all four suites - identical to the v1.1.5 release baseline since Phase 1 is doc-only on Markdown files and does not touch any tested surface.

*   **Current Status**: Phase 1 complete and ready for commit. Five Markdown files modified, one new file created (`docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md`), one entry appended to `[Unreleased]` in CHANGELOG.md, one entry prepended to DEVLOG.md (this entry). Working tree shows exactly the expected six modified files plus `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/known-gaps.md` (new) and `docs/archive/v1/v1.1.5/development/history/...` (Phase 1 session history file, generated next). Plan advances to Phase 2 (P0 cleanup + doc-coauthoring skill) once the user reviews and commits.

## [2026-05-06] - Release 1.1.5: Sectioned-Bullet Structure for Commit Messages

*   **Goal**: Close the structural gap that v1.1.1 / v1.1.4 didn't address. v1.1.1 forbade hard-wrapping inside paragraphs and bullets; v1.1.4 propagated that rule into the downstream commands that actually generate commit messages (`/implement-phase`, `/wrap-up-session`, `/update-version`). But neither release said anything about *body structure* - the agent could still produce a body of 5-6 long flowing paragraphs separated by blank lines, which forces reviewers to read every paragraph linearly to find the change for a specific component. Reported live: a v1.1.4-generated commit (the next `/implement-phase` run after the v1.1.4 install) came out exactly that shape - properly unwrapped paragraphs but six of them in a row, no labeled sections, no scannable anchors. Patch release because the change is content-only across five command and skill files (no schema, no installer, no test changes).

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **`catalog/commands/generate-commit-message.md` body rules + example replaced.** Adds a "Body structure (CRITICAL for non-trivial commits)" rule requiring labeled sections with bullets (headers ending in a colon, grouped by component / module / theme). The 1-2 sentence intro paragraph + sectioned-bullet body shape replaces the prior "paragraphs and/or bullet points" guidance. Always treats **Tests** and **Known gaps** / **Deviations** as dedicated final sections. Whitespace constraint added: exactly one blank line between sections; bullets contiguous within a section. The example block was rewritten to demonstrate the sectioned style on a realistic multi-component commit (Reporting package / Packaging and paths / Desktop UI / Tests / Known gaps - patterned after the user-supplied "after" example). A counter-example block was added showing the multi-paragraph flowing-prose body shape that the agent must NOT produce, even with the no-hard-wrap rule honored.
    *   **`catalog/skills/workflow/code-commit-workflow/SKILL.md` Body subsection + Examples + Rationalizations + Checklist all extended.** New rule paragraph in the Body subsection mirrors the canonical command. New Good Example added at the end of the Good Examples section showing the sectioned style on the same Phase 6 docxtpl scenario (paralleling the user-supplied "after" example). New Bad Example showing the multi-paragraph flowing-prose body shape. New Common Rationalizations row rebutting "flowing paragraphs read better than bulleted lists for prose-heavy commits" - the agent's likely failure mode given its training on academic prose conventions. Quality Checklist gains a sectioned-structure item explicitly naming the Tests and Known gaps / Deviations sections.
    *   **`catalog/commands/implement-phase.md` post-phase sub-step 6 inline rule expanded.** Adds the sectioned-bullet rule with implementation-specific suggested headers. The implement-phase context is the highest-volume commit-generation path in the project (every phase implementation produces one commit), so this is the most important file to get right.
    *   **`catalog/commands/wrap-up-session.md` Phase 7 inline rule expanded.** Adds the sectioned-bullet rule with wrap-up-specific suggested headers (`Session history:` / `DEVLOG:` / `Documentation:` / `Gitignore:` / `Memory:` / `Version bump:`).
    *   **`catalog/commands/update-version.md` Phase E4 inline rule + example + counter-example replaced.** Format and rules block now requires the sectioned-bullet structure using CHANGELOG section names as headers directly (`Added:` / `Changed:` / `Fixed:` / `Removed:` / `Tests:`). The example was rewritten to map CHANGELOG entries to the matching section headers (Phase B layout / gitignore changes also called out as their own sections if relevant); a counter-example showing the previous flat `Changes:` bullet-soup style was added so the agent cannot fall back to that shape. Disambiguation note added: the 72-char subject-line cap is a hard limit, not a wrap (re-stated from v1.1.4 to make sure the structure rule doesn't accidentally re-introduce confusion about that line).
    *   **Canonical version bump 1.1.4 -> 1.1.5** across the same 13 files as v1.1.4. CHANGELOG migrated from `[Unreleased]` to `[1.1.5] - 2026-05-06`; CHANGELOG footer Unreleased link bumped to `v1.1.5...HEAD`, and a new `[1.1.5]: v1.1.4...v1.1.5` comparison link added.

*   **Design Rationale**: Most important call was **how strictly to enforce the sectioned style**. Three options: (1) require sectioned-bullet structure for *every* commit body, including trivial 1-2 file commits where it's overkill; (2) require it only for "non-trivial" commits with no clear definition; (3) define the threshold explicitly as "any commit touching multiple components." Option 3 - the choice this release makes - has a clear rule the agent can apply (count the distinct components in the diff; if > 1, use sectioned style). The same rule now appears in all five files with identical wording, so a future regression in any one is detectable by diffing against the others. The second key call was **what counts as a section header**. The user's example used `Reporting package (`src/reporting/`):` style (component path in backticks, then a colon) which works well for code-component commits. For wrap-up commits, headers are activity-typed (`DEVLOG:`, `Documentation:`); for release commits, they map directly to CHANGELOG section names (`Added:`, `Changed:`, `Fixed:`). The release deliberately gives different suggested header palettes per command so the agent has a context-appropriate starting point rather than a generic "use sections" instruction it has to invent headers for. The third call was **rejecting an enforcement test** - a static check that scans command bodies for "generate" + "commit message" without a sectioned-style rule could prevent regressions but adds maintenance burden and false positives. Tracked as a v1.2.0+ candidate; for now, the cross-file consistency of the rule wording IS the regression signal.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE. No schema changes, no settings.json changes, no installer behavior changes, no test surface changes. Existing v1.1.4 installations re-run the installer and pick up the five patched command + skill bodies under `~/.claude/commands/` / `~/.claude/skills/` (and `commands/` / `workflows/` / `prompts/` mirror locations for Gemini / Codex). **Important (carried from v1.1.4)**: Claude Code reads slash command and skill bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them. After running the v1.1.5 installer, restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / Copilot sessions for the new bodies to take effect.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: No new follow-ups opened by v1.1.5. The sectioned-bullet rule depends on the agent's interpretation of "what counts as a component" in the diff - for diffs that span multiple loosely-related files in the same package, the agent might collapse them into one bullet or split them into multiple sections; that judgment call is left to the agent because hard-coding it would over-fit the rule. Carried-over follow-ups from earlier patches remain (5 PSUseApprovedVerbs warnings in `scripts/installer.ps1` from v1.1.0 era; Codex / OpenCode CLI invocation forms may need adjusting if their CLIs change syntax). A future enhancement could add a static-presence linter for the sectioned-style rule across command bodies (already noted in v1.1.4 as a v1.2.0+ candidate); v1.1.5 reinforces the case for it but doesn't add it.

*   **Test Posture**: All four quality gates clean. `make validate` (JSON integrity) passes. `bash -n` clean on installer.sh and session-start.sh. Hook test suite from v1.1.3 (310/310) still passes against v1.1.5 with zero changes - this release is content-only on five command and skill bodies and does not touch any tested surface. The user-facing fix is verifiable by re-running `/implement-phase` after a session restart and inspecting whether the generated commit message uses labeled section headers (`Reporting package:`, `Packaging:`, `Desktop UI:`, etc.) or falls back to the multi-paragraph flowing-prose shape.

*   **Current Status**: All v1.1.5 surfaces shipped. The annotated `v1.1.5` tag is created locally with the release commit; push to origin happens at user direction.

## [2026-05-06] - Release 1.1.4: Commit-Message No-Hard-Wrap Rule Reaches the Generators

*   **Goal**: Close a gap that v1.1.1 missed and v1.1.3 did not catch. v1.1.1 extended the no-hard-wrap rule from bullets to all body content in `/generate-commit-message` and the `code-commit-workflow` skill, on the assumption that any command path ending in commit-message generation would route through the patched canonical command. That assumption was wrong: a textual reference to `/generate-commit-message` inside another command body (e.g. `/implement-phase` post-phase sub-step 6 "Run `/generate-commit-message`") is just text - the agent does NOT auto-load that file's body when it sees the reference. So the canonical fix only applied when the user typed `/generate-commit-message` directly. Every other path - `/implement-phase`, `/wrap-up-session`, `/update-version` - kept producing commit messages wrapped at ~70 columns. The user reported the bug live: a v0.3.0 phase-implementation commit, generated from a fresh v1.1.3 install in a brand-new Claude Code session, came out fully wrapped. Patch release because the change is content-only on three command bodies (no schema, no installer, no permission edits) and strictly additive (each patched command gains a new rule paragraph; nothing is removed or restructured).

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **`catalog/commands/implement-phase.md` post-phase sub-step 6 patched.** Sub-step 6 is the one that runs at the end of every successful `/implement-phase` execution and tells the agent to "Run `/generate-commit-message`" as a sub-step. Inlined the explicit no-hard-wrap rule with all three reinforcement clauses: (a) every paragraph and every bullet point in the commit body and footer MUST be a single continuous source line regardless of length, (b) explicit wrap-column callouts (50, 72, 80, 100) so the agent cannot rationalize one as "the convention," and (c) the obsolete-72-char-convention rebuttal that names modern Git, GitHub, GitLab, and `git log` as soft-wrap-on-display so the agent cannot fall back to the historical justification. Subject-line 50-char cap explicitly carved out as the only exception (a hard limit, not a wrap).
    *   **`catalog/commands/wrap-up-session.md` Phase 7 patched.** Phase 7 ("Final Commit") is structurally identical to `/implement-phase` sub-step 6 - it tells the agent to "Run `/generate-commit-message`" with scope guidance. Same rule injection, before the existing scope-and-format bullets.
    *   **`catalog/commands/update-version.md` Phase E4 rules extended.** Phase E4 ("Generate Commit Message") was different from the other two: it does NOT delegate to `/generate-commit-message`, it has its OWN inline format and rules block (lines 418-437). The v1.1.1 fix didn't apply because there was no transitive reference. The single bullet "Keep each bullet point concise and on a single line" was the closest the section got to the no-wrap rule, but it covered only bullets and it carried the dangerous "single line" framing without the explicit "regardless of length" / "no column-width breaks" reinforcement. Replaced with the full paragraph-and-bullet rule. Also disambiguated the existing "First line: `vX.X.X:` followed by a concise summary (under 72 chars)" rule by adding "The 72-char cap on the subject line is a hard limit, not a wrap" so the agent cannot generalize the cap to body lines.
    *   **Canonical version bump 1.1.3 -> 1.1.4** across the same 13 files as v1.1.3: `.claude-plugin/{plugin,marketplace}.json`, `data/{bundles,marketplace,templates,workflows}.json`, `catalog/hooks/session-start.sh`, `scripts/installer.sh`, `scripts/installer.ps1`, `catalog/skills/README.md`, `guides/SUBAGENTS_GUIDE.md`, `infrastructure/{hooks,tools}/README.md`. CHANGELOG migrated from `[Unreleased]` to `[1.1.4] - 2026-05-06`; CHANGELOG footer Unreleased link bumped to `v1.1.4...HEAD`, new `[1.1.4]: v1.1.3...v1.1.4` comparison link added.

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **inline duplication vs. an upstream "include this skill" mechanism**. Slash commands in Claude Code do not have a transitive-import primitive - "Run `/generate-commit-message`" in a command body is text, not an import directive. Three options exist: (1) inline the rule in every command that generates commit messages, accepting per-command duplication (~5 lines x 3 commands = 15 lines of duplication, which can drift); (2) build a "load this skill" sub-command that the agent runs first to read the canonical guidance into context, accepting an extra tool round-trip per invocation; (3) move the rule to a global like `CLAUDE.md` and trust the agent to apply it. Option 3 is what v1.1.1 effectively assumed - and it failed empirically because the agent's training on the obsolete 72-char convention overrode the global no-wrap rule whenever the word "commit message" appeared near generation time. Option 2 is fragile (depends on the agent reliably executing the side-effect read; nothing forces it). Option 1 - this release's choice - is the most robust against agent context drift because the rule is in the same context window as the generation step. The duplication cost is real but bounded; a future release could add a static check that flags any new command body containing "generate" and "commit message" without the no-wrap rule, but that's a v1.2.0+ candidate. The second key call was **scope of the patch**. Only three commands were known to generate commit messages directly today; I deliberately did not sweep through every other command speculatively (`/generate-changelog`, `/generate-devlog`, etc., produce structured Markdown but not commit messages). Every changed line traces to the user's reported bug.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE. No schema changes, no settings.json changes, no installer behavior changes, no permission edits. Existing v1.1.3 installations re-run the installer and pick up the three patched command bodies under `~/.claude/commands/` (and the `commands/` / `workflows/` / `prompts/` mirror locations for Gemini / Codex). **Important**: Claude Code reads slash command bodies at session start and does NOT hot-reload them. After running the v1.1.4 installer, restart any already-running Claude Code / Cursor / Gemini / Codex / Copilot sessions for the new bodies to take effect.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: No new follow-ups opened by v1.1.4. The carryover follow-ups from v1.1.3 remain (5 PSUseApprovedVerbs warnings in `scripts/installer.ps1` from v1.1.0 era; Codex / OpenCode CLI invocation forms may need adjusting if their CLIs change syntax in future releases). A future enhancement could be a static "commit-message rule presence" linter that flags any new command body containing both "commit message" and "generate" without the explicit no-wrap rule, preventing this exact regression class from recurring; tracked as a v1.2.0+ candidate.

*   **Test Posture**: All four quality gates clean. `make validate` (JSON integrity) passes. `bash -n` clean on installer.sh and session-start.sh. The hook test suite from v1.1.3 (310/310) still passes against v1.1.4 with zero changes - this release is content-only on three command bodies and does not touch any tested surface. The user-facing bug fix is verifiable by re-running `/implement-phase` and inspecting whether the generated commit message has paragraph or bullet line breaks at any column width; per-session restart is required after install so the patched command body is loaded fresh.

*   **Current Status**: All v1.1.4 surfaces shipped. The annotated `v1.1.4` tag is created locally with the release commit; push to origin happens at user direction.

## [2026-05-06] - Release 1.1.3: Platform-Independent Pre-Commit Review Hooks

*   **Goal**: v1.1.2 shipped a Claude-only git pre-commit review hook (`claude-diff-review.sh`) and an `/install-claude-pre-commit-hook` command. Within hours, the user pushed back on the architecture: forcing Cursor / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode users to install Anthropic's `claude` CLI just to use the review hook violated the project's cross-platform independence rule (each AI platform should use its own native equivalents, never depend on a sibling vendor). v1.1.3 reworks the v1.1.2 design into four parallel, fully-independent hooks - one per supported AI CLI - and replaces the Claude-only slash command with an auto-detecting variant. Patch release because the change is strictly additive at the hook level (the v1.1.2 Claude hook is preserved unchanged) and the breaking removal of the v1.1.2 slash command is mitigated by zero-hour migration window (v1.1.2 shipped earlier the same day).

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **Three new platform-parallel hook scripts**: `catalog/hooks/gemini-diff-review.sh` (calls `gemini -p`), `catalog/hooks/codex-diff-review.sh` (calls `codex exec`), and `catalog/hooks/opencode-diff-review.sh` (calls `opencode run`). Each is a fully self-contained ~125-line bash script copied from the v1.1.2 Claude hook with three substitutions: the binary name in the `command -v` presence check, the CLI invocation in the run-command call, and the platform-name string in every log-message prefix. Bypass paths, merge / rebase short-circuit, fail-open behavior, diff-size cap, and `VERDICT: PASS|WARN|BLOCK` parsing are byte-identical across the four variants because they all need to behave the same way as a git pre-commit hook regardless of which CLI runs the actual review.
    *   **Codex and OpenCode use a combined-argument pattern** rather than the `printf '%s' "$DIFF" | claude -p "$PROMPT"` stdin-context pattern that the Claude and Gemini hooks use. Codex's `exec` subcommand and OpenCode's `run` subcommand do not consistently read context from stdin across versions / model providers, so the prompt and diff are concatenated into a single positional argument (`codex exec "$COMBINED"` / `opencode run "$COMBINED"`). The 50 KB diff cap remains relevant because shell argument-list limits on Linux are ~128 KB total - well above the cap - and on Windows are ~32 KB total, so the cap also serves as a Windows-safety guarantee for the Codex / OpenCode variants specifically.
    *   **Slash command renamed and rewritten**. v1.1.2 `catalog/commands/install-claude-pre-commit-hook.md` was deleted via `git rm` (zero-hour migration window since v1.1.2 shipped the same day). New `catalog/commands/install-pre-commit-review-hook.md` replaces it with auto-detection logic: runs `command -v claude/gemini/codex/opencode` to discover which CLIs are on PATH, then either auto-selects (one match), asks the user (multiple matches), or asks the user to install one (zero matches). Optional `--platform=<name>` flag forces a specific platform (useful when the user wants a hook for a CLI not yet installed); `--force` flag skips existing-hook prompts. The marker-comment detection now matches any of the four `# <platform>-diff-review.sh` headers so a re-run can detect which platform is currently installed and offer to switch / re-install cleanly.
    *   **Test refactor**: v1.1.2 `catalog/hooks/tests/test_claude_diff_review.py` was renamed to `test_diff_review_hooks.py` (plural) via `git mv` to preserve history, then rewritten to parametrize every test class over a `(hook_filename, cli_binary_name)` tuple list. Each of the 11 logical scenarios from v1.1.2 now runs 4 times - one per platform variant - for 44 parametrized tests. Plus a new `TestPlatformIndependence` class (4 more tests, one per variant) that inspects each hook's source file directly and asserts no `command -v <other-cli>` check or `<other-cli> -p` / `<other-cli> exec` / `<other-cli> run` invocation exists. Total 48 tests in the new file. Full hook suite: 310 tests (262 v1.1.1 baseline + 48 new), all green.
    *   **Installer wiring expanded**. The single `safe_copy` line from v1.1.2 in both `scripts/installer.sh` (line 1424) and `scripts/installer.ps1` (line 1733) was replaced by a 4-element loop covering all four `*-diff-review.sh` variants. The loop body still uses `safe_copy` / `Safe-Copy` (with the existing pre-existing-warning verb-naming - PSUseApprovedVerbs - inherited unchanged from v1.1.0 era, deliberately not refactored as that would be scope-creep cleanup unrelated to the v1.1.3 directive). Per-variant existence guard (`[ -f ]` / `Test-Path`) silently skips missing files so a partial catalog still installs cleanly.
    *   **Canonical version bump 1.1.2 -> 1.1.3** across the same 13 files as v1.1.2: `.claude-plugin/{plugin,marketplace}.json`, `data/{bundles,marketplace,templates,workflows}.json`, `catalog/hooks/session-start.sh`, `scripts/installer.sh`, `scripts/installer.ps1`, `catalog/skills/README.md`, `guides/SUBAGENTS_GUIDE.md`, `infrastructure/{hooks,tools}/README.md`. `session-start.sh` `COMMAND_COUNT` stays at 33 (one slash command removed, one added - net zero). CHANGELOG migrated from `[Unreleased]` to `[1.1.3] - 2026-05-06`; CHANGELOG footer Unreleased link bumped to `v1.1.3...HEAD`, and a new `[1.1.3]: v1.1.2...v1.1.3` comparison link added.

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **fully duplicate scripts vs. shared bash library**. A shared library (`catalog/hooks/diff-review-lib.sh` sourced by each thin platform-specific wrapper) would be ~75% less code duplication and easier to maintain a single shared prompt, but it has two real problems for the git-hook use case. First, the hook gets copied into `.git/hooks/pre-commit` as a freestanding artifact - a dependency on `~/.devai-hub/hooks/diff-review-lib.sh` makes the hook fail if the user moves their dotfiles, switches DevAI-Hub install paths, or copies the hook to a new machine. Self-contained scripts survive all those cases. Second, the user's directive ("each platform should be independent") was clear; a shared-library design would technically be 4 thin wrappers but the actual review logic would still funnel through one shared file, which is a different shape than the user asked for. The duplication cost is real (~360 extra lines of bash) but the durability and clarity gains outweigh it. The second key call was **breaking the v1.1.2 command vs. shipping an alias**. The v1.1.2 slash command had only existed for a few hours when v1.1.3 went out, so an alias adds permanent vestigial code to defend against a migration that affects nobody. Per the project rule "Avoid backwards-compatibility hacks ... if you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely" - clean removal was correct. The third call was **Cursor and Copilot scope**. Both lack a usable headless review CLI; pretending to support them via a stub hook would be dishonest. Documenting the gap explicitly in the slash command body lets the affected users self-route either to one of the four supported CLIs as a side dependency or to CI-side review.

*   **Migration Impact**: Effectively NONE for v1.1.2 users. The v1.1.2 Claude hook (`~/.devai-hub/hooks/claude-diff-review.sh`) is unchanged byte-for-byte in v1.1.3. Existing v1.1.2 installations of `.git/hooks/pre-commit` (created by the now-removed `/install-claude-pre-commit-hook`) keep working - the hook source still exists at the same path. The only breaking change is the v1.1.2 slash command name, which had a few-hour install window and was almost certainly invoked by nobody beyond the release author.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: The 5 PSUseApprovedVerbs warnings in `scripts/installer.ps1` (`Safe-Copy`, `Safe-Folder-Copy`, `Detect-Languages`, `Detect-ProjectMetadata`, `Render-Template`) pre-date v1.1.0 and remain - inherited unchanged because every cmdlet rename would propagate to dozens of call sites. Tracked as a v1.2.0 candidate. The Codex and OpenCode hook variants use `codex exec` and `opencode run` respectively as the most-documented current invocation forms; future CLI changes may require updating the call line in those two scripts. If users find their CLI uses different syntax, they can edit `~/.devai-hub/hooks/<platform>-diff-review.sh` directly - the script is self-contained and not regenerated by the installer between releases. Cursor and GitHub Copilot remain genuinely unsupported by this command; that gap is documented in the slash command body, not paved over.

*   **Test Posture**: All four quality gates clean. `make validate` (JSON integrity) passes. `bash -n` clean on all 4 hook scripts plus installer.sh and session-start.sh. The new test file (`test_diff_review_hooks.py`) is 48/48 green; full hook test suite is 310/310 against v1.1.3. The v1.1.1 installer-smoke resilience fix from earlier today still works as designed: the v1.1.3 bump did not require a test-code change to keep the smoke test passing.

*   **Current Status**: All v1.1.3 surfaces shipped. The annotated `v1.1.3` tag is created locally with the release commit; push to origin happens at user direction.

## [2026-05-06] - Release 1.1.2: Opt-In Git Pre-Commit Claude Review Hook

*   **Goal**: Close a gap that the existing agent-level `secret-scan.sh` Claude Code hook cannot close on its own. `secret-scan.sh` is a `PreToolUse:Write/Edit` hook, so it only fires when Claude Code itself proposes a write. Code edited from Cursor, Copilot, Codex, Gemini, a terminal editor, or a teammate's machine never passes through that gate. A patch release adds an **opt-in** git pre-commit hook that pipes `git diff --cached` through `claude -p` for an LLM review. Because the trigger surface is `git commit`, the new hook fires on every commit regardless of which agent (or human) authored the change. The two hooks are complementary, not redundant: regex at write-time (deterministic, zero token cost, Claude Code only) plus LLM at commit-time (judgment-driven, catches debug artifacts and TODOs that regex never could, all editor paths). Patch release rather than minor bump because the change is strictly additive and gated behind explicit per-repo opt-in - no existing repository's behavior changes until the user runs the new slash command.

*   **What Changed**:
    *   **`catalog/hooks/claude-diff-review.sh`** - the hook itself, ~120 lines of bash. Reads `git diff --cached`, caps the diff at 50 KB by default (configurable via `DEVAI_DIFF_REVIEW_MAX_BYTES`), pipes it through `claude -p` with a strict review prompt covering hardcoded credentials, debug artifacts, unfinished TODOs, and large commented-out code blocks. Parses Claude's response on the first line (`VERDICT: PASS|WARN|BLOCK`) and exits accordingly: `PASS` silent allow, `WARN` prints findings to stderr but allows the commit, `BLOCK` refuses the commit with exit 1. Fail-open on every error path - missing CLI, empty response, unparseable verdict, oversized diff, merge / cherry-pick / rebase in progress, env-var bypass set. Three explicit bypass paths: `DEVAI_DIFF_REVIEW_DISABLE=1 git commit ...` per-commit override, git's standard `--no-verify` flag, and the configurable size cap.
    *   **`catalog/commands/install-claude-pre-commit-hook.md`** - new slash command. When invoked from inside a target repo, copies `~/.devai-hub/hooks/claude-diff-review.sh` to that repo's `.git/hooks/pre-commit`. Detects pre-existing hooks: if the file already contains the DevAI-Hub marker (`# claude-diff-review.sh`), reports already-installed and exits cleanly; otherwise shows the existing hook to the user and offers replace / abort / chain-manually paths, with a `.git/hooks/pre-commit.devai-backup-<timestamp>` backup written before any overwrite. Cross-platform: works on Linux, macOS, and Windows (Git for Windows runs hooks via its bundled bash, so the bash source runs natively).
    *   **Installer wiring** in both `scripts/installer.sh` (line 1424) and `scripts/installer.ps1` (line 1733), inside the existing `install_templates` function next to the style-guides copy. Distributes the hook source to `~/.devai-hub/hooks/claude-diff-review.sh` on POSIX or `%USERPROFILE%\.devai-hub\hooks\claude-diff-review.sh` on Windows, with `chmod +x` on POSIX. The hook is intentionally **not** added to `catalog/hooks/settings.json`, because settings.json wires Claude Code PreToolUse hooks; this is a git hook with a different trigger surface and a different lifecycle. The shared `~/.devai-hub/` location (not per-platform under `~/.claude/hooks/`) reflects that the hook is platform-agnostic on the trigger axis.
    *   **`catalog/hooks/tests/test_claude_diff_review.py`** - 11 new pytest tests covering bash syntax (`bash -n`), env-var bypass short-circuit, empty-diff exit, missing-CLI fail-open with warning to stderr, merge-state skip, rebase-state skip, diff-size-cap warning, and PASS / WARN / BLOCK / unparseable verdict parsing. The stub `claude` CLI is a fake bash binary placed on PATH that emits a fixed response; the temp git repo fixture creates a fresh repo with one staged change so the hook has a real diff to inspect. Combined hook test suite: 273 tests passing (262 prior + 11 new).
    *   **Canonical version bump 1.1.1 -> 1.1.2** across the same 13 files as v1.1.1: `.claude-plugin/{plugin,marketplace}.json`, `data/{bundles,marketplace,templates,workflows}.json`, `catalog/hooks/session-start.sh`, `scripts/installer.sh`, `scripts/installer.ps1`, `catalog/skills/README.md`, `guides/SUBAGENTS_GUIDE.md`, `infrastructure/{hooks,tools}/README.md`. Plus `session-start.sh` `COMMAND_COUNT` 32 -> 33 (one new slash command). CHANGELOG migrated from `[Unreleased]` to `[1.1.2] - 2026-05-06`; CHANGELOG footer Unreleased link bumped to `v1.1.2...HEAD`, and a new `[1.1.2]: v1.1.1...v1.1.2` comparison link added.

*   **Design Rationale**: The most important judgment call was **opt-in vs. opt-out**. An opt-out design (auto-install the hook on every DevAI-Hub install) was rejected for three reasons. First, every commit becomes a `claude -p` call (~3-8k input + ~200 output tokens, ~2-10s latency); silently imposing that on every user every commit is too aggressive. Second, the hook may produce false positives on legitimate `# TODO(jane):` markers, dummy test data, and config templates with placeholder values; a user surprised by their first BLOCK on a routine commit will reach for `git commit -n` and lose trust in the safety guarantee permanently. Third, a Claude-CLI-not-installed warning printed on every commit by default is noise. The opt-in design (run `/install-claude-pre-commit-hook` once per repo) makes activation an explicit consent gesture and gives the user one clear place to look ('which repos have this enabled?') rather than a per-machine policy that's hard to audit. The second key call was placing the hook source under `~/.devai-hub/hooks/` rather than `~/.claude/hooks/` (where existing PreToolUse hooks live). The hook is not a Claude Code hook - it's a git hook that happens to call the Claude CLI - and putting it under `~/.claude/` would mislead users into thinking it's wired through the Claude Code hook system. The third call was the verdict format: a strict first-line `VERDICT: PASS|WARN|BLOCK` that the bash hook can grep and `case` on, rather than free-form prose the hook would have to parse. The cost is one extra prompt-engineering instruction; the benefit is a deterministic mapping from LLM output to exit code, with a fail-open default for any unparseable response.

*   **Migration Impact**: NONE for any existing repository. The hook ships to `~/.devai-hub/hooks/` but is never auto-wired into any `.git/hooks/pre-commit` - users opt in per-repo by running the slash command. Existing v1.1.1 installations re-run the installer and pick up one new file (`~/.devai-hub/hooks/claude-diff-review.sh`); no existing files are overwritten, no settings.json schema changes, no permission entries added.

*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: The hook depends on Claude Code CLI being on PATH; users on Cursor / Copilot / Codex / Gemini who haven't separately installed Claude Code will get a non-fatal warning on every commit until they install it or set `DEVAI_DIFF_REVIEW_DISABLE=1`. A future minor bump could ship parallel reviewers for other CLIs (e.g. `gh copilot suggest`, `gemini -p`) and select based on which is on PATH. The 50 KB diff cap means very large commits skip review entirely; teams that want full coverage on big commits should either raise the cap (`DEVAI_DIFF_REVIEW_MAX_BYTES=204800`) or move the review to CI rather than pre-commit. The verdict prompt instructs Claude to default-PASS on documentation, lockfiles, and obvious test fixtures, but the LLM's judgment on what counts as "obvious" will produce some false positives until the prompt is tuned against real-world commit data; the WARN verdict (allow with warning) is the intentional escape valve for this. The carryover follow-ups from v1.1.1 (Gemini / Codex / Copilot stale-sentinel bug class in the permission installers; `[System.Environment]::*` family unmatched by `fnmatch`) remain open for a v1.2.0 candidate slate.

*   **Test Posture**: All four quality gates clean. `make validate` (JSON integrity across `data/*.json`) passes. `bash -n` on `claude-diff-review.sh` and `installer.sh` clean. The new test file (`test_claude_diff_review.py`) is 11/11 green; full hook test suite is 273/273 against v1.1.2. The installer-smoke test from v1.1.1 (which now reads the canonical version from `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` at runtime) passes against `1.1.2` without a code change - the resilience fix from the prior release is working as designed. No new tests were authored for the slash command itself because the command body is interpreted by Claude at invocation time and is not under unit test (the same pattern as every other slash command in `catalog/commands/`).

*   **Current Status**: All v1.1.2 surfaces shipped. The annotated `v1.1.2` tag is created locally with the release commit; push to origin happens at user direction.

---

## [2026-05-06] - Release 1.1.1: Commit-Message No-Hard-Wrap Rule + Smoke-Test Robustness

*   **Goal**: Close two small but recurring papercuts shipped to users in v1.1.0. First, `/generate-commit-message` and the `code-commit-workflow` skill enforced a no-hard-wrap rule on bullet points but said nothing about body paragraphs, so the agent kept producing commit messages with paragraph bodies wrapped at ~72 columns - the user had to correct this manually after every commit. Second, the installer-smoke test hard-coded the canonical version string, so every version bump caused the smoke test to fail until a follow-up commit patched the literal. Patch release; no behavioral or schema changes; no installer-rerun side effects.
*   **What Changed**:
    *   **No-hard-wrap rule extended from bullets to all body content** in `catalog/commands/generate-commit-message.md` and `catalog/skills/workflow/code-commit-workflow/SKILL.md`. Both files now require every paragraph and every bullet point in the commit body and footer to be a single continuous source line, regardless of length. The common wrap columns (50, 72, 80, 100) are called out explicitly so the agent cannot rationalize one of them as "the convention." The subject-line 50-character cap remains the only exception (a hard limit, not a wrap).
    *   **Examples reworked** in `code-commit-workflow/SKILL.md` to match the rule. Three "Good Examples" that previously demonstrated wrapped paragraph bodies were unwrapped; a hard-wrapped "Bad Example" was added so the failure mode appears side-by-side with a Good Example. Two new entries in the Common Rationalizations table rebut the "72-column convention" excuse (modern Git tooling, GitHub, GitLab, IDE diff views, and `git log` all soft-wrap on display; hard-wrapped source breaks copy-paste round-trips into changelogs and review comments) and the "split for readability" excuse (visual readability is the renderer's job; if a bullet is genuinely too long, split it into two distinct bullets, not into a continuation line that breaks the bullet's identity in Markdown and Git UIs). Quality Checklist and Verification gain a no-wrap item with a `git show --no-patch HEAD` spot-check.
    *   **Cross-platform reach without an installer edit**: `catalog/commands/generate-commit-message.md` reaches Claude Code (`commands/`), Gemini / Antigravity (`workflows/`), and Codex (`prompts/`) via the installer's recursive copy of `catalog/commands/`. `catalog/skills/workflow/code-commit-workflow/SKILL.md` reaches all five platforms via the skill index in their respective `base-*.md` instruction files. Cursor / OpenCode / Copilot, which do not get a per-command file copy, still inherit the rule because their `base-*.md` instruction templates already carry the global "Never hard-wrap paragraph text" rule and now point at a skill body whose examples and rebuttals are aligned with that rule.
    *   **Installer-smoke test no longer hard-codes the canonical version string** (`catalog/hooks/tests/test_installer_smoke.py`, committed as `3e80f88` between v1.1.0 and v1.1.1). The test now reads the version from `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` at test time. Future PATCH / MINOR / MAJOR bumps will not require a follow-up commit to keep the smoke test green - the v1.1.1 bump itself is the first to benefit.
    *   **Canonical 14-file version bump** plus the two installer scripts: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `data/bundles.json`, `data/marketplace.json`, `data/templates.json`, `data/workflows.json`, `catalog/hooks/session-start.sh`, `scripts/installer.sh`, `scripts/installer.ps1`, `catalog/skills/README.md`, `guides/SUBAGENTS_GUIDE.md`, `infrastructure/hooks/README.md`, `infrastructure/tools/README.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, plus this DEVLOG entry. CHANGELOG migrated from `[Unreleased]` to `[1.1.1] - 2026-05-06`; CHANGELOG footer Unreleased link bumped to compare from `v1.1.1...HEAD`, and a new `[1.1.1]: v1.1.0...v1.1.1` comparison link added.
*   **Design Rationale**: The bullet-only scope of the original rule was an oversight, not a deliberate scoping decision. The user's `CLAUDE.md` global already carried "Never hard-wrap paragraph text at a fixed column width" as a communication-style rule, but the more specific commit-message guidance in the command and skill files only mentioned bullets - and agents follow the more specific local rule, so the global was effectively shadowed in commit-message generation. The fix is to make the local rule cover the same surface as the global. Adding the explicit column callouts (50, 72, 80, 100) closes off the most common rationalization paths; adding a hard-wrapped Bad Example side-by-side with a Good Example is more durable than a prose rule alone because the agent can pattern-match on shape. The smoke-test fix is a structural improvement: the original test asserted equality against a literal `"1.1.0"`, which is a forward-incompatible coupling between test code and release state. Reading from `plugin.json` makes the test self-updating at every bump.
*   **Migration Impact**: NONE. No file paths changed, no APIs changed, no settings.json schema changed, no permission entries added. Users who installed v1.1.0 will see the new commit-message behavior the next time they invoke `/generate-commit-message` (the command body is re-read on every invocation). The installer can be re-run if desired but is not required for this release - the v1.1.0 install copy of `~/.devai-hub/skills/code-commit-workflow/SKILL.md` is the only file users would refresh, and only because its body content changed.
*   **Known Issues / Follow-ups**: None opened by v1.1.1. The follow-ups carried over from v1.1.0 remain open: Gemini / Codex / Copilot branches of the permission installers carry the same stale-sentinel bug class as the Claude branch fixed in v1.1.0 (not fixed in v1.1.1 because those branches do not ship new entries in v1.1.1 either); the `[System.Environment]::*` family of read-only patterns cannot be added to the PowerShell allow-list via `fnmatch` because Python interprets `[...]` as a character class. Both are still tracked as v1.2.0 candidates.
*   **Test Posture**: All four quality gates clean. `make validate` (JSON integrity across `data/*.json`) passes. `make lint` (ShellCheck on installer + hooks) carries no new warnings. The installer-smoke test now derives its expected version from `plugin.json` and so passes against `1.1.1` without code changes. No new tests were authored for v1.1.1 because the changeset is documentation-only on the catalog side and a coupling fix on the test side; the catalog files are not under test (they are templates rendered into user-installed paths, validated via the installer-smoke test which already covers the destination structure).
*   **Current Status**: All v1.1.1 surfaces shipped. The annotated `v1.1.1` tag is created locally with the release commit; push to origin happens at user direction.
